---
title: "2. Nāmakaṇḍa (Noun section)"
description: "Buddhappiya's Nāmakaṇḍa: the inflection of nouns, stem by stem, with full paradigms — masculine, feminine, neuter, pronouns, numerals — then the suffixes that stand for inflections, prefixes and particles."
sidebar:
  label: 2. Nāmakaṇḍa
---

import { LinkCard } from "@astrojs/starlight/components";

:::note
Where Kaccāyana orders his noun rules by operation, Buddhappiya orders them by stem: the masculine stems in `a`, `ā`, `i`, `ī`, `u`, `ū`, `o`, each with its paradigm (`padamālā`) and the derivation of every form; then the feminine and neuter stems, the pronouns (`sabbanāma`), the genderless words (numerals), the suffixes that take the place of an inflection (`to`, `tra`, `dā` …), the prefixes and the particles. Kaccāyana's rules are quoted where each form needs them, so a rule may appear more than once; the paradigms are set out as inflection tables.

:::

:::note[Reading Rachiwong]
This section is one of the three that Phramaha Sriporn Rachiwong translated in her Pune thesis *Rūpasiddhi: A Study of Some Aspects* (1995), reproduced on this site as [Rachiwong: Rūpasiddhi (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong). The translation has been collated with hers rule by rule, and the footnotes below record where her text or her construal differs from what is printed here. Three things to know when comparing:

* **Her numbers.** Each of her rules is headed `(K-N)`: K is the Kaccāyana sutta, N the rule's number in the Thai edition of the Padarūpasiddhi (Bangkok 1984), her base text. In this section her N is the number used on this page (her rule (159-139) is 139 here). Her K is occasionally misprinted, so the sutta's wording is the surer guide.
* **Her editions.** Her Explanatory Notes collate the Thai edition (Rūp T.) with the Burmese Buddhasāsanasamiti edition (B.) — the Chaṭṭha Saṅgāyana text these pages print — and the Sinhalese Mahārūpasiddhi (S.), and cite the Burmese Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (PDṬ) and a Thai handbook (PDP). Where she reports that the Thai and Sinhalese editions read one way and the Burmese another, the reading printed here is the Burmese one alone; the footnotes say so and keep the CST's text except where the sense requires otherwise.
* **Her English.** A literal, clause-by-clause rendering in the older idiom: `liṅga` is "gender" where Buddhappiya means the stem, `vibhatti` "case-ending", `vuddhi` "lengthening"; `vā` is "optionally", `kvaci` "sometimes" and `navā` "may or may not", and `vavatthitavibhāsā` is named only where Buddhappiya names it, with its three domains stated only where his verse or the Thai handbook lists the forms. Her form lists carry the typescript's slips and the Thai print's habits (`bhi` before `hi`), so a single divergent form is evidence of a reading only when it recurs or is the better Pāli. Her Explanatory Notes on 69 and 96 set out the three domains of the `vā` — `nicca`, `anicca`, `asanta` — in the same terms as the Analyses here, and her citations of the ṭīkā (PDṬ pp. 24–39) supply glosses these pages had lacked; where she reads Buddhappiya's compounds more literally than the first draft of this page did (`sasmāsu` at 139–140, the `su` of 169–170, `thamādi` at 226, the particle lists under 282), the text has been corrected and the footnote says so.

:::

:::note[Buddhappiya's order of the nouns]
The Nāmakaṇḍa proceeds by gender and by the final of the stem, giving each model noun its paradigm; the ranges are Rūpasiddhi numbers.

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  N(["Nāmakaṇḍa 60–282"]) --> M["Pulliṅga 60–175"]
  N --> F["Itthiliṅga 176–194"]
  N --> Nt["Napuṃsakaliṅga 195–199"]
  N --> P["Sabbanāma 200–231<br/>the pronouns in three genders"]
  N --> A["Aliṅganāma 232–259<br/>the numerals"]
  N --> Vb["Vibhattippaccayavidhāna 260–281<br/>to, tra, tha, dhi, dā, dāni, rahi …"]
  N --> Op["Opasaggikapada 282 and Nepātikapada<br/>prefixes and particles"]
  M --> M1["a: purisa 60–143, with mana, guṇavantu, gacchanta,<br/>bhavanta, rāja, brahma, atta, sakha, puma"]
  M --> M2["ā: sā 144 · i: aggi 145–149 · ī: daṇḍī 150–154<br/>u: bhikkhu, satthu, pitu 155–168 · ū: sabbaññū · o: go 169–175"]
  F --> F1["ā: kaññā 176–181 · i: ratti 182–186<br/>ī: itthī, nadī 187–194 · u, ū: yāgu, vadhū, mātu"]
  Nt --> Nt1["a: citta 195–198<br/>aṭṭhi, sukhakārī, āyu, gotrabhū 199"]
```

:::

> Atha nāmikavibhatyāvatāro vuccate.

Now the introduction to the nominal inflections (`nāmikavibhatti`) is set forth.

> Atthābhimukhaṃ namanato, attani catthassa nāmanato nāmaṃ, dabbābhidhānaṃ.

A noun is called `nāma` because it bends (`namati`) towards a meaning, and because it makes a meaning bend towards itself: it is the naming of a substance (`dabba`).

> Taṃ pana duvidhaṃ anvattharuḷhīvasena, tividhaṃ pumitthinapuṃsakaliṅgavasena. Yathā – rukkho, mālā, dhanaṃ.

It is of two kinds, as `anvattha` (a name that says what it means) and `ruḷhī` (a name fixed by convention); and of three kinds by gender, masculine, feminine and neuter, as `rukkho` (a tree), `mālā` (a garland), `dhanaṃ` (wealth).

> Catubbidhaṃ sāmaññaguṇakriyāyadicchāvasena, yathā – rukkho, nīlo, pācako, sirivaḍḍhotiādi.

It is of four kinds by what it names: a genus (`sāmañña`), a quality (`guṇa`), an action (`kriyā`) or a bearer's arbitrary choice (`yadicchā`), as `rukkho` (a tree), `nīlo` (blue), `pācako` (a cook), `Sirivaḍḍho` (a personal name), and so on.

> Aṭṭhavidhaṃ avaṇṇivaṇṇuvaṇṇokāraniggahītantapakatibhedena.

And it is of eight kinds by the division of its stems (`pakati`), which end in the `a`-sounds (`a`, `ā`), the `i`-sounds (`i`, `ī`), the `u`-sounds (`u`, `ū`), `o`, and the niggahīta.

## Pulliṅga (Masculine nouns)

> Tattha paṭhamaṃ a kārantamhā pulliṅgā jātinimittā purisa saddā syā divibhattiyo parā yojīyante.

Among these, first, the inflections `si` and the rest are attached after the masculine word `purisa` (a man), which ends in `a` and is applied on the ground of a genus (`jātinimitta`).

---

<a name="r60"></a>

### 60 (§52): Jinavacanayuttañhi (Suitable for the teachings of the Conqueror) :badge[sīhagatika]{variant=caution}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§52 (60): Jinavacanayuttaṃ hi" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m52" description="Suitable for the teachings of the Conqueror" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The Nāmakaṇḍa opens, as Kaccāyana's Nāmakappa does, with this governing rule (`adhikāra`), after a short preface on what a noun (`nāma`) is and how nouns are classified. The Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (PDṬ pp. 24–25, cited by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong)) glosses that preface: an `anvattha` name has "gone to its meaning" (`atthaṃ gataṃ anvatthaṃ`), a `ruḷhī` name is one given at will without regard to meaning (`atthanirapekkhaṃ nāmaṃ yadicchānāmaṃ`, so that the `ruḷhī` of the twofold division is the `yadicchā` of the fourfold), and the eight stem classes are exemplified by `purisa` and `sā`, `aggi` and `daṇḍī`, `bhikkhu` and `abhibhū`, `go` and `sutano`, `kiṃ` and `idaṃ`. Buddhappiya keeps Kaccāyana's first five suttas in their order (60–64) and then, unlike Kaccāyana, follows them at once with the inflections of `purisa` (65–94), so that the governing rules are read as the frame of the paradigm.

Kaccāyana's vutti is a single sentence; Buddhappiya's gloss adds the etymology of `jina`, the identification of the Conqueror's word with Māgadhī, the verse on the root language and the threefold classification of the `adhikāra`, and closes by announcing the `paribhāsā` of [61 (§53): Liṅgañca nipaccate](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r61). The grammar itself illustrates the three kinds: this rule is the lion's glance; the `vā` that [152 (§245): Jhalapā rassaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r152) fetches from [180 (§216): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r180) is the frog's leap (`maṇḍūkagatiyā`, Buddhappiya's own word there); and the ordinary carrying-over of a word from the rule before is `yathānupubbika`, which the Thai handbook *Padarūpasiddhi Plae lae Athibai* also calls `nadīsotagatika`, running like the stream of a river (PDP p. 43, cited by Rachiwong 1995). There is no option marker.

:::

> Adhikāro yaṃ. Tattha pañca māre jitavāti jino, buddho. Jinassa vacanaṃ jinavacanaṃ, tassa jinavacanassa yuttaṃ jinavacanayuttaṃ, tepiṭakassa buddhavacanassa māgadhikāya sabhāvaniruttiyā yuttaṃ anurūpamevāti idaṃ adhikāratthaṃ veditabbaṃ.

This is a governing rule (`adhikāra`). In it, he conquered the five Māras, hence `jina`, the Conqueror: the Buddha. The word of the Conqueror is `jinavacana`; what is suited to that word of the Conqueror is `jinavacanayutta`. It is to be understood as governing with this sense: [whatever is taught here] is suited, that is, conformable, to the natural language (`sabhāvanirutti`) of Magadha, the language of the Buddha's word in the three Piṭakas.

> Sā māgadhī mūlabhāsā, narā yāyādikappikā; \
> Brahmāno ca’ssutālāpā, sambuddhā cāpi bhāsare.

That Māgadhī is the root language, in which the people of the first aeon, \
the Brahmās, those who have never heard speech, and the Fully Awakened Ones too, speak.

> Adhikāro pana tividho sīhagatikamaṇḍūkagatikayathānupubbikavasena, ayaṃ pana sīhagatiko pubbāparavilokanato, yathānupubbikoyeva vā.

A governing rule is of three kinds: `sīhagatika` (proceeding like a lion), `maṇḍūkagatika` (proceeding like a frog) and `yathānupubbika` (proceeding in due order). This one is `sīhagatika`, since it looks both backward and forward; or else it is simply `yathānupubbika`.

> Sakkatavisadisaṃ katvā jinavacanānurūpavasena pakatiṭṭhapanatthaṃ paribhāsamāha.

In order to set up the stems (`pakati`) in conformity with the Conqueror's word, making them unlike Sanskrit, he states an interpretive rule (`paribhāsā`):

:::tip[Example]

The stem this governing rule first admits is the one the whole section inflects.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(purisa) | puriso | a man — the Māgadhī stem `purisa`, not the Sanskrit `puruṣa` |

:::

---

<a name="r61"></a>

### 61 (§53): Liṅgañca nipaccate (And lemmas are derived) :badge[sīhagatika]{variant=caution}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§53 (61): Liṅgañca nippajjate" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m53" description="And lemmas are derived" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The second governing rule, read by Buddhappiya as the interpretive rule (`paribhāsā`) that [60 (§52): Jinavacanayuttañhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r60) announced. Where Kaccāyana's vutti is silent, his gloss equates `liṅga` with `pāṭipadika`, the Sanskrit grammarians' name for the nominal stem, borrowing their definition of it under [§9 (11): Parasamaññā payoge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m9), gives the `ca` a job (roots) and states the `anuvutti` of `liṅga`. The Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (PDṬ p. 26, cited by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong)) reduces the equation to a synonymy — `liṅgaṃ pāṭipadikaṃ` is `nāmassa eva pariyāyo`, so that `liṅga`, `pāṭipadika` and `nāma` are three names for one thing — and the Thai handbook agrees (PDP p. 103, `liṅgaṃ nāmaṃ pāṭipadikasadisaṃ`). The sutta's `nipaccate` is the CST Rūpasiddhi's spelling; Kaccāyana's vutti section has `nippajjate`. There is no option marker.

:::

> Liṅgaṃ pāṭipadikaṃ, yathā yathā jinavacanayuttañhi liṅgaṃ, tathā tathā idha liṅgaṃ nipaccate ṭhapīyati. Ca saddena dhātavo cāti jinavacanānurūpato ‘‘purisa’’iti liṅge ṭhapite tato tassa dhātuppaccayavibhattivajjitassa atthavato saddassa ‘‘parasamaññā payoge’’ti paribhāsato liṅgasaññāyaṃ –

`liṅga` is the nominal stem (`pāṭipadika`). In whatever way a stem is suited to the Conqueror's word, in that way the stem is here `nipaccate`, that is, it is set up. By the word `ca`, roots too. So when, in conformity with the Conqueror's word, the stem `purisa` has been set up, then — that meaningful word, free of root, suffix and inflection, receiving the name `liṅga` by the interpretive rule [§9 (11): Parasamaññā payoge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m9) —

> Ito paraṃ vibhattippaccayādividhāne sabbattha liṅga ggahaṇamanuvattate.

from here on, in every prescription of inflections, suffixes and the like, the word `liṅga` is carried over (`anuvattate`) throughout.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹(purisa) | purisa | a man — the stem set up before any inflection is added |

:::

---

<a name="r62"></a>

### 62 (§54): Tato ca vibhattiyo (And their inflections) :badge[sīhagatika]{variant=caution}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§54 (62): Tato ca vibhattiyo" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m54" description="And their inflections" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The third governing rule. Buddhappiya's gloss gives the `ca` a reach Kaccāyana's one-sentence vutti does not: the inflections are added also after words in `tave`, `tuna` and the like and after particles, which [282 (§221): Sabbāsamāvusopasagganipātādīhi ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r282) then strips of them again, as he says outright under [637 (§595): Tavetunādīsu kā](/rupasiddhi/7-kibbidhanakanda#r637), `tavetunamantato ‘‘sabbāsamāvuso’’tiādinā vibhattilopo` (the sentence [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) cites from the Thai edition, Rūp T. p. 361, with the examples `kātave`, `kātūna`, `katvā`, `katvāna`). The etymology of `vibhatti` is his addition too. No option marker.

:::

> Tato jinavacanayuttehi liṅgehi parā vibhattiyo honti. Ca saddaggahaṇena tavetunādipaccayantanipātatopi. Kammādivasena, ekattādivasena ca liṅgatthaṃ vibhajantīti vibhattiyo.

After those stems suited to the Conqueror's word come the inflections (`vibhatti`). By the mention of `ca`, also after words ending in the suffixes `tave`, `tuna`[^1] and the like, and after particles. They are called `vibhatti` because they divide (`vibhajanti`) the meaning of the stem, by its role as object and the rest and by its number as one and the rest.

> Kā ca pana tā vibhattiyo? ‘‘Vibhattiyo’’ti adhikāro.

And what are those inflections? The word `vibhattiyo` governs [the next rule].

:::tip[Examples]

The words the `ca` reaches take an inflection which is then elided.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(kātave) | kātave | to do (a `tave` infinitive, an uninflected word) |
| 🔽(katvā) | katvā | having done (a `tvā` absolutive) |
| 🔼(ca) | ca | and (a particle) |

:::

[^1]: CST4 prints `tavetunādi`, with the short `tuna`, here and in the Kibbidhāna gloss, as the CST Kaccāyana writes the suffix at [§564 (640): Pubbakālekakattukānaṃ tuna tvāna tvā vā](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m564); the Thai edition read by Rachiwong (1995) has `tūna` in both places (`kātūna`).

---

<a name="r63"></a>

### 63 (§55): Siyo, aṃyo, nāhi, sanaṃ, smāhi, sanaṃ, smiṃsu (inflection forms) :badge[rūḷhī]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§55 (63): Si yo, aṃ yo, nā hi, sa naṃ, smā hi, sa naṃ, smiṃ su" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m55" description="inflection forms" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The list of the fourteen inflections stands where it stands in Kaccāyana, answering the question of [62 (§54): Tato ca vibhattiyo](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r62). What the gloss adds to Kaccāyana's vutti is the argument that this is a `saññā` sutta in its own right, one of the four kinds, and not one rule with the sutta before. Later handbooks make six kinds, adding `niyama` (restriction) and `atidesa` (transfer) to these four — `saññā ca paribhāsā ca vidhipi niyamo pi ca, atidesādhikāroti chabbidhaṃ suttalakkhaṇaṃ` (the Kaccāyanasuttattha, KS p. 15, quoted by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong)) — and Buddhappiya uses both terms himself, at [152 (§245): Jhalapā rassaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r152) and [120 (§270): Amhatumhanturājabrahmattasakhasatthupitādīhi smā nāva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r120). There is no option marker.

:::

> Syādayo dvisatta vibhattiyo nāma honti. Tattha si, yo iti paṭhamā, aṃ, yo iti dutiyā, nā, hi iti tatiyā, sa, naṃ iti catutthī, smā, hi iti pañcamī, sa, naṃ iti chaṭṭhī, smiṃ, su iti sattamī.

`si` and the rest, twice seven in number, are called `vibhatti` (inflections). Among them `si` and `yo` are the first form ①, `aṃ` and `yo` the second ②, `nā` and `hi` the third ③, `sa` and `naṃ` the fourth ④, `smā` and `hi` the fifth ⑤, `sa` and `naṃ` the sixth ⑥, `smiṃ` and `su` the seventh ⑦.

| symbol | ⨀ | ⨂ | inflection form |
| :-: | :-: | :-: | --- |
| ① | `si` | `yo` | `paṭhamā` ("first") |
| ② | `aṃ` | `yo` | `dutiyā` ("second") |
| ③ | `nā` | `hi` | `tatiyā` ("third") |
| ④ | `sa` | `naṃ` | `catutthī` ("fourth") |
| ⑤ | `smā` | `hi` | `pañcamī` ("fifth") |
| ⑥ | `sa` | `naṃ` | `chaṭṭhī` ("sixth") |
| ⑦ | `smiṃ` | `su` | `sattamī` ("seventh") |

> Idaṃ pana saññādhikāraparibhāsāvidhisuttesu saññāsutta nti daṭṭhabbaṃ, vuttañhi vuttiyaṃ ‘‘vibhattiiccanena kvattho, amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se’’ti, itarathā purimasuttena ekayogo kattabboti. Ettha ca paṭhamādivohāro, ekavacanādivohāro ca anvatthavasena, parasamaññāvasena vā siddhoti veditabbo.

Among suttas of the four kinds — `saññā` (defining), `adhikāra` (governing), `paribhāsā` (interpretive) and `vidhi` (operational) — this is to be seen as a defining sutta; for it is said in the vutti, "What is the use of the term `vibhatti`? [The rule] [§120 (243): Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m120)." Otherwise it would have to be made one rule (`ekayoga`) with the preceding sutta.[^2] And here the usage "first" and the rest, and the usage "singular" and the rest, are to be understood as established either by transparent meaning (`anvattha`) or by the terminology of other [grammarians] (`parasamaññā`).

> Ekassa vacanaṃ ekavacanaṃ, bahūnaṃ vacanaṃ bahuvacanaṃ, dvinnaṃ pūraṇī dutiyātiādi, itarathā purimasutte ca saddena saññākaraṇe appakataniratthakavidhippasaṅgo siyā.

The expression of one is the singular (`ekavacana`), the expression of many the plural (`bahuvacana`); `dutiyā` ("second") is the ordinal of two, and so on. Otherwise, if the naming were done by the `ca` of the preceding sutta, there would arise a prescription that is beside the point and without purpose.

> ‘‘Jinavacanayuttañhi, liṅgañca nipaccate’’ti ca vattate. Idha pana padanipphādanampi jinavacanassāvirodhenāti ñāpetuṃ paribhāsantaramāha.

And [60 (§52): Jinavacanayuttañhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r60) and [61 (§53): Liṅgañca nipaccate](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r61) continue. Now, to make known that here the formation of words (`pada`) too is without conflict with the Conqueror's word, he states a further interpretive rule:

:::tip[Example]

The vutti's proof that `vibhatti` is a term in use: a rule that operates on a word "together with its inflection" (`savibhattissa`).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️④⨀(amha) | mamaṃ | to me (`amha` + `sa` replaced as a whole) |

:::

[^2]: Rachiwong (1995) renders `itarathā purimasuttena ekayogo kattabbo` as "Alternatively, this rule should be joined with the previous rules", as though Buddhappiya offered a second reading, and makes the sentence on `paṭhamā` and `ekavacana` its reason; the Pāli is a reductio — otherwise the sutta would have to be made one rule with the one before — and the next sentence is a new point. She likewise refers the closing `paribhāsantaramāha` to this sutta itself ("the present sutta is composed separately to indicate …"); it announces the next rule, [64 (§56): Tadanuparodhena](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r64).

---

<a name="r64"></a>

### 64 (§56): Tadanuparodhena (those word endings) :badge[sīhagatika]{variant=caution}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§56 (64): Tadanuparodhena" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m56" description="those word endings" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last of the governing rules before the paradigm begins. Kaccāyana's vutti repeats the wording of §53; Buddhappiya's reads the `ca` as taking in the verb and draws from the rule the two points on which Pāli grammar departs from Sanskrit. His second block is the hinge of the whole section: the choice among the fourteen inflections is made by the speaker's intention (`vatticchā`), and every step of the paradigm from [65 (§284): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r65) on opens with that choice. No option marker.

:::

> Yathā yathā tesaṃ jinavacanānaṃ uparodho na hoti, tathā tathā idha liṅgaṃ, ca saddenākhyātañca nipaccate, nipphādīyatīti attho. Teneva idha ca ākhyāte ca dvivacanāggahaṇaṃ, sakkatavisadisato vibhattippaccayādividhānañca katanti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

In whatever way there is no obstruction of those words of the Conqueror, in that way the stem — and, by the word `ca`, the verb (`ākhyāta`) too — is here `nipaccate`, that is, is formed. For that very reason it is to be seen that here [among nouns] and among verbs the dual is not admitted, and the prescription of inflections, suffixes and the rest is made in a way unlike Sanskrit.

> Tattha avisesena sabbasyādivibhattippasaṅge ‘‘vatticchānupubbikā saddappavattī’’ti vatticchāvasā –

There, when all the inflections `si` and the rest would apply without distinction, by [the maxim] "the occurrence of words follows the speaker's intention", according to the speaker's intention (`vatticchā`) —

:::tip[Example]

Where Sanskrit has a dual, Pāli has the plural.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| ⚧️①⨂(dvi) 🚹①⨂(purisa) | dve purisā | two men (plural, not a dual) |

:::

---

<a name="r65"></a>

### 65 (§284): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā (the first form ① for the bare meaning of the stem) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§284 (283): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m284" description="the first form ① for the bare meaning of the stem" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states this rule in the Kāraka chapter, once the `kāraka` have been defined; Buddhappiya cites it twice, here and at [283 (§284): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r283), because his paradigm of `purisa` takes the fourteen inflections in order and the first form must be motivated before `si` can be added. The definition of `liṅgattha` waits for 283. The closing `anuvutti` runs backwards: `ato` is taken from [79 (§103): Ato nena](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r79), a rule still to come in his order, for [66 (§104): So](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r66) to use. There is no option marker.

:::

> Liṅgatthābhidhānamatte paṭhamāvibhatti hotīti paṭhamā. Tatthāpi aniyamenekavacanabahuvacanappasaṅge ‘‘ekamhī vattabbe ekavacana’’nti paribhāsato liṅgatthassekattavacanicchāyaṃ paṭhamekavacanaṃ si.

In the mere expression of the meaning of the stem (`liṅgattha`) the first inflection ① is used: hence the first. There too, when singular and plural would apply without restriction, by the interpretive rule "when one is to be expressed, the singular", when there is the wish to express the singleness of the stem's meaning, [the inflection is] the first-form singular `si`.

> ‘‘Ato nenā’’ti ito ‘‘ato’’ti vattate, liṅga ggahaṇañca.

From [79 (§103): Ato nena](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r79) the word `ato` ("after `a`") continues, and the word `liṅga` as well.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(purisa) | puriso | a man — `purisa` + `si`, completed under 66 and 67 |

:::

---

<a name="r66"></a>

### 66 (§104): So (`si` to `o`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§104 (66): So" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m104" description="`si` to `o`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The first operational rule of the section. Kaccāyana places it among the rules for stems in `a` (§101–§108); Buddhappiya takes it out of that group and puts it first, because it is the first thing that happens to the first inflection of his model noun. To Kaccāyana's one-sentence vutti his gloss adds three remarks of method that serve every later rule — on the first-form citation of the `kāriyī`, on `si` meaning the inflection, and on the generality of both — and a lead-in to [67 (§83): Saralopo’mādesappaccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r67) as the `apavāda` of the sandhi rule. There is no option marker.

:::

> Si, oiti dvipadamidaṃ. Liṅgassa a kārato parassa si vacanassa o kāro hoti.

This is two words: `si` and `o`. The inflection `si` following the `a` of the stem becomes the letter `o`.

> Suttesu hi paṭhamāniddiṭṭhassa kāriyino chaṭṭhīvipariṇāmena vivaraṇaṃ ādesāpekkhanti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

For in the suttas, what undergoes the operation (`kāriyī`) is stated in the first form, and its explanation, converting it to the sixth form ("of `si`"), is to be understood as looking to the replacement (`ādesa`).

> Ettha ca sī ti vibhatti gayhate vibhattikāriyavidhippakaraṇato, ‘‘tato ca vibhattiyo’’ti ito vibhatti ggahaṇānuvattanato vā, evaṃ sabbattha syā dīnaṃ kāriyavidhāne vibhattiyevāti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

And here `si` is taken as the inflection, because this section prescribes operations on inflections, or because the word `vibhatti` continues from [62 (§54): Tato ca vibhattiyo](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r62). In the same way, everywhere that an operation is prescribed for `si` and the rest, it is the inflection alone that is to be understood.

> ‘‘Vā paro asarūpā’’ti paralope sampatte tadapavādena pubbalopamāha.

When elision of the following [vowel] would apply by [§13 (15): Vā paro asarūpā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m13), he states the elision of the preceding [vowel] as an exception to it:

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(purisa) | puriso | a man |

|     | purisa + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (si→o)        | §104 |
| →   | puris + a + o          | (the two vowels meet: 67 decides which is elided) |

:::

---

<a name="r67"></a>

### 67 (§83): Saralopo’mādesappaccayādimhi saralope tu pakati (in vowel elision in `aṃ` [inflections], replacements, suffixes etc., that elided vowel can be restored) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m83" description="in vowel elision in `aṃ` [inflections], replacements, suffixes etc., that elided vowel can be restored" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states this rule among the general rules of the Nāmakappa (§82–§90); Buddhappiya brings it forward to the second step of `puriso`, where `puris` + `a` + `o` needs a rule to elide the `a`, and it then serves every derivation of the chapter. The two memory verses are his addition to Kaccāyana's vutti; the `abbha` and `āha` of the second are the replacements of `abhi` ([§44 (24): Abbho abhi](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m44)) and of `brū` ([§475 (465): Brūbhūnamāhabhūvā parokkhāyaṃ](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m475)), which replace stems and do not follow one. The `tu` is not an option marker but a restriction (Kaccāyana's vutti calls it `avadhāraṇa`), under which [§13 (15): Vā paro asarūpā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m13) elides the following vowel in `bhikkhunī` instead of the preceding one.

:::

> Pubbassarassa lopo hoti aṃ vacane, ādesappaccayādibhūte ca sare pare, saralope kate tu parasarassa pakatibhāvo hoti. Ettha ca ‘‘saralope’’ti punaggahaṇaṃ imināva katasaralopanimitteyeva parassa vikāre sampatte pakatibhāvatthaṃ. Parasarassa pakatibhāvavidhānasāmatthiyato amādesappaccayādibhūte sare paretipi siddhaṃ.

The preceding vowel is elided when the inflection `aṃ`, or a vowel that is a replacement (`ādesa`), a suffix (`paccaya`) or the like, follows; but when that vowel elision has been made, the following vowel keeps its natural form (`pakatibhāva`).[^3] Here the repetition of `saralope` ("when the vowel is elided") is so that the natural form is kept only where a modification of the following vowel would apply because of an elision made by this very rule. And from the force of prescribing the natural form for the following vowel it is established that [the elision takes place] when the vowel that follows is `aṃ`, a replacement, a suffix or the like.

> Tyādivibhattiyo cettha, paccayattena gayhare; \
> Ādiggahaṇamākhyāta-kitakesvāgamatthidaṃ.

And here the inflections `ti` and the rest [the verb endings] are taken as suffixes; \
the word `ādi` is for the augments in verbs and in `kita` words.

> Paccayasāhacariyā, cādeso pakatīparo; \
> Padantassaralopo na, tena’bbhāhādike pare.

And by its company with `paccaya`, the replacement [meant] is one that follows a stem; \
hence a word-final vowel is not elided before `abbha`, `āha` and the like.

> Tu ggahaṇaṃ bhikkhunī ādīsu saralopanivattanatthaṃ, ‘‘naye paraṃ yutte’’ti paraṃ netabbaṃ. Puriso tiṭṭhati.

The word `tu` is to block the vowel elision in `bhikkhunī` and the like. By [§11 (14): Naye paraṃ yutte](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m11) the following [letter] is to be joined on: `puriso tiṭṭhati`.

> Puriso ca puriso cāti purisa purisaiti vattabbe –

When `puriso ca puriso ca` ("a man and a man") is to be said, as `purisa purisa` —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(purisa) | puriso tiṭṭhati | the man stands |
| 🚺①⨀(bhikkhunī) | bhikkhunī | a nun (the `u` kept, the `i` of `inī` elided) |

|     | purisa + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (si→o)        | §104 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + o)    | §83  |
| →   | puriso                 | §11  |
| →   | **puriso**             |      |

:::

[^3]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) renders `saralope tu pakati` as "when, however, the vowel is elided, the word remains in its base form", making `pakati` the base form of the word; the Pāli, `parasarassa pakatibhāvo hoti`, says that the following vowel keeps its natural form — which is why [76 (§297): Kammatthe dutiyā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r76) gives `purisaṃ` and not a lengthened `purisāṃ`. Her "the word 'ādi' is for inclusion of the verb, derivative etc." also drops the `āgama` of the verse: `ādiggahaṇamākhyātakitakesvāgamatthaṃ` is "the word `ādi` is for the augments in verbs and `kita` words".

---

<a name="r68"></a>

### 68 (§388): Sarūpānamekasesvasakiṃ (of like forms occurring more than once, one alone remains) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§388 (68): Sarūpānamekasesvasakiṃ" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m388" description="of like forms occurring more than once, one alone remains" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states the `ekasesa` in the Taddhita chapter, where the numerals need it; Buddhappiya moves it into the paradigm of `purisa`, at the point where the plural is wanted, because for him the plural is an `ekasesa`: `puriso ca puriso ca` reduces to a single `purisa`, which then takes `yo`. The `vā` of the verse's `yogavibhāga` is an open option, since `mātāpitūnaṃ` remains good Pāli beside `pitūnaṃ`. The `vā` the last block carries over for [69 (§107): Sabbayonīnamāe](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r69) is, in Kaccāyana, that of `So vā` (§105), a sutta the Rūpasiddhi omits.

:::

:::note[The kinds of ekasesa]

The Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (PDṬ p. 29) and the Thai handbook *Padarūpasiddhi Plae lae Athibai* (PDP p. 114), both cited by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) in her Explanatory Note on rule 68, sort the `ekasesa` further. The ṭīkā resolves the two terms — `samānaṃ rūpaṃ yesanti sarūpā; eko ca so seso cāti` — and the `ekasesa` of like forms (`sarūpekasesa`) is threefold: of the word only (`saddasarūpa`: `māso ca māso ca māsā`, a month and a bean), of the meaning only (`atthasarūpa`: `vaṅko ca kuṭilo ca kuṭilā`, crooked things), of both word and meaning (`saddatthasarūpa`: `puriso ca puriso ca purisā`), as the handbook's verse puts it, `sarūpasseva saddattha-saddatthānaṃ sabhāvato, tibbidhattaṃ yathā māsā kuṭilā purisāti ca`. The `ekasesa` of unlike forms (`virūpekasesa`) is twofold, the earlier word dropping (`pubbavirūpa`: `mātā ca pitā ca pitaro`) or the later (`paravirūpa`: `sāriputto ca moggallāno ca sāriputtā`), and only between words related in meaning, as `mātu` and `pitu`, `ayyaka` and `ayyikā`.

:::

> Sarūpānaṃ samānarūpānaṃ padabyañjanānaṃ majjhe ekova sissate, aññe lopamāpajjante asakinti ekaseso. Ettha ca ‘‘sarūpāna’’nti vuttattāva siddhe asakimpayoge punāsakiṃ gahaṇaṃ e kavibhattivisayānamevāsakimpayoge evāyanti dassanatthaṃ, na ca vicchāpayoge’tippasaṅgo. ‘‘Vaggā pañcapañcaso mantā’’ti ettha ‘‘pañcapañcaso’’ti niddeseneva vicchāpayogasiddhiyā ñāpitattā, atha vā sahavacanicchāya’maya’mekaseso.

Among words and sounds of the same form (`sarūpa`, that is, of like shape) occurring more than once (`asakiṃ`), one alone remains and the others are elided: this is `ekasesa` (the remaining of one). Here, although the occurrence more than once is already established by the mere saying of `sarūpānaṃ`, the further mention of `asakiṃ` is to show that this [`ekasesa`] holds only in a repeated occurrence of words that carry one and the same inflection, and that there is no over-application to a distributive use (`vicchā`) — because the validity of the distributive use has already been made known by the very statement `pañcapañcaso` in [§7 (9): Vaggā pañcapañcaso mantā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m7). Or else this `ekasesa` [takes place] when there is the wish to speak of [the things] together (`sahavacanicchā`).

> Yogavibhāgato cettha, ekasesvasakiṃ iti; \
> Virūpekaseso hoti, vā ‘‘pitūna’’ntiādisu.

And here, by splitting the rule and reading `ekasesvasakiṃ` [alone], \
there is optionally an `ekasesa` of unlike forms, as in `pitūnaṃ` and the like.

> Tattheva liṅgatthassa ba huttavacanicchāyaṃ ‘‘bahumhi vattabbe bahuvacana’’nti paṭhamābahuvacanaṃ yo, purisa yo itīdha ‘‘ato, vā’’ti ca vattate.

In that very [stem],[^4] when there is the wish to express the plurality of the stem's meaning, by [the maxim] "when many are to be expressed, the plural", [the inflection is] the first-form plural `yo`; and here, in `purisa` + `yo`, `ato` and `vā` continue.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(purisa) | purisā | men (`puriso ca puriso ca`, one `purisa` kept) |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pitu) | pitūnaṃ | of the parents (for `mātāpitūnaṃ`, by the split rule) |

|     | purisa purisa + yo (①⨂)    | rule |
| --- | -------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ~~purisa~~ purisa + yo     | §388 |
| →   | purisa + yo                | (continued under 69) |

:::

[^4]: CST4 prints `Tattheva` (`tattha` + `eva`), the Burmese reading, here and in the eight repetitions of the formula through the paradigm of `purisa` (69, 74, 76, 81, 83, 87, 90, 92); the Thai and Sinhalese editions read `tatheva` (`tathā` + `eva`, "likewise") throughout (Rachiwong 1995, Explanatory Note on rule 68; she prefers the Burmese reading). The CST is kept and rendered "In that very [stem]", that is, in `purisa`, at each place.

---

<a name="r69"></a>

### 69 (§107): Sabbayonīnamāe (For both `yo`: `ā`, `e`) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m107" description="For both `yo`: `ā`, `e`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The plural of the first form; Kaccāyana's rule stands among the `a`-stem rules (§101–§108), Buddhappiya reaches it as the third step of his paradigm. The `vā` is a `vavatthitavibhāsā` whose three domains the verse names (Ruiz-Falqués, *Boundaries and Domains*, §3.3, translates this very verse); the `asanta` domain is the `jha` stem whose final [§96 (148): Yosvakatarasso jho](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m96) has made `a`, so that the plural of `aggi` is `aggayo`, never `aggā`. Kaccāyana's `Vāti kimatthaṃ?` answer, `aggayo, munayo, isayo`, is thus read as a statement of domain rather than as a free alternative.

:::

> A kārantato liṅgamhā paresaṃ sabbesaṃ paṭhamāyonīnaṃ, dutiyāyonīnañca yayākkamaṃ ākārekārādesā honti vāti ā kāro, sabba ggahaṇaṃ sabbādesatthaṃ, saralopādi purimasadisameva, purisā tiṭṭhanti.

After a stem ending in `a`, all the `yo` inflections of the first form ① and the `yo` inflections of the second form ② are optionally replaced by `ā` and `e` respectively; hence [here] `ā`. The word `sabba` ("all") is so that the whole [inflection] is replaced. The vowel elision and the rest are just as before: `purisā tiṭṭhanti`.

> Vā icceva rūpā rūpāni, aggayo, munayo.

Precisely because of `vā`: `rūpā` and `rūpāni`; `aggayo`, `munayo`.

> Vā saddoyaṃ vavatthitavibhāsattho[^5], tena cettha –

This word `vā` has the sense of a `vavatthitavibhāsā` (restricted option); hence on this point —

> Niccameva ca pulliṅge, aniccañca napuṃsake; \
> Asantaṃ jhe katatte tu, vidhiṃ dīpeti vā suti.

obligatory in the masculine, and not obligatory in the neuter, \
but inapplicable for a `jha` stem made to end in `a`: such is the operation the word `vā` shows.

> Tatthevālapanavacanicchāyaṃ ‘‘liṅgatthe paṭhamā’’ti vattate.

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express address, [65 (§284): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r65) continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(purisa) | purisā tiṭṭhanti | the men stand (`nicca`) |
| 🚻①⨂(rūpa) | rūpā, rūpāni | forms (`anicca`) |
| 🚹①⨂(aggi) | aggayo | fires (`asanta`: the `a` of `agga` + `yo` is not a stem's `a`) |
| 🚹①⨂(muni) | munayo | sages (`asanta`) |

|     | purisa + yo (①⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (yo→ā)        | §107 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + ā)    | §83  |
| →   | **purisā**             |      |

:::

[^5]: CST4 prints `vavatthitapibhāsattho`.

---

<a name="r70"></a>

### 70 (§285): Ālapane ca (and in address ⓪) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§285 (70): Ālapane ca" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m285" description="and in address ⓪" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states the vocative use of the first form in the Kāraka chapter, next to `Liṅgatthe paṭhamā`; Buddhappiya cites it only here, where the paradigm of `purisa` reaches the address forms, and does not repeat it in his Kārakakaṇḍa — the `(70)` of the Kaccāyana page is this citation. His gloss adds the etymology of `ālapana` to Kaccāyana's vutti. The next four rules then settle the singular address form. No option marker beyond the `ca`.

:::

> A bhimukhaṃ katvā lapanaṃ ālapanaṃ, sambodhanaṃ. Tasmiṃ ālapanatthādhike liṅgatthābhidhānamatte ca paṭhamāvibhatti hoti. Pure viya ekavacanādi.

Speaking (`lapana`) having turned face to face (`abhimukhaṃ katvā`) is `ālapana`, address (`sambodhana`). In the mere expression of the stem's meaning with that sense of address added, too, the first inflection ① is used. The singular and the rest as before.

> Purisa si iccatra –

Here, in `purisa` + `si` —

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | bho purisa | man! (`purisa` + `si`, completed under 71–74) |

:::

---

<a name="r71"></a>

### 71 (§57): Ālapane si gasañño (for vocatives `si` is called `ga`) :badge[rūḷhī]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§57 (71): Ālapane si ga sañño" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m57" description="for vocatives `si` is called `ga`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana defines the term `ga` immediately after the list of inflections (§57), with the other defining rules; Buddhappiya waits until the vocative `si` of `purisa` is actually in hand. The `ge` he fetches from [110 (§243): Bho ge tu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r110), forty suttas ahead, is needed because [72 (§244): Akārapitādyantānamā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r72) and [73 (§246): Ākāro vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r73), which in Kaccāyana follow `Bho ge tu` and inherit the word from it, are about to be cited out of that order. No option marker.

:::

> Ālapanatthe vihito si ga sañño hotīti ga saññāyaṃ ‘‘bho ge tū’’ti ito ‘‘ge’’ti vattate.

The `si` prescribed in the sense of address is named `ga`; so, [the `si`] being named `ga`, the word `ge` ("before `ga`") continues from [110 (§243): Bho ge tu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r110).

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | bho purisa | man! — the `si` of `purisa` + `si` is now `ga` |

:::

---

<a name="r72"></a>

### 72 (§244): Akārapitādyantānamā (`ā` for a final `a` and for the final of `pitu` and the like [before `ga`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§244 (72): Akārapitādyantānamā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m244" description="`ā` for a final `a` and for the final of `pitu` and the like [before `ga`]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

In Kaccāyana this rule follows `Bho ge tu` (§243) in the last section of the Nāmakappa, among the rules for `bhavanta`; Buddhappiya brings it, with [73 (§246): Ākāro vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r73) and [74 (§220): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r74), to the vocative of `purisa`, so that the three rules settle the address form in one place (the Kaccāyana page notes this order, 72–74). His gloss adds `liṅgassa sambandhī` to Kaccāyana's vutti. The `rassaṃ` it carries forward comes from [152 (§245): Jhalapā rassaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r152), another rule still to come. No option marker: before `ga` the `ā` is made without exception, and 73 then optionally shortens it.

:::

> Liṅgassa sambandhī a kāro ca pitusatthu iccevamādīnamanto ca ā kārattamāpajjate ge pare.

The `a` belonging to the stem, and the final of `pitu`, `satthu` and the like, become the letter `ā` when `ga` follows.

> ‘‘Ge, rassa’’miti ca vattate.

`ge` and `rassaṃ` ("short") continue.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | bho purisā | man! |
| 🚹⓪⨀(pitu) | bho pitā | father! |
| 🚹⓪⨀(satthu) | bho satthā | teacher! |

|     | purisa + ga (⓪⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (a→ā) + ga     | §244 |
| →   | purisā + ga            | (continued under 73 and 74) |

:::

---

<a name="r73"></a>

### 73 (§246): Ākāro vā (Optionally, `ā` [is shortened before `ga`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§246 (73): Ākāro vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m246" description="Optionally, `ā` [is shortened before `ga`]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The companion of [72 (§244): Akārapitādyantānamā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r72), taken over with it from Kaccāyana's last section; Kaccāyana's `Jhalapā rassaṃ` stands between the two, and Buddhappiya, who has moved that rule to the `i`-stems ([152 (§245): Jhalapā rassaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r152)), supplied its `rassaṃ` by `anuvutti` in the previous sutta. He glosses `vā` as `vikappena`: an open option (`vikappa`, Ruiz-Falqués §3.1), `bho purisa` and `bho purisā` both good Pāli. The condition of use — near or far — is his addition to the rule.

:::

> Liṅgassa sambandhī ā kāro rassamāpajjate ge pare vikappena, adūraṭṭhassālapanevāyaṃ.

The `ā` belonging to the stem becomes short when `ga` follows, optionally (`vikappena`); and this [shortening] is only in addressing one who is not far off.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | bho purisa | man! (near) |
| 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | bho purisā | man! (at a distance) |

|     | purisā + ga (⓪⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (ā→a) + ga     | §246 |
| →   | purisa + ga            | (the `ga` is elided under 74) |

:::

---

<a name="r74"></a>

### 74 (§220): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi (after the rest, `ga` and `si` too are elided) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m220" description="after the rest, `ga` and `si` too are elided" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The general rule that elides `si` and `ga` wherever no special rule has replaced them; Kaccāyana states it in his third section (§220), Buddhappiya at the point where the vocative `ga` of `purisa`, having done its work, must disappear. The `ñāpaka` he draws from the sutta's separate naming of `ga` is what keeps the `o` of [66 (§104): So](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r66) off the vocative: `puriso` is never an address form. No option marker of its own.

:::

> ‘‘Siṃ, so, syā ca, sakhato gasse vā, ghate cā’’ti evamādīhi niddiṭṭhehi añño seso nāma, tato sesato liṅgamhā gasi iccete lopamāpajjante.

What is other than [the stems] specified by [§219 (195): Siṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m219), [§104 (66): So](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m104), [§189 (113): Syā ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m189), [§113 (132): Sakhato gasse vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m113), [§114 (178): Ghate ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m114) and the like is called the rest (`sesa`); after such a remaining stem, `ga` and `si` are elided.

> Api ggahaṇaṃ dutiyatthasampiṇḍanatthaṃ, ettha ca satipi si ggahaṇe ga[^6] iti vacanameva ñāpakamaññatthāpi si ggahaṇe ālapanāggahaṇassa. Keci ālapanābhibyattiyā bhavanta saddaṃ vā he saddaṃ vā payujjante. Bho purisa tiṭṭha, he purisā vā.

The word `api` is for gathering in the second item [`si`]. And here, though `si` is mentioned, the very use of the word `ga` is the indicator (`ñāpaka`) that elsewhere too, where `si` is mentioned, the [`si` of] address is not included. Some, to make the address explicit, employ the word `bhavanta` or the word `he`: `bho purisa tiṭṭha` ("stand, man!"), or `he purisā`.

> Bahuvacane na viseso, bhavanto purisā tiṭṭhatha.

In the plural there is no difference: `bhavanto purisā tiṭṭhatha` ("stand, men!").

> Tattheva kammatthavacanicchāyaṃ ‘‘vā’’ti[^7] vattate.

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express the sense of the object (`kamma`), `vā` continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | bho purisa tiṭṭha | stand, man! |
| 🚹⓪⨀(purisa) | he purisā | O man! |
| 🚹⓪⨂(bhavanta) 🚹⓪⨂(purisa) | bhavanto purisā tiṭṭhatha | stand, men! |

|     | purisa + ga (⓪⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (a→ā) + ga     | §244 |
| →   | puris + (ā→a) + ga     | §246 (optional) |
| →   | purisa + ~~ga~~        | §220 |
| →   | **bho purisa**, **bho purisā** | |

|     | purisa + yo (⓪⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (yo→ā)        | §107 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + ā)    | §83  |
| →   | **bhavanto purisā**    |      |

:::

[^6]: CST4 prints `va iti vacanameva`; the argument turns on the sutta naming `ga` beside `si`, and `ga` is read here — so the Thai text, which [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) translates with `ga` ("'ga' is mentioned separately to indicate that here 'si' does not include 'ga'").

[^7]: CST4 prints `‘‘va,’’ti vattate`; the word carried over is the `vā` of 69, which the gloss of 75 reads as `yaṃ vā karoti`.

---

<a name="r75"></a>

### 75 (§280): Yaṃ karoti taṃ kammaṃ (what one does: `kamma`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§280 (285): Yaṃ karoti taṃ kammaṃ" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m280" description="what one does: `kamma`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana defines the six `kāraka` together in the Kāraka chapter and assigns the forms to them afterwards; Buddhappiya cites each definition twice — here, where his paradigm of `purisa` reaches the form that expresses it, and in his own Kārakakaṇḍa ([285 (§280): Yaṃ karoti taṃ kammaṃ](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r285)), where the full treatment is given. His gloss differs from Kaccāyana's vutti (`karoti`, `passati`, `suṇāti`) in its three verbs and adds `kriyānimittaṃ`. The `vā` of `yaṃ vā karoti` is the one [74 (§220): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r74) carried over; as in Kaccāyana, where it comes from §279, it is the open `vā` of the definitions, widening the class rather than choosing between forms (Ruiz-Falqués §3.1; the Kaccāyana page of §276 quotes the vutti's own `vāti vikappanatthaṃ`).

:::

> Yaṃ vā karoti, yaṃ vā vikaroti, yaṃ vā pāpuṇāti, taṃ kārakaṃ kriyānimittaṃ kammasaññaṃ hoti.

What one makes, or what one alters, or what one reaches — that `kāraka` (participant in the action), the occasion of the action, is termed `kamma` (the object).

:::tip[Examples]

The three verbs give the three kinds of object Buddhappiya names at 285.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨀(ghaṭa) | ghaṭaṃ karoti | he makes a pot (an object produced) |
| 🚹②⨂(kaṭṭha) | kaṭṭhe aṅgāre karoti | he turns firewood into charcoal (an object altered) |
| 🚹②⨀(gāma) | gāmaṃ pāpuṇāti | he reaches the village (an object reached) |

:::

---

<a name="r76"></a>

### 76 (§297): Kammatthe dutiyā (② for the `kamma` [object]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§297 (284): Kammatthe dutiyā" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m297" description="② for the `kamma` [object]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Cited here for the second form of `purisa` and again at [284 (§297): Kammatthe dutiyā](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r284), where the condition `anabhihite` and the further uses of the second form are added; here the gloss is only the derivation of `purisaṃ` and `purise`. No option marker.

:::

> Kammatthe dutiyāvibhatti hoti. Pure viya dutiyekavacanaṃ aṃ, ‘‘saralopo’’tiādinā sare lutte ‘‘dīgha’’nti dīghe sampatte pakatibhāvo ca, purisaṃ passa.

In the sense of the object the second inflection ② is used. As before, [the inflection is] the second-form singular `aṃ`; when the vowel has been elided by [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m83) and lengthening would apply by [§15 (17): Dīghaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m15), there is also the keeping of the natural form: `purisaṃ passa` ("see the man").

> Bahuvacane ‘‘sabbayonīnamāe’’ti yo vacanasse kāro, purise passa.

In the plural, `e` for the inflection `yo` by [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m107): `purise passa` ("see the men").

> Tattheva kattuvacanicchāyaṃ –

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express the agent —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨀(purisa) | purisaṃ passa | see the man |
| 🚹②⨂(purisa) | purise passa | see the men |

|     | purisa + aṃ (②⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + aṃ)   | §83  |
| →   | purisaṃ (not lengthened) | §83 `pakati` |
| →   | **purisaṃ**            |      |

|     | purisa + yo (②⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (yo→e)        | §107 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + e)    | §83  |
| →   | **purise**             |      |

:::

---

<a name="r77"></a>

### 77 (§281): Yo karoti sa kattā (the one who does: `kattā`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§281 (294): Yo karoti sa kattā" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m281" description="the one who does: `kattā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The agent is defined before the instrument, reversing Kaccāyana's order (§279 `karaṇa`, §280 `kamma`, §281 `kattā`), because Buddhappiya introduces the third form through the agent, as the Bālāvatāra does (¶51), and treats the instrument as taking the same forms ([83 (§286): Karaṇe tatiyā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r83), `sesaṃ kattusamaṃ`). The second citation is [294 (§281): Yo karoti sa kattā](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r294). His gloss adds `attappadhāno` to Kaccāyana's vutti. The `tatiyā` carried over is taken from 83, a rule still ahead, since in his order [78 (§288): Kattari ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r78), which needs the word, comes first. No option marker.

:::

> Yo attappadhāno kriyaṃ karoti, so kattusañño hoti.

He who, himself the principal (`attappadhāna`), performs the action is termed `kattā` (the agent).

> ‘‘Tatiyā’’ti vattate.

`tatiyā` ("the third") continues.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(purisa) | purisena kataṃ | done by the man (the agent, expressed by the third form under 78–79) |

:::

---

<a name="r78"></a>

### 78 (§288): Kattari ca (and for the agent) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§288 (293): Kattari ca" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m288" description="and for the agent" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

In Kaccāyana the `ca` of this rule extends `Karaṇe tatiyā` (§286) to the agent; Buddhappiya cites it first, before the instrument rule, so that the `ca` is read with the `tatiyā` he has just carried over from [83 (§286): Karaṇe tatiyā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r83), and the agent becomes the primary bearer of the third form. The second citation, [293 (§288): Kattari ca](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r293), adds the condition that the agent be not already expressed by the verb. No option marker.

:::

> Kattari ca kārake tatiyāvibhatti hotīti tatiyekavacanaṃ nā.

For the agent `kāraka` too the third inflection ③ is used; so [the inflection is] the third-form singular `nā`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(purisa) | purisena kataṃ | done by the man (`purisa` + `nā`, completed under 79) |

:::

---

<a name="r79"></a>

### 79 (§103): Ato nena (From `a`, `nā` becomes `ena`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§103 (79): Ato nena" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m103" description="From `a`, `nā` becomes `ena`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The rule whose `ato` Buddhappiya has been borrowing since [65 (§284): Liṅgatthe paṭhamā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r65) is now reached in its own place, the third form singular of `purisa`. Kaccāyana has it in the `a`-stem group (§101–§108) with its `ato` carried from §101 `Suhisvakāro e`; Buddhappiya makes it the head of the `ato` rules and lets the word run backwards to 66, 69 and 90. No option marker: after a stem in `a`, `nā` is always `ena`.

:::

> Enā ti avibhattikaniddeso. A kārantato liṅgamhā parassa nā vacanassa enā deso hoti, saralopādi, purisena kataṃ.

`Enā` is a citation without inflection (`avibhattikaniddesa`).[^8] The inflection `nā` following a stem ending in `a` is replaced by `ena`; the vowel elision and the rest: `purisena kataṃ` ("done by the man").

> Bahuvacanamhi –

In the plural —

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(purisa) | purisena kataṃ | done by the man |

|     | purisa + nā (③⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (nā→ena)      | §103 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + ena)  | §83  |
| →   | **purisena**           |      |

:::

[^8]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) inverts `Enāti avibhattikaniddeso`, making it say that the inflection is not denoted by the word `ena`; the Pāli says that `ena` is cited in its bare shape, without an inflection of its own — the sutta's `nena` is `nā` + `ena`, not a third-form word.

---

<a name="r80"></a>

### 80 (§101): Suhisvakāro e (In `su`, `hi`, `a` becomes `e`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m101" description="In `su`, `hi`, `a` becomes `e`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The first of Kaccāyana's `a`-stem rules (§101), reached by Buddhappiya at the third form plural, the first place where `hi` follows the stem; it will serve again for `su` at [94 (§302): Okāse sattamī](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r94). His `vibhattirūpesu`, "in the inflection-shapes", is an addition to Kaccāyana's vutti that covers the `bhi` of the next rule as well as `hi`. No option marker: `purisehi`, never `purisāhi`.

:::

> Su, hi iccetesu vibhattirūpesu paresu liṅgassa sambandhī a kāro e ttamāpajjate.

When the inflection-shapes `su` and `hi` follow, the `a` belonging to the stem becomes `e`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(purisa) | purisehi | by the men |
| 🚹⑦⨂(purisa) | purisesu | among the men |

|     | purisa + hi (③⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (a→e) + hi     | §101 |
| →   | **purisehi**           |      |

:::

---

<a name="r81"></a>

### 81 (§99): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā (Optionally, `smā`, `hi`, `smiṃ` become `mhā`, `bhi`, `mhi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m99" description="Optionally, `smā`, `hi`, `smiṃ` become `mhā`, `bhi`, `mhi`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana gives this rule for all stems (§99, `sabbato liṅgato`), just before the `a`-stem group; Buddhappiya cites it at the third form plural of `purisa`, where its `bhi` first shows, and refers back to it for `mhā` at [90 (§108): Smāsmiṃna vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r90) and for `mhi` at [94 (§302): Okāse sattamī](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r94). The `vā` is an open option (`vikappa`): `purisehi` and `purisebhi` are both good Pāli, and neither is confined to a domain — the reading the Kaccāyana page also gives.

:::

> Sabbato liṅgamhā smā hi smiṃ iccetesaṃ yathākkamaṃ mhā, bhi, mhi iccete ādesā honti vā, purisehi, purisebhi kataṃ.

After every stem, `smā`, `hi` and `smiṃ` are optionally replaced by `mhā`, `bhi` and `mhi` respectively: `purisehi`, `purisebhi kataṃ` ("done by the men").

> Tattheva karaṇavacanicchāyaṃ –

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express the instrument —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(purisa) | purisehi kataṃ | done by the men |
| 🚹③⨂(purisa) | purisebhi kataṃ | done by the men |

|     | purisa + hi (③⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (a→e) + hi     | §101 |
| →   | purise + (hi→bhi)      | §99 (optional) |
| →   | **purisehi**, **purisebhi** | |

:::

---

<a name="r82"></a>

### 82 (§279): Yena vā kayirate taṃ karaṇaṃ (that by which [the action] is done: `karaṇa`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§279 (292): Yena vā kayirate taṃ karaṇaṃ" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m279" description="that by which [the action] is done: `karaṇa`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The definition of the instrument, cited here after the agent's forms and again at [292 (§279): Yena vā kayirate taṃ karaṇaṃ](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r292), where Buddhappiya adds the gloss `karīyate anenāti karaṇaṃ` and the division into inner and outer instruments; here he repeats Kaccāyana's vutti almost word for word. The `vā` is the open `vā` of the definitions, widening the class beyond the one verb the sutta names; in Kaccāyana it also carries over into the definition of the object, a service Buddhappiya's order does not need, since he has already supplied that `vā` from 74.

:::

> Yena vā kayirate, yena vā passati, yena vā suṇāti, taṃ kārakaṃ karaṇasaññaṃ hoti.

That by which [something] is done,[^9] or by which one sees, or by which one hears — that `kāraka` is termed `karaṇa` (the instrument).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(purisa) | āviṭṭhena purisena | through the man he has entered (the instrument, under 83) |
| 🚻③⨀(cakkhu) | cakkhunā passati | he sees with the eye |
| 🚹③⨀(sota) | sotena suṇāti | he hears with the ear |

:::

[^9]: CST4 prints `kayirate`, with the metathesis of `kariyate`, here as in the Kaccāyana sutta; the sutta as [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) prints it from the Thai edition reads `kariyate`, the regular passive, though she gives no apparatus for the word.

---

<a name="r83"></a>

### 83 (§286): Karaṇe tatiyā (the third form ③ for the instrument) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§286 (291): Karaṇe tatiyā" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m286" description="the third form ③ for the instrument" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's primary rule for the third form, cited by Buddhappiya after the agent rules that borrowed its `tatiyā` ([77 (§281): Yo karoti sa kattā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r77), [78 (§288): Kattari ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r78)); the second citation is [291 (§286): Karaṇe tatiyā](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r291). His example, `āviṭṭhena purisena so puññaṃ karoti`, shows a man used as an instrument; the Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (PDṬ p. 30, cited by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong)) spells it out: a non-human being (`amanussa`) that has entered a man does merit — salutations and the like — through him, `so amanusso abhivādanādīni karonto puññaṃ karoti`. No option marker.

:::

> Karaṇakārake tatiyāvibhatti hoti, sesaṃ kattusamaṃ, āviṭṭhena purisena so puññaṃ karoti, purisehi, purisebhi.

For the instrument `kāraka` the third inflection ③ is used; the rest is the same as for the agent: `āviṭṭhena purisena so puññaṃ karoti` ("through the man he has entered, he does a good deed"); `purisehi`, `purisebhi`.

> Tattheva sampadānavacanicchāyaṃ –

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express the recipient —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(purisa) | āviṭṭhena purisena so puññaṃ karoti | through the man he has entered, he does a good deed |
| 🚹③⨂(purisa) | purisehi, purisebhi | through the men |

:::

---

<a name="r84"></a>

### 84 (§276): Yassa dātukāmo rocate dhārayate vā taṃ sampadānaṃ (the one to whom one wishes to give, who is pleased, or to whom one owes: `sampadāna`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§276 (302): Yassa dātukāmo rocate dhārayate vā taṃ sampadānaṃ" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m276" description="the one to whom one wishes to give, who is pleased, or to whom one owes: `sampadāna`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The definition of the recipient, cited before the fourth form of `purisa` and again at [302 (§276): Yassa dātukāmo rocate dhārayate vā taṃ sampadānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r302), where the gloss `sammā padīyate assāti sampadānaṃ`, the note that `dhārayate` means owing a debt and the counter-example of a fine paid to the king are given. Here the gloss is Kaccāyana's vutti sentence; its `vā` is the open `vā` of the definitions, which Kaccāyana's own vutti of this rule explains as `vāti vikappanatthaṃ`.

:::

> Yassa vā dātukāmo, yassa vā rocate, yassa vā dhārayate, taṃ kārakaṃ sampadānasaññaṃ hoti.

The one to whom one wishes to give, or to whom [something] is pleasing, or to whom one owes[^10] — that `kāraka` is termed `sampadāna` (the recipient).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(purisa) | purisassa dhanaṃ dadāti | he gives wealth to the man (under 86) |
| 🚹④⨀(samaṇa) | samaṇassa rocate saccaṃ | the truth pleases the ascetic |

:::

[^10]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) renders `yassa vā rocate, yassa vā dhārayate` as "one who entertains by something or one from whom something is borne"; the Pāli names the one to whom something is pleasing and the one to whom one owes — Buddhappiya's own note at [302 (§276): Yassa dātukāmo rocate dhārayate vā taṃ sampadānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r302) makes `dhārayate` the owing of a debt.

---

<a name="r85"></a>

### 85 (§293): Sampadāne catutthī (④ for the `sampadāna` [recipient]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§293 (85, 301): Sampadāne catutthī" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m293" description="④ for the `sampadāna` [recipient]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The primary rule of the fourth form, cited here for the paradigm — the Kaccāyana page counts this as its Rūpasiddhi number — and again at [301 (§293): Sampadāne catutthī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r301) in the Kārakakaṇḍa. No option marker.

:::

> Sampadānakārake catutthīvibhatti hotīti catutthiyā ekavacanaṃ sa.

For the recipient `kāraka` the fourth inflection ④ is used; so [the inflection is] the fourth-form singular `sa`.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(purisa) | purisassa | to the man (`purisa` + `sa`, completed under 86) |

:::

---

<a name="r86"></a>

### 86 (§61): Sāgamo se (Before `sa` inflections: insert `s`) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§61 (86): Sāgamo se" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m61" description="Before `sa` inflections: insert `s`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states the augment `s` early, among the defining rules (§61); Buddhappiya cites it where the fourth form of `purisa` first needs it and refers to it again for the sixth ([92 (§301): Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhī](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r92)). No option marker.

:::

> Sabbato liṅgamhā sa kārāgamo hoti se vibhattimhi pare. Purisassa dhanaṃ dadāti.

After every stem the augment `s` is inserted when the inflection `sa` follows: `purisassa dhanaṃ dadāti` ("he gives wealth to the man").

> Bahuvacanamhi ‘‘dīgha’’nti vattate.

In the plural, `dīghaṃ` ("long") continues.[^11]

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(purisa) | purisassa dhanaṃ dadāti | he gives wealth to the man |

|     | purisa + sa (④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (s) + sa      | §61  |
| →   | **purisassa**          |      |

:::

[^11]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) refers this `dīghaṃ` to the sandhi rule [§15 (17): Dīghaṃ](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m15) ("the word 'dīghaṃ' (15-17) is to be understood"); it is the `dīghaṃ` of [§88 (147): Yosu katanikāralopesu dīghaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m88), which [87 (§89): Sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r87) continues in Kaccāyana's own order, and which Buddhappiya's gloss of [117 (§169): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r117) cites as `‘‘sunaṃhisu cā’’ti dīgho`.

---

<a name="r87"></a>

### 87 (§89): Sunaṃhisu ca (And in `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m89" description="And in `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The lengthening of a final short vowel before `su`, `naṃ` and `hi`, needed first for `purisānaṃ`. In Kaccāyana it follows §88, from which its `dīghaṃ` is understood; Buddhappiya has separated the two — §88 belongs to the `i`-stems in his order ([147 (§88): Yosu katanikāralopesu dīghaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r147)) — and supplied `dīghaṃ` by the `anuvutti` stated at [86 (§61): Sāgamo se](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r86). The `ca` he reads as `kvaci nivattanatthaṃ` Kaccāyana's vutti calls `avadhāraṇa`, with the examples `sukhettesu brahmacārisu`, `bhikkhunaṃ`, `pāṇibhi`; either way it does the work of `kvaci`, marking an exception within the rule's own scope for stems in `i` and `u`, and the `a` stems are untouched by it — `purisānaṃ` has no short alternative.

:::

> Su naṃ hi iccetesu paresu liṅgassa antabhūtā sabbe rassasarā dīghamāpajjante, ca ggahaṇamikārukārānaṃ kvaci nivattanatthaṃ. Purisānaṃ.

When `su`, `naṃ` or `hi` follows, all short vowels standing at the end of a stem become long; the word `ca` is to block [the lengthening] of `i` and `u` in some places. `purisānaṃ`.

> Tatthevāpādānavacanicchāyaṃ –

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express the point of departure —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨂(purisa) | purisānaṃ | to the men |
| 🚹③⨂(aggi) | aggīhi, aggihi | by the fires (the `ca` blocks the lengthening in some places) |

|     | purisa + naṃ (④⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (a→ā) + naṃ    | §89  |
| →   | **purisānaṃ**          |      |

:::

---

<a name="r88"></a>

### 88 (§271): Yasmā dapeti bhayamādatte vā tadapādānaṃ (that from which one departs, fears or takes: `apādāna`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§271 (88, 308): Yasmā dapeti bhayamādatte vā tadapādānaṃ" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m271" description="that from which one departs, fears or takes: `apādāna`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's opening definition of the Kāraka chapter (§271), cited here before the fifth form of `purisa` and worked out at [308 (§271): Yasmādapeti bhayamādatte vā tadapādānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r308). The gloss adds one word to Kaccāyana's vutti, `avadhibhūtā`. The `vā` is the open `vā` of the definitions, joining the three clauses; the class stays open to Kaccāyana's §272–§275.

:::

> Yasmā vā avadhibhūtā apeti, yasmā vā bhayaṃ, yasmā vā ādatte, taṃ kārakaṃ apādānasaññaṃ hoti.

That from which, as a limit (`avadhi`), one departs, or from which fear [arises], or from which one takes — that `kāraka` is termed `apādāna` (the point of departure).

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑤⨀(purisa) | purisā apeti | he goes away from the man (the point of departure, under 90) |

:::

---

<a name="r89"></a>

### 89 (§295): Apādāne pañcamī (⑤ for the `apādāna` [point of departure]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§295 (307): Apādāne pañcamī" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m295" description="⑤ for the `apādāna` [point of departure]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The primary rule of the fifth form, cited here and at [307 (§295): Apādāne pañcamī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r307). The words it carries over — `ato`, `sabbesaṃ`, `ā e` — come from [69 (§107): Sabbayonīnamāe](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r69) (`ato` itself from [79 (§103): Ato nena](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r79)) and let [90 (§108): Smāsmiṃna vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r90) be read as "after `a`, the whole of `smā` and `smiṃ` become `ā` and `e`". No option marker.

:::

> Apādānakārake pañcamīvibhatti hotīti pañcamiyā ekavacanaṃ smā.

For the point-of-departure `kāraka` the fifth inflection ⑤ is used; so [the inflection is] the fifth-form singular `smā`.

> ‘‘Ato, sabbesaṃ, ā e’’ti ca vattate.

`ato`, `sabbesaṃ` and `ā e` continue.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑤⨀(purisa) | purisā apeti | he goes away from the man (`purisa` + `smā`, completed under 90) |

:::

---

<a name="r90"></a>

### 90 (§108): Smāsmiṃna vā (Optionally, for `smā`, `smiṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m108" description="Optionally, for `smā`, `smiṃ`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last of Kaccāyana's `a`-stem rules (§108), cited by Buddhappiya at the fifth form singular and used again for `smiṃ` at [94 (§302): Okāse sattamī](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r94). His `aññattha mhādeso` is an addition to Kaccāyana's vutti, pointing to the `mhā` of [81 (§99): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r81). The `vā` is an open option among three forms, `purisā`, `purisamhā`, `purisasmā`, all good Pāli; Buddhappiya reads no `vavatthitavibhāsā` into it, and Ruiz-Falqués lists no domain for it.

:::

> A kārantato liṅgamhā sabbesaṃ smā smiṃ iccetesaṃ yathākkamaṃ ākārekārādesā honti vā, aññattha mhā deso. Purisā apeti, purisamhā, purisasmā.

After a stem ending in `a`, the whole of `smā` and `smiṃ` are optionally replaced by `ā` and `e` respectively; elsewhere [there is] the replacement `mhā`. `purisā apeti` ("he goes away from the man"), `purisamhā`, `purisasmā`.

> Bahuvacane sabbattha tatiyāsamaṃ, hi ssa bhi ādeso hoti. Purisehi, purisebhi apeti.

In the plural, everywhere the same as the third form; `hi` is replaced by `bhi`. `purisehi`, `purisebhi apeti`.

> Tattheva sāmivacanicchāyaṃ –

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express the owner —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑤⨀(purisa) | purisā apeti | he goes away from the man |
| 🚹⑤⨀(purisa) | purisamhā, purisasmā | from the man |
| 🚹⑤⨂(purisa) | purisehi, purisebhi apeti | he goes away from the men |

|     | purisa + smā (⑤⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (smā→ā)       | §108 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + ā)    | §83  |
| →   | **purisā**             |      |

|     | purisa + smā (⑤⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (smā→mhā)     | §99 (optional) |
| →   | **purisamhā**, or unchanged **purisasmā** | |

:::

---

<a name="r91"></a>

### 91 (§283): Yassa vā pariggaho taṃ sāmi (the one whose possession [a thing] is: `sāmī`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§283 (316): Yassa vā pariggaho taṃ sāmī" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m283" description="the one whose possession [a thing] is: `sāmī`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The definition of the owner, the last of Kaccāyana's definitions (§283), cited here — with the spelling `sāmi`, where Kaccāyana's sutta and the Thai edition read by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) have `sāmī` — before the sixth form of `purisa`, and again at [316 (§283): Yassa vā pariggaho taṃ sāmī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r316), where Buddhappiya explains why the owner is not strictly a `kāraka`. Here his gloss is Kaccāyana's vutti sentence without the word `kāraka`, a deliberate omission, as the Kaccāyana page notes. The `vā` is the open `vā` of the definitions, which at 316 he uses to admit further constructions.

:::

> Yassa vā pariggaho, taṃ sāmisaññaṃ hoti.

The one whose possession (`pariggaha`) [a thing] is — that is termed `sāmī` (the owner).

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(purisa) | purisassa etaṃ dhanaṃ | this wealth is the man's (the owner, under 92) |

:::

---

<a name="r92"></a>

### 92 (§301): Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhī (⑥ for the `sāmī` [owner]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§301 (315): Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhī" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m301" description="⑥ for the `sāmī` [owner]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The primary rule of the sixth form, cited here and at [315 (§301): Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r315). The observation his gloss adds — that the fourth and sixth forms coincide except for the `āya` of [304 (§109): Āya catutthekavacanassa tu](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r304) — saves him every further sixth-form derivation in the chapter. No option marker.

:::

> Sāmismiṃ chaṭṭhīvibhatti hoti. Ṭhapetvā āyā desaṃ sabbattha catutthīchaṭṭhīnaṃ samānaṃ rūpaṃ. Purisassa etaṃ dhanaṃ, purisānaṃ.

For the owner the sixth inflection ⑥ is used. Except for the replacement `āya` [of [§109 (304): Āya catutthekavacanassatu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m109)], the fourth and the sixth form have the same shape everywhere. `purisassa etaṃ dhanaṃ` ("this wealth is the man's"), `purisānaṃ`.

> Tattheva okāsavacanicchāyaṃ –

In that very [stem], when there is the wish to express the location —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨀(purisa) | purisassa etaṃ dhanaṃ | this wealth is the man's |
| 🚹⑥⨂(purisa) | purisānaṃ | of the men |

:::

---

<a name="r93"></a>

### 93 (§278): Yodhāro tamokāsaṃ (the support of the action: `okāsa`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§278 (320): Yodhāro tamokāsaṃ" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m278" description="the support of the action: `okāsa`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The definition of the location, cited here before the seventh form of `purisa` and again at [320 (§278): Yodhāro tamokāsaṃ](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r320), where the four kinds of support are set out. His gloss adds `kattukammānaṃ kriyāya` to Kaccāyana's vutti. No option marker.

:::

> Yo kattukammānaṃ kriyāya ādhāro, taṃ kārakaṃ okāsasaññaṃ hoti.

What is the support (`ādhāra`) of the action of agent and object — that `kāraka` is termed `okāsa` (the location).

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(purisa) | purise patiṭṭhitaṃ | established in the man (the location, under 94) |

:::

---

<a name="r94"></a>

### 94 (§302): Okāse sattamī (⑦ for the `okāsa` [location]) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§302 (319): Okāse sattamī" href="/kaccayana/3-karakakappa#m302" description="⑦ for the `okāsa` [location]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The primary rule of the seventh form, cited here and at [319 (§302): Okāse sattamī](/rupasiddhi/3-karakakanda#r319), completes the paradigm of `purisa`. Buddhappiya then does what Kaccāyana never does: he sets out the whole `padamālā`, repeats it for `sugata`, lists in three verses the masculine `a` stems that follow it, and opens the exceptions with the `mano`-group. No option marker in the rule; the three-way choice for `smiṃ` is the `vā` of 90 and 81.

:::

> Okāsakārake sattamīvibhatti hotīti sattamiyā ekavacanaṃ smiṃ, tassa ‘‘smāsmiṃnaṃ vā’’ti e kāro, mhi ādeso ca, purise patiṭṭhitaṃ, purisamhi, purisasmiṃ.

For the location `kāraka` the seventh inflection ⑦ is used; so [the inflection is] the seventh-form singular `smiṃ`, which becomes `e` by [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m108) or is replaced by `mhi` [by [§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m99)]: `purise patiṭṭhitaṃ` ("established in the man"), `purisamhi`, `purisasmiṃ`.

> Bahuvacane ‘‘suhisvakāro e’’ti e kāro, purisesu.

In the plural, `e` by [§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m101): `purisesu`.

> Puriso, purisā, bho purisa bho purisā vā, bhavanto purisā, purisaṃ, purise, purisena, purisehi purisebhi, purisassa, purisānaṃ, purisā purisasmā purisamhā, purisehi purisebhi, purisassa, purisānaṃ, purise purisasmiṃ purisamhi, purisesu.

The whole paradigm of `purisa` ("a man"):

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | puriso | purisā |
| ⓪ | bho purisa, bho purisā | bhavanto purisā |
| ② | purisaṃ | purise |
| ③ | purisena | purisehi, purisebhi |
| ④ | purisassa | purisānaṃ |
| ⑤ | purisā, purisasmā, purisamhā | purisehi, purisebhi |
| ⑥ | purisassa | purisānaṃ |
| ⑦ | purise, purisasmiṃ, purisamhi | purisesu |

> Tathā sugato, sugatā, bho sugata bho sugatā vā, bhavanto sugatā, sugataṃ, sugate, sugatena, sugatehi sugatebhi, sugatassa, sugatānaṃ, sugatā sugatasmā sugatamhā, sugatehi sugatebhi, sugatassa, sugatānaṃ, sugate sugatasmiṃ sugatamhi, sugatesu.

In the same way `sugata` ("the Well-gone One"):

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | sugato | sugatā |
| ⓪ | bho sugata, bho sugatā | bhavanto sugatā |
| ② | sugataṃ | sugate |
| ③ | sugatena | sugatehi, sugatebhi |
| ④ | sugatassa | sugatānaṃ |
| ⑤ | sugatā, sugatasmā, sugatamhā | sugatehi, sugatebhi |
| ⑥ | sugatassa | sugatānaṃ |
| ⑦ | sugate, sugatasmiṃ, sugatamhi | sugatesu |

> Evaṃ surā’sura naro’raga nāga yakkhā, \
> Gandhabba kinnara manussa pisāca petā; \
> Mātaṅga jaṅgama turaṅga varāha sīhā, \
> Byaggha’ccha kacchapa taraccha miga’ssa soṇā.

So too `sura` (god), `asura` (titan), `nara` (man), `uraga` (snake), `nāga` (nāga), `yakkha` (yakkha), \
`gandhabba` (celestial musician), `kinnara` (kinnara), `manussa` (human being), `pisāca` (goblin), `peta` (ghost); \
`mātaṅga` (elephant), `jaṅgama` (moving creature), `turaṅga` (horse), `varāha` (boar), `sīha` (lion), \
`byaggha` (tiger), `accha` (bear), `kacchapa` (tortoise), `taraccha` (hyena), `miga` (deer), `assa` (horse), `soṇa` (dog).

> Āloka loka nilayā’nila cāga yogā, vāyāma gāma \
> Nigamā’gama dhamma kāmā; \
> Saṅgho’gha ghosa paṭighā’sava kodha lobhā, \
> Sārambha thambha mada māna pamāda makkhā.

`āloka` (light), `loka` (world), `nilaya` (abode), `anila` (wind), `cāga` (generosity), `yoga` (bond), `vāyāma` (endeavour), `gāma` (village), \
`nigama` (market town), `āgama` (scripture), `dhamma` (teaching), `kāma` (sense pleasure); \
`saṅgha` (community), `ogha` (flood), `ghosa` (sound), `paṭigha` (repulsion), `āsava` (defilement), `kodha` (anger), `lobha` (greed), \
`sārambha` (contentiousness), `thambha` (obstinacy), `mada` (intoxication), `māna` (conceit), `pamāda` (negligence), `makkha` (denigration).

> Punnāga pūga panasā’sana campaka’mbā, \
> Hintāla tāla bakula’jjuna kiṃsukā ca; \
> Mandāra kunda pucimanda karañja rukkhā, \
> Ñeyyā mayūra sakunaṇḍaja koñca haṃsā –

`punnāga` (Alexandrian laurel), `pūga` (betel palm), `panasa` (jackfruit), `asana` (asana tree), `campaka` (champak), `amba` (mango), \
