`hintāla` (marsh date palm), `tāla` (palmyra palm), `bakula` (bakula), `ajjuna` (arjuna), and `kiṃsuka` (bastard teak); \
`mandāra` (coral tree), `kunda` (jasmine), `pucimanda` (neem), `karañja` (Indian beech), `rukkha` (tree) \
are to be known [as such], and `mayūra` (peacock), `sakuṇa` (bird), `aṇḍaja` (egg-born bird), `koñca` (crane), `haṃsa` (swan) —

> Iccādayopi.

and others like these.

> Manogaṇādissa tu nāsasmāsmiṃsu viseso. Aññattha purisasamaṃ.

But the `mano`-group and the like differ before `nā`, `sa`, `smā` and `smiṃ`. Elsewhere they are the same as `purisa`.

> Mano, manā, he mana he manā vā, bhavanto manā, manaṃ, mane.

`mano` ("mind"), `manā`; `he mana` or `he manā`, `bhavanto manā`; `manaṃ`, `mane`.

> ‘‘Vā’’ti vattate.

`vā` continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(purisa) | purise patiṭṭhitaṃ | established in the man |
| 🚹⑦⨀(purisa) | purisamhi, purisasmiṃ | in the man |
| 🚹⑦⨂(purisa) | purisesu | among the men |
| 🚹①⨀(mana) | mano | mind (the `si` elided, the final made `o`) |
| 🚹①⨂(mana) | manā | minds |
| 🚹⓪⨀(mana) | he mana, he manā | O mind! |
| 🚹②⨀(mana) | manaṃ | mind |
| 🚹②⨂(mana) | mane | minds |

|     | purisa + smiṃ (⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (smiṃ→e)      | §108 |
| →   | puris + (~~a~~ + e)    | §83  |
| →   | **purise**             |      |

|     | purisa + smiṃ (⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | purisa + (smiṃ→mhi)    | §99 (optional) |
| →   | **purisamhi**, or unchanged **purisasmiṃ** | |

|     | purisa + su (⑦⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | puris + (a→e) + su     | §101 |
| →   | **purisesu**           |      |

|     | mana + si (①⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + ~~si~~          | §220 |
| →   | man + (a→o)            | §183 |
| →   | **mano**               |      |

:::

---

<a name="r95"></a>

### 95 (§181): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā (`i`, `ā` for `smiṃ`, `nā` after the `mano`-group) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§181 (95): Manogaṇādito smiṃnānamiā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m181" description="`i`, `ā` for `smiṃ`, `nā` after the `mano`-group" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana treats the `mano`-group in his third section (§181–§184), between the `sakha` and `santa` rules; Buddhappiya attaches it to the `a`-stem paradigm as the first group of exceptions, since these stems are `purisa` in every form but four. Like Kaccāyana's vutti, his `ādi` reaches `bila` and `pada`, words outside the old `-as` stems. The `vā` is the one [94 (§302): Okāse sattamī](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r94) carried over; here it is an open option, `manasi` beside `mane`, and Buddhappiya restricts it only in the next rule, for the augment `s` on which these replacements depend.

:::

> Manopabhuti gaṇo manogaṇo, manogaṇādito smiṃ, nā iccetesaṃ yathākkamaṃ ikārākārādesā honti vā. Ādi ggahaṇena bilapadā ditopi.

The group beginning with `mano`[^12] is the `manogaṇa`; after the `mano`-group and the like, `smiṃ` and `nā` are optionally replaced by `i` and `ā` respectively. By the word `ādi`, also after `bila`, `pada` and the like.

> ‘‘Manogaṇādito’’ti vattate.

`manogaṇādito` ("after the `mano`-group and the like") continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(mana) | manasi | in the mind |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manasā | by the mind |
| 🚻⑦⨀(bila) | bilasi | in the hole |
| 🚻③⨀(pada) | padasā | on foot |

|     | mana + smiṃ (⑦⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (smiṃ→i)        | §181 |
| →   | mana + (s) + i         | §184 |
| →   | **manasi**             |      |

:::

[^12]: CST4 prints `manopabhuti`, the Burmese and Sinhalese reading; the Thai edition read by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) has `manoppabhūti`, and writes the long vowel throughout the chapter — `bhikkhuppabhūtito` at 155, `mahāmandhātupabhūtayo` at 167, where the CST has `bhikkhuppabhutito`, `mahāmandhātuppabhutayo` — though she judges the short `pabhuti` the better reading (her Explanatory Note on rule 95).

---

<a name="r96"></a>

### 96 (§184): Sa sare vāgamo (Optionally `s` inserted before a vowel) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§184 (96): Sa sare vāgamo" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m184" description="Optionally `s` inserted before a vowel" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states the augment `s` after the `o` rules of the group (§184); Buddhappiya cites it directly after the `i` and `ā` replacements, because those begin with a vowel and the `s` is what turns `mana` + `ā` into `manasā`. The `vā` he declares a `vavatthitavibhāsā`, stating its `asanta` and `nicca` domains and leaving the `anicca` — the option itself — between them; in the `asanta` words the vowel that follows is not a replacement of `smiṃ`, `nā` or `sa`. The Thai handbook *Padarūpasiddhi Plae lae Athibai* (PDP p. 127, cited by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong)) states the same three domains and adds the fourth-form pair `manaso`, `manassa` to the `anicca` examples. Kaccāyana's vutti reaches the `nicca` domain through a second `ādi` ("another suffix too"); Buddhappiya reaches it through the domains of the `vā` (Ruiz-Falqués §3.2 on the standard formula `vavatthitavibhāsatthoyaṃ vāsaddo`).

:::

> Eteheva manogaṇādīhi sare pare sā gamo hoti vā. Manasā, manena.

After these same stems of the `mano`-group and the like, when a vowel follows, the augment `s` is optionally inserted: `manasā`, `manena`.

> Vavatthitavibhāsatthoyaṃ vā saddo, tena ‘‘mano manā manaṃ mane manaāyatana’’ntiādīsu na hoti. ‘‘Mānasikaṃ, cetasika’’ntiādīsu niccaṃ. Manehi, manebhi.

This word `vā` has the sense of a `vavatthitavibhāsā` (restricted option): hence in `mano`, `manā`, `manaṃ`, `mane`, `manaāyatanaṃ` and the like it does not occur, and in `mānasikaṃ`, `cetasikaṃ` and the like it is obligatory. `manehi`, `manebhi`.

> ‘‘Manogaṇādito, vā’’ti ca vattate.

`manogaṇādito` and `vā` continue.[^13]

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manasā | by the mind (`anicca`: the option taken) |
| 🚹③⨀(mana) | manena | by the mind (`anicca`: the option not taken) |
| 🚹④⨀(mana) | manaso | to the mind (`anicca`: the option taken, with the `o` of 97) |
| 🚹④⨀(mana) | manassa | to the mind (`anicca`: the option not taken) |
| 🚹③⨂(mana) | manehi, manebhi | by the minds |
| 🚻①⨀(mana + ṇika) | mānasikaṃ | mental (`nicca`) |
| 🚻①⨀(ceta + ṇika) | cetasikaṃ | mental (`nicca`) |
| 🚻①⨀(mana + āyatana) | manaāyatanaṃ | the mind-base (`asanta`) |

|     | mana + nā (③⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (nā→ā)          | §181 |
| →   | mana + (s) + ā         | §184 |
| →   | **manasā**             |      |

|     | mana + nā (③⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (nā→ena)        | §103 |
| →   | man + (~~a~~ + ena)    | §83  |
| →   | **manena**             |      |

:::

[^13]: The CST's `‘‘Manogaṇādito, vā’’ti ca vattate` is the Burmese reading; the Thai and Sinhalese editions carry over `vā` alone, `vāti vattate` (Rachiwong 1995, Explanatory Note on rule 96; she prefers the Burmese, since [97 (§182): Sassa co](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r97) needs both words).

---

<a name="r97"></a>

### 97 (§182): Sassa co (`o` for `sa` [after the `mano`-group]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§182 (97): Sassa co" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m182" description="`o` for `sa` [after the `mano`-group]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The third `mano`-group rule. Kaccāyana's `ca` extends the replacements of §181 to `sa`; Buddhappiya cites the rule after the augment, so that `sāgamo` can be added in a word, and reads the carried-over `vā` into it — `manaso` beside `manassa`, both good Pāli, as the Kaccāyana page also reads the rule. The verse lists the group, the old `-as` stems. The fifth form singular shows no `manasā`: [120 (§270): Amhatumhanturājabrahmattasakhasatthupitādīhi smā nāva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r120) does not list the `mano`-group, so its `smā` takes the ordinary `ā`, `smā`, `mhā`. The governing words stated for the `ntu` stems, `savibhattissa` and `ntussa`, are taken from [243 (§120): Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r243) and [99 (§122): Ntussa nto](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r99).

:::

> Manogaṇādito parassa sa ssa vibhattissa o kāro hoti vā. Sā gamo.

After the `mano`-group and the like the inflection `sa` optionally becomes `o`; [and there is] the augment `s`.

> Manaso manassa, manānaṃ, manā manasmā manamhā, manehi manebhi, manaso manassa, manānaṃ, manasi mane manasmiṃ manamhi, manesu.

The paradigm of `mana` ("mind"), with the forms given under 94 and 96:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | mano | manā |
| ⓪ | he mana, he manā | bhavanto manā |
| ② | manaṃ | mane |
| ③ | manasā, manena | manehi, manebhi |
| ④ | manaso, manassa | manānaṃ |
| ⑤ | manā, manasmā, manamhā | manehi, manebhi |
| ⑥ | manaso, manassa | manānaṃ |
| ⑦ | manasi, mane, manasmiṃ, manamhi | manesu |

> Evaṃ vaco vayo tejo, \
> Tapo ceto tamo yaso; \
> Ayo payo siro chando, \
> Saro uro raho aho –

In the same way `vaco` (speech), `vayo` (age), `tejo` (heat), \
`tapo` (austerity), `ceto` (mind), `tamo` (darkness), `yaso` (fame), \
`ayo` (iron), `payo` (milk), `siro` (head), `chando` (metre), \
`saro` (lake), `uro` (chest), `raho` (solitude), `aho` (day) —

> Iccādi manogaṇo.

and the like are the `mano`-group.

> Guṇavantu saddassa bhedo. Guṇavantu si itīdha –

The word `guṇavantu` ("virtuous") differs. Here, in `guṇavantu` + `si` —

> ‘‘Savibhattissa, ntussā’’ti ca adhikāro.

`savibhattissa` ("together with its inflection") and `ntussa` ("of `ntu`") are the governing words.[^14]

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(mana) | manaso | to the mind |
| 🚹④⨀(mana) | manassa | to the mind |
| 🚹①⨀(vaca) | vaco | speech |
| 🚹①⨀(teja) | tejo | heat |

|     | mana + sa (④⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (sa→o)          | §182 |
| →   | mana + (s) + o         | §184 |
| →   | **manaso**             |      |

|     | mana + sa (④⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | mana + (s) + sa        | §61  |
| →   | **manassa**            |      |

:::

[^14]: The CST's `‘‘Savibhattissa, ntussā’’ti ca adhikāro` is the Burmese reading; the Thai and Sinhalese editions read `savibhattissa ntussanto, sabbasseva ntussāti ca` ([Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong), Explanatory Note on rule 97; she follows the Burmese and calls the second phrase redundant), a wording that would also be the source of the `sabbasseva` opening every gloss from 98 to 104. The Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā, as she cites it, takes `savibhattissa` from [243 (§120): Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r243) and `ntussa` from [99 (§122): Ntussa nto](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r99), as the Analysis does.

---

<a name="r98"></a>

### 98 (§124): Ā simhi (`ā` for `ntu` before `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§124 (98): Ā simhi" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m124" description="`ā` for `ntu` before `si`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The `ntu` stems — the possessive suffix `ntu` of `guṇavantu` — are Kaccāyana's §122–§128 (with §92–§94 among the general rules); Buddhappiya gathers all ten rules here, after the `mano`-group, and takes them inflection by inflection, beginning with `si`. His gloss is Kaccāyana's vutti. The `yomhi paṭhame` fetched by the lion's glance comes from [233 (§121): Mayaṃ yomhi paṭhame](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r233), the pronoun rule Kaccāyana's §122 follows; the `vā` is still the `vā` of 94–97. No option marker here: before `si`, `guṇavā` is the only form the rule teaches, though [105 (§94): Simhī vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r105) will admit `himavanto` for one word.

:::

> Sabbasseva ntu paccayassa savibhattissa ā ādeso hoti simhi vibhattimhi. Guṇavā.

The whole of the suffix `ntu` together with its inflection is replaced by `ā` when the inflection `si` follows: `guṇavā`.

> ‘‘Yomhi, paṭhame’’sīhagatiyā ‘‘vā’’ti ca vattate.

`yomhi paṭhame` ("before the `yo` of the first form") — by a lion's glance — and `vā` continue.[^15]

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavā | a virtuous man |

|     | guṇavantu + si (①⨀)    | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + si→ā)  | §124 |
| →   | **guṇavā**             |      |

:::

[^15]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) reads the `vā` too as fetched forward by the lion's glance, from [103 (§123): Ntassa se vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r103), the rule from which the `vā` of Kaccāyana's `ntu` series descends; the sentence allows either, and her reading explains why `sīhagatiyā` is stated at all — a `vā` carried since 94 would need no lion's glance. The Analysis keeps the `vā` of 94–97.

---

<a name="r99"></a>

### 99 (§122): Ntussa nto (`ntu` becomes `nto` before `yo` of the first form) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§122 (99): Ntussa nto" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m122" description="`ntu` becomes `nto` before `yo` of the first form" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The first form plural of the `ntu` stems. In Kaccāyana this is the first `ntu` rule (§122), inheriting `yomhi paṭhame` from the pronoun rule before it, which Buddhappiya has moved to [233 (§121): Mayaṃ yomhi paṭhame](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r233) — hence the lion's glance of [98 (§124): Ā simhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r98). Where Kaccāyana's vutti has no `vā`, Buddhappiya's gloss has one, the `vā` carried from 94: it opens the alternative `guṇavantā` of the next rule, and the option is open. The `sunaṃhisu` and `attaṃ` carried forward come from [87 (§89): Sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r87) and [252 (§90): Pañcādīnamattaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r252), the rule from which Kaccāyana's §92 inherits the word.

:::

> Sabbasseva ntu paccayassa savibhattissa nto ādeso hoti vā yo mhi paṭhame, guṇavanto tiṭṭhanti.

The whole of the suffix `ntu` together with its inflection is optionally replaced by `nto` before the `yo` of the first form: `guṇavanto tiṭṭhanti` ("the virtuous stand").

> ‘‘Sunaṃhisu, atta’’nti ca vattate.

`sunaṃhisu` ("before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`") and `attaṃ` ("becoming `a`") continue.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavanto tiṭṭhanti | the virtuous stand |
| 🚹①⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavantā | the virtuous (by 100) |

|     | guṇavantu + yo (①⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + yo→nto)  | §122 |
| →   | **guṇavanto**            |      |

:::

---

<a name="r100"></a>

### 100 (§92): Ntussanto yosu ca (And `ntu` ending in `yo`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§92 (100): Ntussanto yosuca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m92" description="And `ntu` ending in `yo`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states this rule among the general rules (§92), where `attaṃ` comes to it from §90; Buddhappiya cites it as the alternative to [99 (§122): Ntussa nto](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r99) and as the rule that makes the `ntu` stem an `a` stem in most of its forms, which is why its examples run through the paradigm. Both points of the gloss are in Kaccāyana's vutti too; the argument from the superfluous `anto` rests on the convention of [66 (§104): So](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r66), that a sixth-form `ntussa` already means the last letter. The `kvaci` of `guṇavanti` marks an exception, not an open choice. The `aṃ` carried forward comes from [198 (§125): Aṃ napuṃsake](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r198), Kaccāyana's §125, for the vocative rule that follows.

:::

> Ntu paccayassa anto u kāro a ttamāpajjate sunaṃhi yo iccetesu, ca ggahaṇena aññesu aṃ nā smāsmiṃ su ca. Guṇavantā, chaṭṭhiyā siddhepi antā dese puna anta ggahaṇakaraṇato yo naṃ i kāro ca kvaci. Guṇavanti.

The final `u` of the suffix `ntu` becomes `a` before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi` and `yo`; by the word `ca`, also before the others, `aṃ`, `nā`, `smā`, `smiṃ`. `guṇavantā`. Although the replacement of the final is already established by the sixth form ["of `ntu`"], from the further mention of `anta` ("final") the `yo` inflections also become `i` in some places: `guṇavanti`.

> ‘‘A’’miti vattate.

`aṃ` continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavantā | the virtuous |
| 🚹①⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavanti | the virtuous (`yo` made `i`, in some places) |
| 🚹③⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavantena | by the virtuous man (by the `ca`) |

|     | guṇavantu + yo (①⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇavant + (u→a) + yo    | §92  |
| →   | guṇavanta + (yo→ā)       | §107 |
| →   | guṇavant + (~~a~~ + ā)   | §83  |
| →   | **guṇavantā**            |      |

|     | guṇavantu + yo (①⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇavant + (u→a) + (yo→i) | §92 |
| →   | guṇavant + (~~a~~ + i)   | §83  |
| →   | **guṇavanti**            |      |

:::

---

<a name="r101"></a>

### 101 (§126): Avaṇṇā ca ge (`aṃ`, `a`, `ā` for `ntu` before `ga`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§126 (101): Avaṇṇā ca ge" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m126" description="`aṃ`, `a`, `ā` for `ntu` before `ga`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The vocative of the `ntu` stems. In Kaccāyana the `aṃ` this rule's `ca` draws in comes from the neuter rule just before it (§125); Buddhappiya has placed that rule at [198 (§125): Aṃ napuṃsake](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r198) and supplied its `aṃ` by the `anuvutti` at the end of [100 (§92): Ntussanto yosu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r100), so that the three vocatives come out here as in Kaccāyana. The plurality of vocatives is itself the option; no marker is needed.

:::

> Sabbasseva ntu paccayassa savibhattissa aṃ a vaṇṇaiccete ādesā honti ge pare.

The whole of the suffix `ntu` together with its inflection is replaced by `aṃ` and by the `a`-sounds (`a`, `ā`)[^16] when `ga` follows.

> Bho guṇavaṃ bho guṇava bho guṇavā, bhavanto guṇavanto guṇavantā, guṇavantaṃ, guṇavante.

The address and second forms:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ⓪ | bho guṇavaṃ, bho guṇava, bho guṇavā | bhavanto guṇavanto, guṇavantā |
| ② | guṇavantaṃ | guṇavante |

> ‘‘Vā’’ti vattate.

`vā` continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(guṇavantu) | bho guṇavaṃ, bho guṇava, bho guṇavā | virtuous one! |
| 🚹⓪⨂(guṇavantu) | bhavanto guṇavanto, guṇavantā | virtuous ones! |
| 🚹②⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavantaṃ | the virtuous man |
| 🚹②⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavante | the virtuous men |

|     | guṇavantu + ga (⓪⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + ga→aṃ)   | §126 |
| →   | **bho guṇavaṃ**          |      |

|     | guṇavantu + aṃ (②⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇavant + (u→a) + aṃ    | §92  |
| →   | guṇavant + (~~a~~ + aṃ)  | §83  |
| →   | **guṇavantaṃ**           |      |

:::

[^16]: CST4, with the Burmese edition, reads `aṃ avaṇṇa iccete ādesā honti` (printed `aṃ a vaṇṇaiccete`), "`aṃ` and the `a`-vowel"; the Thai and Sinhalese editions spell the two vowels out, `aṃ a ā ādesā honti` ([Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong), Explanatory Note on rule 101). The sense is the same, `avaṇṇa` being `a` and `ā`, and the CST is kept.

---

<a name="r102"></a>

### 102 (§127): Totitā sasmiṃnāsu (`to`, `ti`, `tā` for `ntu` before `sa`, `smiṃ`, `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§127 (102): To titā sasmiṃnāsu" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m127" description="`to`, `ti`, `tā` for `ntu` before `sa`, `smiṃ`, `nā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The third form of the `ntu` stems. Kaccāyana's §127 gives all six forms it covers at once; Buddhappiya, taking the paradigm in order, gives here only `guṇavatā` beside `guṇavantena` and leaves `guṇavato` and `guṇavati` to their places at [104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r104). The `vā` is the one carried from 101 (in Kaccāyana it comes from §123): an open option between the `ntu` form and the `a`-stem form made by [100 (§92): Ntussanto yosu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r100) and [79 (§103): Ato nena](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r79).

:::

> Sabbasseva ntu paccayassa savibhattissa toti tā ādesā honti vā sasmiṃnā iccetesu yathāsaṅkhyaṃ. Guṇavatā guṇavantena, guṇavantehi guṇavantebhi.

The whole of the suffix `ntu` together with its inflection is optionally replaced by `to`, `ti` and `tā` before `sa`, `smiṃ` and `nā` respectively: `guṇavatā`, `guṇavantena`; `guṇavantehi`, `guṇavantebhi`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavatā | by the virtuous man |
| 🚹③⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavantena | by the virtuous man |
| 🚹③⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavantehi, guṇavantebhi | by the virtuous men |

|     | guṇavantu + nā (③⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + nā→tā)   | §127 |
| →   | **guṇavatā**             |      |

|     | guṇavantu + nā (③⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇavant + (u→a) + nā    | §92  |
| →   | guṇavanta + (nā→ena)     | §103 |
| →   | guṇavant + (~~a~~ + ena) | §83  |
| →   | **guṇavantena**          |      |

:::

---

<a name="r103"></a>

### 103 (§123): Ntassa se vā (Optionally `ntassa` for `ntu` before `sa`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§123 (103): Ntassa se vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m123" description="Optionally `ntassa` for `ntu` before `sa`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The fourth form singular. Kaccāyana's §123 comes second in his `ntu` series and is the source of the `vā` that runs through it; Buddhappiya reaches it fourth, at the inflection `sa`, and pairs it with the `to` of [102 (§127): Totitā sasmiṃnāsu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r102). The `vā` is an open option, `guṇavantassa` and `guṇavato` both good Pāli — the reading of the Kaccāyana page as well. The replacement `ntassa` swallows the augment `s` of [86 (§61): Sāgamo se](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r86) along with the inflection.

:::

> Sabbasseva ntu paccayassa savibhattissa ntassa iccayamādeso hoti vā se vibhattimhi. Guṇavantassa guṇavato.

The whole of the suffix `ntu` together with its inflection is optionally replaced by this `ntassa` when the inflection `sa` follows: `guṇavantassa`, `guṇavato`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavantassa | to the virtuous man |
| 🚹④⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavato | to the virtuous man (by 102) |

|     | guṇavantu + sa (④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | -------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + sa→ntassa) | §123 |
| →   | **guṇavantassa**           |      |

|     | guṇavantu + sa (④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | -------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + sa→to)     | §127 |
| →   | **guṇavato**               |      |

:::

---

<a name="r104"></a>

### 104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā (Optionally `taṃ` for `ntu` before `naṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§128 (104): Naṃmhi taṃ vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m128" description="Optionally `taṃ` for `ntu` before `naṃ`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last of Kaccāyana's `ntu` rules (§128), reached at the fourth form plural; with it Buddhappiya closes the paradigm of `guṇavantu`, anticipating [120 (§270): Amhatumhanturājabrahmattasakhasatthupitādīhi smā nāva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r120) for the fifth form. The `vā` is an open option, `guṇavataṃ` beside `guṇavantānaṃ`. The `attaṃ` and `ntussa anto` put in force for `himavantu` come from [100 (§92): Ntussanto yosu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r100).

:::

> Sabbasseva ntu paccayassa savibhattissa taṃ ādeso hoti vā naṃ mhi vibhattimhi. Guṇavataṃ guṇavantānaṃ.

The whole of the suffix `ntu` together with its inflection is optionally replaced by `taṃ` when the inflection `naṃ` follows: `guṇavataṃ`, `guṇavantānaṃ`.[^17]

> ‘‘Amha tumha ntu’’iccādinā smā vacanassa nābyapadeso.

By [§270 (120): Amha tumha ntu rāja brahmatta sakha satthu pitādīhi smā nāva](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m270) the inflection `smā` is designated `nā`.[^18]

> Guṇavatā guṇavantā guṇavantasmā guṇavantamhā, guṇavantehi guṇavantebhi, guṇavantassa guṇavato, guṇavataṃ guṇavantānaṃ, guṇavati guṇavante guṇavantasmiṃ guṇavantamhi[^19], guṇavantesu.

The whole paradigm of `guṇavantu` ("virtuous"), with the forms of 98–103:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | guṇavā | guṇavanto, guṇavantā |
| ⓪ | bho guṇavaṃ, bho guṇava, bho guṇavā | bhavanto guṇavanto, guṇavantā |
| ② | guṇavantaṃ | guṇavante |
| ③ | guṇavatā, guṇavantena | guṇavantehi, guṇavantebhi |
| ④ | guṇavantassa, guṇavato | guṇavataṃ, guṇavantānaṃ |
| ⑤ | guṇavatā, guṇavantā, guṇavantasmā, guṇavantamhā | guṇavantehi, guṇavantebhi |
| ⑥ | guṇavantassa, guṇavato | guṇavataṃ, guṇavantānaṃ |
| ⑦ | guṇavati, guṇavante, guṇavantasmiṃ, guṇavantamhi | guṇavantesu |

> Evaṃ gaṇavā kulavā balavā yasavā dhanavā sutavā bhagavā himavā phalavā sīlavā paññavā iccādayo.

In the same way `gaṇavā` (having a following), `kulavā` (of good family), `balavā` (strong), `yasavā` (famous), `dhanavā` (wealthy), `sutavā` (learned), `bhagavā` (the Blessed One), `himavā` (the Himalaya), `phalavā` (fruitful), `sīlavā` (virtuous), `paññavā` (wise), and so on.

> Himavantu saddato si mhi kate –

When `si` has been added after the word `himavantu` —

> ‘‘Attaṃ, ntussa’nto’’ti ca vattamāne –

with `attaṃ` ("becoming `a`") and `ntussa anto` ("the final of `ntu`") in force —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavataṃ | to the virtuous men |
| 🚹④⨂(guṇavantu) | guṇavantānaṃ | to the virtuous men |
| 🚹⑤⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavatā | from the virtuous man (the `smā` designated `nā`) |
| 🚹⑦⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavati | in the virtuous man |
| 🚹①⨀(bhagavantu) | bhagavā | the Blessed One |

|     | guṇavantu + naṃ (④⨂)     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇava + (ntu + naṃ→taṃ) | §128 |
| →   | **guṇavataṃ**            |      |

|     | guṇavantu + naṃ (④⨂)     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | guṇavant + (u→a) + naṃ   | §92  |
| →   | guṇavant + (a→ā) + naṃ   | §89  |
| →   | **guṇavantānaṃ**         |      |

:::

[^19]: CST4 prints `guṇavantamhī`; the Thai text read by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) has `guṇavantamhi`, the form printed here.

[^17]: CST4 prints the sutta `Naṃmhi taṃ vā`; the Thai edition read by Rachiwong (1995) has `Naṃhi taṃ vā`, and likewise `Raññaṃ naṃhi vā` at [119 (§136): Raññaṃ naṃ mhī vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r119), where the CST itself wavers between `mhī` and `mhi`. Consistent in both places, it is probably the Thai edition's reading rather than a slip; the CST is kept.

[^18]: `nābyapadeso`, "designation as `nā`", is the wording the Thai edition also uses when the rule is reached at [120 (§270): Amhatumhanturājabrahmattasakhasatthupitādīhi smā nāva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r120), where CST4 prints `nābhāvātideso`; see the note there.

---

<a name="r105"></a>

### 105 (§94): Simhī vā (Optionally in `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§94 (105): Simhi vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m94" description="Optionally in `si`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §94 stands among the general rules with §92 and §93, inheriting `ntupaccayassa anto` and `attaṃ` from them; Buddhappiya takes it out of that group and puts it after the whole `guṇavantu` paradigm, as the rule for a single word, `himavantu`, whose `himavanto` beside `himavā` is Kaccāyana's only example. The repeated `vā` he reads as a `vavatthitavibhāsā` with two domains: `anicca` for `himavantu`, `asanta` for `guṇavantu` and every other `ntu` stem. The list that follows is of `mantu` stems, which go like `guṇavantu`; two of them are singled out for the next rule.

:::

> Ntu paccayassa anto a ttaṃ hoti vā si mhi vibhattimhi. Himavanto, himavā, sesaṃ samaṃ.

The final of the suffix `ntu` optionally becomes `a` when the inflection `si` follows: `himavanto`, `himavā`; the rest is the same.

> Puna vā ggahaṇakaraṇaṃ himavantu saddato aññatra attanisedhanatthaṃ, vavatthitavibhāsatthoyaṃ vā saddo. Tena guṇavantādīsu nātippasaṅgo.

The repeated mention of `vā` is to forbid the becoming-`a` elsewhere than after the word `himavantu`; this word `vā` has the sense of a `vavatthitavibhāsā` (restricted option). Hence there is no over-application to `guṇavantu` and the like.

> Evaṃ satimā dhitimā gatimā matimā mutimā muttimā jutimā sirimā hirimā thutimā ratimā yatimā sucimā kalimā balimā kasimā rucimā buddhimā cakkhumā bandhumā hetumā setumā ketumā rāhumā bhāṇumā khāṇumā vijjumā iccādayo.

In the same way [as `guṇavā`]: `satimā` (mindful), `dhitimā` (steadfast), `gatimā` (of retentive mind), `matimā` (wise), `mutimā` (intelligent), `muttimā` (liberated), `jutimā` (radiant), `sirimā` (glorious), `hirimā` (having a sense of shame), `thutimā` (praised), `ratimā` (delighting), `yatimā` (restrained), `sucimā` (pure), `kalimā` (unlucky), `balimā` (mighty), `kasimā` (a ploughman), `rucimā` (bright), `buddhimā` (intelligent), `cakkhumā` (having eyes), `bandhumā` (having kinsmen), `hetumā` (having a cause), `setumā` (having a bridge), `ketumā` (having a banner), `rāhumā` (having Rāhu), `bhāṇumā` (radiant), `khāṇumā` (having stumps), `vijjumā` (having lightning), and so on.

> Tattha satimantu bandhumantu saddānaṃ aṃse su rūpabhedo. ‘‘Attaṃ, ntussā’’ti ca vattate.

Among these, the words `satimantu` and `bandhumantu` differ in form before `aṃ` and `sa`. `attaṃ` and `ntussa` continue.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(himavantu) | himavanto | the Himalaya (`anicca`: the option taken) |
| 🚹①⨀(himavantu) | himavā | the Himalaya (`anicca`: the option not taken) |
| 🚹①⨀(guṇavantu) | guṇavā | a virtuous man (`asanta`: no `guṇavanto` in the singular) |
| 🚹①⨀(satimantu) | satimā | a mindful man |
| 🚹①⨀(cakkhumantu) | cakkhumā | one who has eyes |

|     | himavantu + si (①⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | himavant + (u→a) + si    | §94  |
| →   | himavanta + (si→o)       | §104 |
| →   | himavant + (~~a~~ + o)   | §83  |
| →   | **himavanto**            |      |

:::

---

<a name="r106"></a>

### 106 (§93): Sabbassa vā aṃsesu (Optionally for all in `aṃ`, `sa`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§93 (106): Sabbassa vā aṃsesu" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m93" description="Optionally for all in `aṃ`, `sa`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §93 precedes §94 among the general rules; Buddhappiya reverses the two and makes this the rule of `satimantu` and `bandhumantu`, the two words whose forms Kaccāyana's vutti gives. The `vā` is again a `vavatthitavibhāsā` — `idhāpi`, "here too", as at [105 (§94): Simhī vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r105) — with the same two domains: `anicca` for these two words, `asanta` for `guṇavantu` and the rest, so that there is no `guṇavaṃ` as a second form singular. Within its domain the option is open, and of the three fourth-form singulars `satimato` is by [102 (§127): Totitā sasmiṃnāsu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r102) and `satimantassa` by [103 (§123): Ntassa se vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r103).

:::

> Sabbasseva ntu paccayassa a ttaṃ hoti vā aṃsa iccetesu. Idhāpi vā saddassa vavatthitavibhāsattā nātippasaṅgo. Satimaṃ satimantaṃ, bandhumaṃ bandhumantaṃ, satimassa satimato satimantassa, bandhumassa bandhumato bandhumantassa, sesaṃ samaṃ.

The whole of the suffix `ntu` optionally becomes `a` before `aṃ` and `sa`. Here too, because the word `vā` is a `vavatthitavibhāsā`, there is no over-application. `satimaṃ`, `satimantaṃ`; `bandhumaṃ`, `bandhumantaṃ`; `satimassa`, `satimato`, `satimantassa`; `bandhumassa`, `bandhumato`, `bandhumantassa`; the rest is the same.

> Gacchanta saddassa bhedo, gacchanta si,

The word `gacchanta` ("going") differs: `gacchanta` + `si`,

> ‘‘Vā’’ti vattate.

`vā` continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨀(satimantu) | satimaṃ, satimantaṃ | the mindful man |
| 🚹②⨀(bandhumantu) | bandhumaṃ, bandhumantaṃ | the man with kinsmen |
| 🚹④⨀(satimantu) | satimassa, satimato, satimantassa | to the mindful man |
| 🚹④⨀(bandhumantu) | bandhumassa, bandhumato, bandhumantassa | to the man with kinsmen |

|     | satimantu + aṃ (②⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | satima + (ntu→a) + aṃ    | §93  |
| →   | satim + (~~a~~ + aṃ)     | §83  |
| →   | **satimaṃ**              |      |

|     | satimantu + sa (④⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | satima + (ntu→a) + sa    | §93  |
| →   | satima + (s) + sa        | §61  |
| →   | **satimassa**            |      |

:::

---

<a name="r107"></a>

### 107 (§186): Simhi gacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ (`nta` of `gacchanta` and the like becomes `aṃ` before `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§186 (107): Simhigacchantādīnaṃ ntasaddo aṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m186" description="`nta` of `gacchanta` and the like becomes `aṃ` before `si`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The present participles in `anta` — `gacchanta` and its kind, formed by [§565 (646): Vattamāne mānantā](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m565) — are Kaccāyana's §186–§187, in the third section after the `santa` rule; Buddhappiya places them straight after the `ntu` stems, because [108 (§187): Sesesu ntuva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r108) will make them go like `guṇavantu`. His gloss adds `antappaccayantānaṃ` to Kaccāyana's vutti. The `vā` is the one carried from 106 (in Kaccāyana it comes from §184): an open option, `gacchaṃ` beside `gacchanto`.

:::

> Gacchanti ccevamādīnaṃ anta ppaccayantānaṃ nta saddo aṃrūpaṃ āpajjate vā si mhi vibhattimhi. Saralopasi lopā, so gacchaṃ, gacchanto vā gaṇhāti.

Of `gacchanta` and the like, which end in the suffix `anta`, the sound `nta` optionally takes the form `aṃ` when the inflection `si` follows. The vowel elision and the elision of `si`: `so gacchaṃ`, or `gacchanto gaṇhāti` ("he, going, takes").

> ‘‘Gacchantādīnaṃ, ntasaddo’’ti ca vattamāne –

With `gacchantādīnaṃ` and `ntasaddo` in force —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(gacchanta) | so gacchaṃ gaṇhāti | he, going, takes |
| 🚹①⨀(gacchanta) | so gacchanto gaṇhāti | he, going, takes |

|     | gacchanta + si (①⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | gaccha + (nta→aṃ) + si   | §186 |
| →   | gacch + (~~a~~ + aṃ) + si | §83 |
| →   | gacchaṃ + ~~si~~         | §220 |
| →   | **gacchaṃ**              |      |

|     | gacchanta + si (①⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | gacchanta + (si→o)       | §104 |
| →   | gacchant + (~~a~~ + o)   | §83  |
| →   | **gacchanto**            |      |

:::

---

<a name="r108"></a>

### 108 (§187): Sesesu ntuva (in the remaining [inflections], like `ntu`) :badge[vidhyaṅga]{variant=tip}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§187 (108): Sesesu ntuva" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m187" description="in the remaining [inflections], like `ntu`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The instruction that finishes the participle. The `kāriyātidesa` Buddhappiya names is the sixth of the six kinds of `atidesa` he lists at [120 (§270): Amhatumhanturājabrahmattasakhasatthupitādīhi smā nāva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r120), and the whole paradigm of `gacchanta` follows by copying that of `guṇavantu`; the editions bracket a second form singular `gacchaṃ`, the `aṃ` of [106 (§93): Sabbassa vā aṃsesu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r106) transferred. The `bhavato` carried into the next rule comes from [192 (§242): Bhavato bhoto](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r192). No option marker.

:::

> Gacchantā dīnaṃ nta saddo ntu paccayova daṭṭhabbo sesesu vibhattippaccayesu, asmi mhi kāriyātidesoyaṃ. Sesaṃ guṇavantu samaṃ.

The sound `nta` of `gacchanta` and the like is to be seen as just the suffix `ntu` in the remaining inflections and suffixes: this is a transfer of operations (`kāriyātidesa`) for [inflections] other than `si`. The rest is the same as `guṇavantu`.

> Te gacchanto gacchantā, bho gacchaṃ bho gaccha bho gacchā, bhavanto gacchanto gacchantā, [gacchaṃ] gacchantaṃ, gacchante, gacchatā gacchantena, gacchantehi gacchantebhi, gacchato gacchantassa, gacchataṃ gacchantānaṃ, gacchatā gacchantasmā gacchantamhā, gacchantehi gacchantebhi, gacchato gacchantassa, gacchataṃ gacchantānaṃ, gacchati gacchante gacchantasmiṃ gacchantamhi[^20], gacchantesu.

The paradigm of `gacchanta` ("going"), with the singular of 107:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | gacchaṃ, gacchanto | gacchanto, gacchantā |
| ⓪ | bho gacchaṃ, bho gaccha, bho gacchā | bhavanto gacchanto, gacchantā |
| ② | gacchaṃ, gacchantaṃ | gacchante |
| ③ | gacchatā, gacchantena | gacchantehi, gacchantebhi |
| ④ | gacchato, gacchantassa | gacchataṃ, gacchantānaṃ |
| ⑤ | gacchatā, gacchantasmā, gacchantamhā | gacchantehi, gacchantebhi |
| ⑥ | gacchato, gacchantassa | gacchataṃ, gacchantānaṃ |
| ⑦ | gacchati, gacchante, gacchantasmiṃ, gacchantamhi | gacchantesu |

> Evaṃ mahaṃ caraṃ tiṭṭhaṃ, dadaṃ bhuñjaṃ suṇaṃ pacaṃ; \
> Jayaṃ jīraṃ cavaṃ mīyaṃ, saraṃ kubbaṃ japaṃ vajaṃ –

In the same way `mahaṃ` (great), `caraṃ` (wandering), `tiṭṭhaṃ` (standing), `dadaṃ` (giving), `bhuñjaṃ` (eating), `suṇaṃ` (hearing), `pacaṃ` (cooking); \
`jayaṃ` (conquering), `jīraṃ` (growing old), `cavaṃ` (passing away)[^21], `mīyaṃ` (dying), `saraṃ` (remembering), `kubbaṃ` (doing), `japaṃ` (reciting), `vajaṃ` (going) —

> Iccādayo.

and others like these.

> Bhavanta saddassa ga yo nā sa vacanesu viseso. So bhavaṃ.

The word `bhavanta` ("the venerable one") differs in the inflections `ga`, `yo`, `nā` and `sa`. `so bhavaṃ` ("he, the venerable one").

> ‘‘Bhavato’’ti vattate.

`bhavato` ("of `bhavanta`") continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(gacchanta) | gacchatā | by the one going |
| 🚹④⨀(gacchanta) | gacchato | to the one going |
| 🚹⑦⨀(gacchanta) | gacchati | in the one going |
| 🚹①⨀(mahanta) | mahaṃ | great |
| 🚹①⨀(bhavanta) | so bhavaṃ | he, the venerable one |

|     | gacchanta + nā (③⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | gaccha + (nta→ntu) + nā  | §187 |
| →   | gaccha + (ntu + nā→tā)   | §127 |
| →   | **gacchatā**             |      |

|     | bhavanta + si (①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | bhava + (nta→aṃ) + si    | §186 |
| →   | bhav + (~~a~~ + aṃ) + ~~si~~ | §83, §220 |
| →   | **bhavaṃ**               |      |

:::

[^20]: CST4 prints `gacchantamhī`; the Thai text read by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) has `gacchantamhi`, the form printed here.

[^21]: CST4 prints `cavaṃ`, "passing away"; Rachiwong (1995) prints `vacaṃ`, "speaking" — the Thai edition's reading, unless a slip of the thesis. Both are well-formed participles in `aṃ`; the CST is kept.

---

<a name="r109"></a>

### 109 (§0): Obhāvo kvaci yosu vakārassa (`o` for the `va` [of `bhavanta`] before `yo`, in some places) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| kvaci  |        | vakārassa (bhavantassa) | (o)bhāvo | o | yosu | |
| in some places | | the letter `va` (of `bhavanta`) | becoming | `o` | before the `yo` inflections | |

This sutta has no counterpart in the CST's Kaccāyana; Senart's edition, followed by Thiab Malai (1997), prints it, with the same vutti, as Kaccāyana's own rule after [§243 (110): Bho ge tu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m243) (Senart's 244), so whether it is Buddhappiya's or a Kaccāyana rule lost in the Burmese transmission is open. [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong), working from the Thai Padarūpasiddhi, likewise cites it as Kaccāyana 244, Senart's number, and counts it, with [111 (§0): Bhadantassa bhaddanta bhante](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r111), as one of the two suttas the Rūpasiddhi "accepts from the Nyāsa" (p. 20) — a third account of its origin. Where Kaccāyana's vutti of §243 lists the irregular forms of `bhavanta` as so many replacements drawn from the word `tu`, Buddhappiya derives most of them from this one change, after which the `ntu` rules give `bhonto` as they give `guṇavanto`. The `kvaci` places the `o` among the exceptions: it does not reach `bhavantaṃ`, `bhavantehi` or `bhavantesu`. Ruiz-Falqués (§4.1) counts this rule and 111 among the seven in which `kvaci` clearly marks an exception but its exact reach is left to the examples.

:::

> Bhavanta iccetassa va kārassa o bhāvo hoti kvaci yo iccetesu. Te bhonto bhavanto bhavantā.

The letter `va` of `bhavanta` becomes `o` in some places before the `yo` inflections: `te bhonto` ("those venerable ones"), `bhavanto`, `bhavantā`.

> ‘‘Bhavato’’ti vattate.

`bhavato` ("of `bhavanta`") continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(bhavanta) | te bhonto | those venerable ones |
| 🚹①⨂(bhavanta) | bhavanto, bhavantā | the venerable ones |

|     | bhavanta + yo (①⨂)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | bha + (va→o) + nta + yo  | 109  |
| →   | bho + (nta→ntu) + yo     | §187 |
| →   | bho + (ntu + yo→nto)     | §122 |
| →   | **bhonto**               |      |

:::

---

<a name="r110"></a>

### 110 (§243): Bho ge tu (`bho` [for `bhavanta`] before `ga`; and further forms) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§243 (110): Bho ge tu" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m243" description="`bho` [for `bhavanta`] before `ga`; and further forms" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's rule for the vocative `bho` stands in his last section (§243), and its `tu` carries the six irregular forms; Buddhappiya cites it in the paradigm of `bhavanta`, after his own [109 (§0): Obhāvo kvaci yosu vakārassa](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r109), and shares the work between the two rules, splitting 109 (`yogavibhāga`) for `bhotā` and `bhoto`. The word `ge` this sutta contains is the one he borrowed by `anuvutti` as far back as [71 (§57): Ālapane si gasañño](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r71). The `tu` is the only marker: it is not an option but Kaccāyana's way of adding forms the sutta does not state (the Kaccāyana page's "and further forms").

:::

> Sabbasseva bhavanta saddassa bho hoti ge pare. Tu saddena bhante, bhontādi ca, ga lopo, bho bhante bhonta bhontā, bhonto bhavanto bhavantā, bhavantaṃ, bhonte bhavante.

The whole of the word `bhavanta` becomes `bho` when `ga` follows. By the word `tu`, also `bhante`, `bhonta` and the like; `ga` is elided: `bho`, `bhante`, `bhonta`, `bhontā`; `bhonto`, `bhavanto`, `bhavantā`; `bhavantaṃ`; `bhonte`, `bhavante`.

> Nāse su ‘‘obhāvo kvacī’’ti yogavibhāgena o bhāvo.

Before `nā` and `sa`, by splitting the rule [109] as `obhāvo kvaci`, [the `va`] becomes `o`.

> Bhotā bhavatā bhavantena, bhoto bhavato bhavantassa iccādi.

`bhotā`, `bhavatā`, `bhavantena`; `bhoto`, `bhavato`, `bhavantassa`; and so on.

The paradigm of `bhavanta` ("the venerable one", "you, sir") as far as Buddhappiya gives it; the fifth and seventh forms, which his `iccādi` leaves to the reader, follow `guṇavantu`:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | bhavaṃ | bhonto, bhavanto, bhavantā |
| ⓪ | bho, bhante, bhonta, bhontā | bhonto, bhavanto, bhavantā |
| ② | bhavantaṃ | bhonte, bhavante |
| ③ | bhotā, bhavatā, bhavantena | bhavantehi, bhavantebhi |
| ④ | bhoto, bhavato, bhavantassa | bhavataṃ, bhavantānaṃ |
| ⑤ | bhotā, bhavatā, bhavantā, bhavantasmā, bhavantamhā | bhavantehi, bhavantebhi |
| ⑥ | bhoto, bhavato, bhavantassa | bhavataṃ, bhavantānaṃ |
| ⑦ | bhavati, bhavante, bhavantasmiṃ, bhavantamhi | bhavantesu |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(bhavanta) | bho, bhante, bhonta, bhontā | sir! venerable sir! |
| 🚹⓪⨂(bhavanta) | bhonto, bhavanto, bhavantā | sirs! |
| 🚹②⨀(bhavanta) | bhavantaṃ | the venerable one |
| 🚹②⨂(bhavanta) | bhonte, bhavante | the venerable ones |
| 🚹③⨀(bhavanta) | bhotā, bhavatā, bhavantena | by the venerable one |
| 🚹④⨀(bhavanta) | bhoto, bhavato, bhavantassa | to the venerable one |

|     | bhavanta + ga (⓪⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (bhavanta→bho) + ga      | §243 |
| →   | bho + ~~ga~~             | §220 |
| →   | **bho**                  |      |

|     | bhavanta + nā (③⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | bha + (va→o) + nta + nā  | 109 (split as `obhāvo kvaci`) |
| →   | bho + (nta→ntu) + nā     | §187 |
| →   | bho + (ntu + nā→tā)      | §127 |
| →   | **bhotā**                |      |

:::

---

<a name="r111"></a>

### 111 (§0): Bhadantassa bhaddanta bhante (`bhaddanta`, `bhante` for `bhadanta` [before `ga` and `yo`, in some places]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::note[Analysis]

| option | before | from   | operation | to         | after  | context |
| ------ | ------ | ------ | --------- | ---------- | ------ | ------- |
| (kvaci) |       | bhadantassa (sabbassa) | (ādesā) | bhaddanta \| bhante | (ge, yosu) | |
| (in some places) | | the whole of `bhadanta` | (replaced by) | `bhaddanta` \| `bhante` | (before `ga` and the `yo` inflections) | |

A sutta without a counterpart in the CST's Kaccāyana — Senart's edition and Malai (1997) carry it, with this vutti, as Kaccāyana's rule 245 — placed after [110 (§243): Bho ge tu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r110) because its forms of address overlap with those of `bhavanta`: `bhante`, which Kaccāyana's `tu` draws from `bhavanta`, is here derived also from `bhadanta` ("venerable", Sanskrit `bhadanta`). [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong), translating the Thai text, heads it as her rule (243-111) and counts it, with 109, as one of the two suttas the Rūpasiddhi takes from the Nyāsa (p. 20). The words `sabbassa`, `kvaci`, `ge` and `yosu` are supplied from the two rules before it. The `kvaci` again marks an exception with a narrow reach (Ruiz-Falqués §4.1): even before `ga` and `yo`, `bho bhadanta` and `bhadantā` remain good.

:::

> Sabbasseva bhadanta saddassa bhaddanta bhante iccete ādesā honti kvaci ge pare yo su ca. Bho bhaddanta bhante, bhadanta bhadantā vā iccādi purisa saddasamaṃ.

The whole of the word `bhadanta` ("venerable")[^22] is in some places replaced by `bhaddanta` and `bhante` when `ga` follows, and before the `yo` inflections: `bho bhaddanta`, `bhante`; or `bhadanta`, `bhadantā`; and so on, the same as the word `purisa`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(bhadanta) | bho bhaddanta, bhante | venerable sir! |
| 🚹⓪⨀(bhadanta) | bho bhadanta | venerable sir! (the option not taken) |
| 🚹①⨂(bhadanta) | bhadantā | the venerable ones |

|     | bhadanta + ga (⓪⨀)          | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (bhadanta→bhaddanta) + ga   | 111  |
| →   | bhaddanta + ~~ga~~          | §220 |
| →   | **bho bhaddanta**           |      |

:::

[^22]: Rachiwong (1995) renders "the entire word 'bhavanta' is sometimes changed into bhaddanta and bhante"; sutta and gloss say `bhadanta`. Her Kaccāyana numbers for the two extra suttas are also inconsistent — 175 in the heading of her rule 109 but 244 when she cites it under 110, and 243, the number of `Bho ge tu`, for this one — so they do not locate the rules in any Kaccāyana.

---

<a name="r112"></a>

### 112 (§185): Santasaddassa so bhe bo cante (`sa` for `santa` before `bh`, with `b` inserted) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§185 (112): Santasaddassa so bhe bo cante" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m185" description="`sa` for `santa` before `bh`, with `b` inserted" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

`santa` ("good", "existing", the participle of `atthi`) is the last of the participles; Kaccāyana's §185 precedes the `gacchanta` rules, Buddhappiya's 112 follows them, so that `sesaṃ gacchantasaddasamaṃ` can close it. The Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (PDṬ p. 137, cited by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong)) derives `santa` instead from `sama` + `ta` with `m` → `n`, that is, as "calmed", the participle of `sammati`; both derivations are traditional, and the ṭīkā's is recorded here as its view. The use his gloss gives the `ca` differs from Kaccāyana's (`sakkāro`, `sakkato`); `sappuriso` for `santapuriso` is the example (Rachiwong 1995, Explanatory Note on rule 112). There is no option marker in the rule: before `bhi`, `sabbhi` is the only form. The governing word the last block puts in force for `rāja` comes from [115 (§188): Brahmattasakharājādito amānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r115), two rules later.

:::

> Sabbasseva santa saddassa sa saddādeso hoti bha kāre pare, ante ca ba kārāgamo hoti.

The whole of the word `santa` is replaced by the word `sa` when the letter `bh` follows, and at its end the augment `b` is inserted.[^23]

> Ca saddaggahaṇena abha kārepi samāse kvaci sa kārādeso. Sabbhi.

By the mention of the word `ca`, in a compound the replacement `sa` occurs in some places even when `bh` does not follow. `sabbhi` ("with the good").

> Bhe ti kiṃ? Santehi, sesaṃ gacchanta saddasamaṃ.

Why `bhe`? `santehi`. The rest is the same as the word `gacchanta`.

> Atthi rāja brahma atta sakha saddādīnaṃ bhedo, tatheva syā dyuppatti, ‘‘rāja si’’iti ṭhite –

There is a difference for the words `rāja` ("king"), `brahma` ("Brahmā"), `atta` ("self"), `sakha` ("friend") and the like. The inflections `si` and the rest are added in the same way; when `rāja` + `si` stands —

> ‘‘Brahmattasakharājādito’’ti adhikāro.

`brahmattasakharājādito` ("after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like") is the governing word.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(santa) | sabbhi | with the good |

|     | santa + hi (③⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | santa + (hi→bhi)         | §99  |
| →   | (santa→sa) + bhi         | §185 |
| →   | sa + (b) + bhi           | §185 |
| →   | **sabbhi**               |      |

:::

:::danger[Counter-example]

By saying `bhe` (before `bh`) the rule does not apply before the plain `hi`, and `santa` is inflected like `gacchanta`.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(santa) | santehi | by the good |

:::

[^23]: Rachiwong (1995) prints the sutta as `so bhe bho cante` and renders "'bha' is also augmented at the end of it"; the CST's `bo`, `bakārāgamo`, is what the form requires — `sa` + `b` + `bhi` gives `sabbhi`, an augment `bh` would give `sabhbhi`.

---

<a name="r113"></a>

### 113 (§189): Syā ca (and `ā` for `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§189 (113): Syā ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m189" description="and `ā` for `si`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The group `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` — old stems in `-an` that Pāli inflects as `a` stems with irregularities — is Kaccāyana's §188–§198 in the third section, with `rāja` treated separately in §135–§138 and §169, and `atta` in §211–§214. Buddhappiya gathers all of them here (113–135) and takes them stem by stem, `rāja` first (113–121), then `brahma` (122–125), `atta` (125–129) and `sakha` (130–135), each in the order of the inflections. So the first rule is the one for `si`, though in Kaccāyana its `ca` extends §188, whose governing word [112 (§185): Santasaddassa so bhe bo cante](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r112) has already supplied. No option marker: `rājā` is the only first form singular; the `o` of [66 (§104): So](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r66) does not reach these stems.

:::

> Brahma atta sakha rāja iccevamādito si vacanassa ā kāro hoti. Ādi saddena ātumā disaddato ca. Saralopādi. Rājā tiṭṭhati.

After `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like the inflection `si` becomes the letter `ā`. By the word `ādi`, also after the word `ātuma` and the like. The vowel elision and the rest: `rājā tiṭṭhati` ("the king stands").

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(rāja) | rājā tiṭṭhati | the king stands |
| 🚹①⨀(ātuma) | ātumā | the self |

|     | rāja + si (①⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | rāja + (si→ā)            | §189 |
| →   | rāj + (~~a~~ + ā)        | §83  |
| →   | **rājā**                 |      |

:::

---

<a name="r114"></a>

### 114 (§190): Yonamāno (`āno` for `yo`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§190 (114): Yonamāno" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m190" description="`āno` for `yo`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The plural of the first and second forms, taken from Kaccāyana's §190 in its order. The vocatives the gloss adds need no rule of their own: `bho rāja`, `bho rājā` by [72 (§244): Akārapitādyantānamā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r72)–[74 (§220): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r74), `bhavanto rājāno` by this rule. No marker in the rule: `rājāno` displaces the `ā` and `e` of [69 (§107): Sabbayonīnamāe](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r69). The `vā` carried into the next rule is the last one stated, at [106 (§93): Sabbassa vā aṃsesu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r106); Kaccāyana's vutti of §188 has it too.

:::

> Brahmattasakharājā dito yo naṃ āno ādeso hoti. Rājāno tiṭṭhanti, bho rāja bho rājā, bhavanto rājāno.

After `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like the `yo` inflections are replaced by `āno`: `rājāno tiṭṭhanti` ("the kings stand"); `bho rāja`, `bho rājā`; `bhavanto rājāno`.

> ‘‘Vā’’ti vattate.

`vā` continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(rāja) | rājāno tiṭṭhanti | the kings stand |
| 🚹⓪⨀(rāja) | bho rāja, bho rājā | king! |
| 🚹⓪⨂(rāja) | bhavanto rājāno | kings! |

|     | rāja + yo (①⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | rāja + (yo→āno)          | §190 |
| →   | rāj + (~~a~~ + āno)      | §83  |
| →   | **rājāno**               |      |

:::

---

<a name="r115"></a>

### 115 (§188): Brahmattasakharājādito amānaṃ (`ānaṃ` for `aṃ` after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§188 (115): Brahmatta sakha rājādito amānaṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m188" description="`ānaṃ` for `aṃ` after `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `rāja` and the like" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The head of Kaccāyana's group (§188), whose long first word governs §189–§198, is cited by Buddhappiya third, at the second form singular, after having lent its governing word to [113 (§189): Syā ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r113) and [114 (§190): Yonamāno](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r114). The `vā` in his gloss is the one 114 carried over (Kaccāyana's comes from §186); it is an open option, `rājānaṃ` beside `rājaṃ`. The words carried forward come from rules still ahead: `savibhattissa` from [243 (§120): Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r243), `rājassa` from [117 (§169): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r117) and [118 (§135): Rājassa raññorājino se](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r118).

:::

> Brahmā dīhi parassa aṃvacanassa ānaṃ hoti vā. Rājānaṃ passa rājaṃ vā, rājāno.

After `brahma` and the rest the inflection `aṃ` optionally becomes `ānaṃ`: `rājānaṃ passa` ("see the king"), or `rājaṃ`; `rājāno`.

> ‘‘Savibhattissa, rājassā’’ti ca vattate.

`savibhattissa` ("together with its inflection") and `rājassa` ("of `rāja`") continue.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹②⨀(rāja) | rājānaṃ passa | see the king |
| 🚹②⨀(rāja) | rājaṃ passa | see the king |
| 🚹②⨂(rāja) | rājāno | the kings |

|     | rāja + aṃ (②⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | rāja + (aṃ→ānaṃ)         | §188 |
| →   | rāj + (~~a~~ + ānaṃ)     | §83  |
| →   | **rājānaṃ**              |      |

:::

---

<a name="r116"></a>

### 116 (§137): Nāmhi raññā vā (Optionally `raññā` for `rāja` before `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§137 (116): Nāmhi raññā vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m137" description="Optionally `raññā` for `rāja` before `nā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's four `rāja` rules (§135–§138) go `sa`, `naṃ`, `nā`, `smiṃ`; Buddhappiya reorders them by the paradigm, `nā` first, and interleaves §169 for the plural. The `vā` is an open option, `raññā` or `rājena`; Ruiz-Falqués (§3.1) cites this very rule, Rūp 116, as his example of `vā` meaning `vikappa`: "we can form the sentences `raññā kataṃ` or `rājena kataṃ`".

:::

> Sabbasseva rāja saddassa savibhattissa raññā ādeso hoti vā nā mhi vibhattimhi. Raññā kataṃ rājena vā.

The whole of the word `rāja` together with its inflection is optionally replaced by `raññā` when the inflection `nā` follows: `raññā kataṃ` ("done by the king"), or `rājena`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(rāja) | raññā kataṃ | done by the king |
| 🚹③⨀(rāja) | rājena kataṃ | done by the king |

|     | rāja + nā (③⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (rāja + nā→raññā)        | §137 |
| →   | **raññā**                |      |

:::

---

<a name="r117"></a>

### 117 (§169): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca (`rāju` for `rāja` before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§169 (117): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m169" description="`rāju` for `rāja` before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §169 stands far from his other `rāja` rules, among the pronoun and numeral rules of the third section; Buddhappiya brings it next to them, at the third form plural. His gloss reads the `ca` outright as `vikappanattho` — the place Ruiz-Falqués (§2.2) cites for `ca` glossed as `vikappa` — where Kaccāyana's vutti calls the same `ca` `avadhāraṇa`, a restriction; the Kaccāyana page reads it as Buddhappiya does. `savibhattissa` is stated again as governing because this rule replaces the stem alone (`sabbassa rājasaddassa`) and the rules after it return to replacing stem and inflection together.

:::

> Sabbassa rāja saddassa rāju ādeso hoti su naṃ hi iccetesu vacanesu. Ca saddo vikappanattho, ‘‘sunaṃhisu cā’’ti dīgho, rājūhi rājūbhi, rājehi rājebhi vā.

The whole of the word `rāja` is replaced by `rāju` before the inflections `su`, `naṃ` and `hi`. The word `ca` has the sense of an option; the lengthening is by [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m89): `rājūhi`, `rājūbhi`; or `rājehi`, `rājebhi`.

> ‘‘Savibhattissā’’ti adhikāro.

`savibhattissa` ("together with its inflection") is the governing word.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājūhi, rājūbhi | by the kings |
| 🚹③⨂(rāja) | rājehi, rājebhi | by the kings (the `ca`: the option not taken) |

|     | rāja + hi (③⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (rāja→rāju) + hi         | §169 |
| →   | rāj + (u→ū) + hi         | §89  |
| →   | rājū + (hi→bhi)          | §99 (optional) |
| →   | **rājūhi**, **rājūbhi**  |      |

:::

---

<a name="r118"></a>

### 118 (§135): Rājassa raññorājino se (`rañño`, `rājino` for `rāja` before `sa`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§135 (118): Rājassa rañño rājino se" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m135" description="`rañño`, `rājino` for `rāja` before `sa`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The first of Kaccāyana's `rāja` rules (§135), cited fourth by Buddhappiya, at the fourth form singular; the gloss is Kaccāyana's vutti with an example sentence. No option marker, and two results: `rañño` and `rājino` are both taught, and no `rājassa` is — the Kaccāyana page notes that the regular form is found only at the end of compounds.

:::

> Sabbasseva rāja saddassa savibhattissa rañño rājino iccete ādesā honti se vibhattimhi. Rañño, rājino deti.

The whole of the word `rāja` together with its inflection is replaced by `rañño` and `rājino` when the inflection `sa` follows: `rañño`, `rājino deti` ("he gives to the king").

> ‘‘Rājassā’’ti vattate.

`rājassa` ("of `rāja`") continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(rāja) | rañño deti | he gives to the king |
| 🚹④⨀(rāja) | rājino deti | he gives to the king |

|     | rāja + sa (④⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (rāja + sa→rañño)        | §135 |
| →   | **rañño**                |      |

:::

---

<a name="r119"></a>

### 119 (§136): Raññaṃ naṃ mhī vā (Optionally `raññaṃ` for `rāja` before `naṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§136 (119): Raññaṃ naṃ mhi vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m136" description="Optionally `raññaṃ` for `rāja` before `naṃ`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The fourth form plural. The `vā` is an open option among the three forms Buddhappiya lists, `rājānaṃ` being the lengthening alone when the `ca` of [117 (§169): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r117) withholds `rāju`. The CST's Rūpasiddhi prints the sutta with `mhī`, Kaccāyana's vutti section with `mhi`; the Thai edition read by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) has `Raññaṃ naṃhi vā`, as it has `Naṃhi taṃ vā` at [104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r104).

:::

> Sabbasseva rāja saddassa savibhattissa raññaṃ ādeso hoti vā naṃ mhi vibhattimhi. Raññaṃ rājūnaṃ rājānaṃ.

The whole of the word `rāja` together with its inflection is optionally replaced by `raññaṃ` when the inflection `naṃ` follows: `raññaṃ`, `rājūnaṃ`, `rājānaṃ`.

> Pañcamiyaṃ –

In the fifth form —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨂(rāja) | raññaṃ | to the kings |
| 🚹④⨂(rāja) | rājūnaṃ | to the kings (by 117) |
| 🚹④⨂(rāja) | rājānaṃ | to the kings (the `ca` of 117 withholding `rāju`) |

|     | rāja + naṃ (④⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (rāja + naṃ→raññaṃ)      | §136 |
| →   | **raññaṃ**               |      |

|     | rāja + naṃ (④⨂)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (rāja→rāju) + naṃ        | §169 |
| →   | rāj + (u→ū) + naṃ        | §89  |
| →   | **rājūnaṃ**              |      |

:::

---

<a name="r120"></a>

### 120 (§270): Amhatumhanturājabrahmattasakhasatthupitādīhi smā nāva (After `amha`, `tumha`, `ntu`, `rāja`, `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `satthu`, `pitu` etc., `smā` has the form of `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§270 (120): Amha tumha ntu rāja brahmatta sakha satthu pitādīhi smā nāva" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m270" description="After `amha`, `tumha`, `ntu`, `rāja`, `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `satthu`, `pitu` etc., `smā` has the form of `nā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's last sutta of the Nāmakappa, a summary rule for the fifth form singular of nine kinds of stem, is cited by Buddhappiya at the first stem in his order whose `smā` needs it, `rāja`; he has already invoked it by anticipation for `guṇavatā` at [104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r104), and will again for `brahmunā`, `attanā`, `sakhinā`, `satthārā`, `pitarā`, `mayā`, `tayā`. The verse on the six kinds of `atidesa` is his addition to Kaccāyana's vutti; the kind named at [108 (§187): Sesesu ntuva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r108) is the sixth. There is no option marker: `raññā` is the fifth form singular, and no `rājasmā` or `rājamhā` is taught (the Bālāvatāra admits them; Buddhappiya does not).

:::

:::note[The six kinds of atidesa]

The Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā (PDṬ pp. 33–34, cited by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong), Explanatory Note on rule 120) glosses the term — `atidisatīti atideso`, a transfer is so called because it transfers [what is laid down for one thing to another] — and gives an instance of each of the six kinds the verse names.

| kind | Pāli | instance (PDṬ pp. 33–34) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| designation | `byapadesa` | [334 (§601): Taddhitasamāsakitakā nāmaṃvā’tavetunādīsu ca](/rupasiddhi/4-samasakanda#r334): derivatives, compounds and `kita` words count as nouns by designation |
| conditioning context | `nimitta` | [13 (§12): Sarā sare lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r13) and [511 (§444): Tathā kattari ca](/rupasiddhi/6-akhyatakanda#r511) |
| form | `taṃrūpa` | this rule: `brahma` + `smā` → `brahmunā`, the `smā` taking the form of the `nā` |
| nature | `taṃsabhāva` | [353 (§331): Itthiyaṃ bhāsitapumitthī pumāva ce](/rupasiddhi/4-samasakanda#r353) |
| the rule itself | `sutta` | [59 (§51): Anupadiṭṭhānaṃ vuttayogato](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r59) |
| operation | `kāriya` | [108 (§187): Sesesu ntuva](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r108): `yaṃ ntussa kāriyaṃ vihitaṃ, tameva ntasaddassāti atidisati`, "the operation laid down for `ntu`, that very one it transfers to the word `nta`" (PDṬ p. 33) |

:::

> Amhatumhanturājabrahmattasakhasatthupituiccevamādīhi smā vacanaṃ nā va daṭṭhabbanti smā vacanassa nābhāvātideso. Atideso pana chabbidho.

After `amha`, `tumha`, `ntu`, `rāja`, `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha`, `satthu`, `pitu` and the like the inflection `smā` is to be regarded as just like `nā`: this is a transfer (`atidesa`) that gives the inflection `smā` the nature of `nā`.[^24] Now transfer is of six kinds.

> Vuttañca –

And it is said:

> ‘‘Byapadeso nimittañca, taṃrūpaṃ taṃsabhāvatā; \
> Suttañceva tathā kāri-yātidesoti chabbidho’’ti.

"Designation and conditioning context, its form, its nature, \
the rule itself, and likewise the operation — thus transfer is sixfold."

> Tatrāyaṃ brahmattasakhā dīsu pāṭhasāmatthiyato rūpātideso. Sesaṃ tatiyāsamaṃ.

Among these, this one, for `brahma`, `atta`, `sakha` and the rest, is a transfer of form (`rūpātidesa`), by the force of the wording. The rest is the same as the third form.

> Raññā apeti, rājūhi rājūbhi, rājehi rājebhi, rañño, rājino santakaṃ, raññaṃ rājūnaṃ rājānaṃ.

`raññā apeti` ("he goes away from the king"); `rājūhi`, `rājūbhi`, `rājehi`, `rājebhi`; `rañño`, `rājino santakaṃ` ("the king's property"); `raññaṃ`, `rājūnaṃ`, `rājānaṃ`.

> ‘‘Rājassā’’ti vattate.

`rājassa` ("of `rāja`") continues.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑤⨀(rāja) | raññā apeti | he goes away from the king |
| 🚹⑤⨂(rāja) | rājūhi, rājūbhi, rājehi, rājebhi | from the kings |
| 🚹⑥⨀(rāja) | rañño, rājino santakaṃ | the king's property |
| 🚹⑥⨂(rāja) | raññaṃ, rājūnaṃ, rājānaṃ | of the kings |

|     | rāja + smā (⑤⨀)          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | rāja + (smā→nā)          | §270 |
| →   | (rāja + nā→raññā)        | §137 |
| →   | **raññā**                |      |

:::

[^24]: CST4's `nābhāvātideso`, "a transfer giving [`smā`] the nature of `nā`", is the Burmese reading; the Thai edition has `nābyapadeso`, "designation as `nā`", and the Sinhalese `nābyapadesātideso` (Rachiwong 1995, Explanatory Note on rule 120; she prefers the Burmese wording because the Padarūpasiddhi-ṭīkā paraphrases `smāvacanassa nārūpassa atideso`) — and the CST itself uses the Thai wording when it anticipates this rule at [104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r104), `smā vacanassa nābyapadeso`. The CST is kept.

---

<a name="r121"></a>

### 121 (§138): Smiṃmhi raññe rājini (`raññe`, `rājini` for `rāja` before `smiṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§138 (121): Smiṃmhi raññe rājini" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m138" description="`raññe`, `rājini` for `rāja` before `smiṃ`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last `rāja` rule in both orders (Kaccāyana's §138). No option marker, two results, `raññe` and `rājini`. With it the paradigm of `rāja` is complete, and Buddhappiya passes to `brahma`, whose `brahmā`, `brahmāno` are already given by [113 (§189): Syā ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r113) and [114 (§190): Yonamāno](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r114). For its vocative he carries over the letter `e` from [135 (§192): Smime](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r135), the `sakha` rule in which Kaccāyana's `ekāro` originates, fourteen suttas ahead.

:::

> Sabbasseva rāja saddassa savibhattissa raññerājini iccete ādesā honti smiṃ mhi vibhattimhi. Raññe, rājini patiṭṭhitaṃ, rājūsu rājesu.

The whole of the word `rāja` together with its inflection is replaced by `raññe` and `rājini` when the inflection `smiṃ` follows: `raññe`, `rājini patiṭṭhitaṃ` ("established in the king"); `rājūsu`, `rājesu`.

The whole paradigm of `rāja` ("king"), from 113–121:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | rājā | rājāno |
| ⓪ | bho rāja, bho rājā | bhavanto rājāno |
| ② | rājānaṃ, rājaṃ | rājāno |
| ③ | raññā, rājena | rājūhi, rājūbhi, rājehi, rājebhi |
| ④ | rañño, rājino | raññaṃ, rājūnaṃ, rājānaṃ |
| ⑤ | raññā | rājūhi, rājūbhi, rājehi, rājebhi |
| ⑥ | rañño, rājino | raññaṃ, rājūnaṃ, rājānaṃ |
| ⑦ | raññe, rājini | rājūsu, rājesu |

> Brahma saddassa ca ga nā sa smiṃ su viseso. Brahmā, brahmāno.

The word `brahma` ("Brahmā") too differs before `ga`, `nā`, `sa` and `smiṃ`. `brahmā`, `brahmāno`.

> Ālapane ca ‘‘e’’ti vattate.

And in address, `e` continues.[^25]

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(rāja) | raññe patiṭṭhitaṃ | established in the king |
| 🚹⑦⨀(rāja) | rājini patiṭṭhitaṃ | established in the king |
| 🚹⑦⨂(rāja) | rājūsu, rājesu | among the kings |
| 🚹①⨀(brahma) | brahmā | Brahmā |
| 🚹①⨂(brahma) | brahmāno | Brahmās |

|     | rāja + smiṃ (⑦⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (rāja + smiṃ→raññe)      | §138 |
| →   | **raññe**                |      |

|     | rāja + su (⑦⨂)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | (rāja→rāju) + su         | §169 |
| →   | rāj + (u→ū) + su         | §89  |
| →   | **rājūsu**               |      |

:::

[^25]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) takes `ālapane ca` as a second carried item, the sutta [70 (§285): Ālapane ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r70) (which she cites as 287-70), beside `e`; the CST quotes only `e`, and Buddhappiya's parallels — `Ālapane gasaññāyaṃ` at [131 (§194): Sakhantassi nonānaṃsesu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r131) and `Ālapane ‘‘ge’’ti vattate` at [151 (§225): Yonaṃ no](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r151) — show `ālapane` to be a plain locative, "in address". She repeats the construal at 151.

---

<a name="r122"></a>

### 122 (§193): Brahmato gassa ca (and `e` for `ga` after `brahma`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§193 (122): Brahmato gassa ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m193" description="and `e` for `ga` after `brahma`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The vocative of `brahma`. In Kaccāyana this rule follows `Smime` (§192), the `sakha` rule whose `e` its `ca` extends to `ga` after `brahma`; Buddhappiya treats `brahma` before `sakha`, so the `e` had to be fetched by the `anuvutti` stated at the end of [121 (§138): Smiṃmhi raññe rājini](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r121) from [135 (§192): Smime](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r135), which is what his gloss on the `ca` says. The `anto` he puts in force beside `brahmassa` was last used in the `ntu` rules ([100 (§92): Ntussanto yosu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r100)). No option marker: `bho brahme` is the only address form taught.

:::

> Brahma saddato ga ssa ca e kāro hoti, ca ggahaṇaṃ e ggahaṇānukaḍḍhanatthaṃ, bho brahme, bhavanto brahmāno, brahmānaṃ brahmaṃ, brahmāno.

After the word `brahma` the `ga` too becomes the letter `e`; the word `ca` is to draw along the word `e`. `bho brahme`, `bhavanto brahmāno`; `brahmānaṃ`, `brahmaṃ`; `brahmāno`.

> Vipariṇāmena ‘‘brahmassa, anto’’ti ca vattate.

With a change of inflection, `brahmassa` ("of `brahma`") and `anto` ("the final") continue.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⓪⨀(brahma) | bho brahme | Brahmā! |
| 🚹⓪⨂(brahma) | bhavanto brahmāno | Brahmās! |
| 🚹②⨀(brahma) | brahmānaṃ, brahmaṃ | Brahmā |
| 🚹②⨂(brahma) | brahmāno | Brahmās |

|     | brahma + ga (⓪⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | brahma + (ga→e)          | §193 |
| →   | brahm + (~~a~~ + e)      | §83  |
| →   | **bho brahme**           |      |

:::

---

<a name="r123"></a>

### 123 (§198): Uttaṃ sanāsu (`u` before `sa`, `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§198 (123): Uttaṃ sanāsu" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m198" description="`u` before `sa`, `nā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last of Kaccāyana's `brahma` rules (§198), cited by Buddhappiya third, at the third form, because the `u` it makes is the key to the `nā` and `sa` forms alike. The `bhāvaniddesa` his gloss draws from `uttaṃ` is what allows `brahmassa` beside `brahmuno` in the next rule. `brahmunā` needs no further rule, since [79 (§103): Ato nena](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r79) wants a stem in `a`. No option marker in the rule itself.

:::

> Brahma saddassa anto u ttamāpajjate sa nā iccetesu vacanesu. Utta miti bhāvaniddeso katthaci abhāvadassanattho. Brahmunā, brahmehi brahmebhi.

The final of the word `brahma` becomes `u` before the inflections `sa` and `nā`. `uttaṃ` ("`u`-ness"), a statement in the abstract, is to show that in some places [the change] does not occur. `brahmunā`; `brahmehi`, `brahmebhi`.

> Sa smiṃ u tte kate ‘‘ivaṇṇuvaṇṇā jhalā’’ti la saññāyaṃ –

Before `sa`, when the `u` has been made, [the stem] being named `la` by [§58 (29): Ivaṇṇuvaṇṇā jhalā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m58) —

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(brahma) | brahmunā | by Brahmā |
| 🚹③⨂(brahma) | brahmehi, brahmebhi | by the Brahmās |

|     | brahma + nā (③⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | brahm + (a→u) + nā       | §198 |
| →   | **brahmunā**             |      |

:::

---

<a name="r124"></a>

### 124 (§117): Jhalato sassa no vā (`jha`, `la`: `sa` to `no`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§117 (124): Jhalato sassano vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m117" description="`jha`, `la`: `sa` to `no`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's general rule for the `i` and `u` stems (§117), cited by Buddhappiya first for `brahma`, whose final has just become `u`, and referred to afterwards for `sakhino`, `aggino`, `bhikkhuno` and the rest. The `vā` is an open option, `brahmuno` beside `brahmassa` — the `brahmassa` that the `bhāvaniddesa` of [123 (§198): Uttaṃ sanāsu](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r123) allowed for. The gloss then splits that sutta (`yogavibhāga`), which gives `brahmūnaṃ` beside `brahmānaṃ`.

:::

> Jhala saññehi ivaṇṇuvaṇṇehi parassa sa iccetassa vacanassa no iccādeso hoti vā. Brahmuno brahmassa, brahmānaṃ brahmūnaṃ vā, utta miti yogavibhāgena aññatthāpi uttaṃ.

After [stems in] the `i`-sounds and `u`-sounds named `jha` and `la`, the inflection `sa` is optionally replaced by `no`: `brahmuno`, `brahmassa`; `brahmānaṃ` or `brahmūnaṃ` — by splitting the rule [123] as `uttaṃ`, the `u` also [occurs] elsewhere.

> Pañcamiyaṃ nābhāvātideso. Brahmunā, brahmehi brahmebhi, brahmuno brahmassa, brahmānaṃ brahmūnaṃ vā.

In the fifth form, the transfer that gives [`smā`] the nature of `nā`: `brahmunā`; `brahmehi`, `brahmebhi`; `brahmuno`, `brahmassa`; `brahmānaṃ` or `brahmūnaṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(brahma) | brahmuno, brahmassa | to Brahmā |
| 🚹④⨂(brahma) | brahmānaṃ, brahmūnaṃ | to the Brahmās |
| 🚹⑤⨀(brahma) | brahmunā | from Brahmā |
| 🚹⑥⨀(brahma) | brahmuno, brahmassa | Brahmā's |

|     | brahma + sa (④⨀)         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | brahm + (a→u) + sa       | §198 |
| →   | brahmu + (sa→no)         | §117 |
| →   | **brahmuno**             |      |

|     | brahma + naṃ (④⨂)        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | brahm + (a→u) + naṃ      | §198 (split as `uttaṃ`) |
| →   | brahm + (u→ū) + naṃ      | §89  |
| →   | **brahmūnaṃ**            |      |

:::

---

<a name="r125"></a>

### 125 (§197): Brahmato tu smiṃ ni (but after `brahma`, `ni` for `smiṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§197 (125): Brahmato tu smiṃ ni" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m197" description="but after `brahma`, `ni` for `smiṃ`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The seventh form of `brahma`, Kaccāyana's §197, completes its paradigm. Kaccāyana's `tu` marks the contrast with the `e` that `sakha` takes for `smiṃ`; Buddhappiya, whose `sakha` rule comes later, reads the `tu` only as Kaccāyana's vutti also reads it, as extending `ni` to `kamma`, `camma`, `muddha` and the like, and adds `kvaci`, since for those stems `ni` is one form among others (as he shows at [197 (§160): A kammantassa ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r197)). Ruiz-Falqués (§2.2) lists this `tu` among the words that stand for `kvaci` in the Rūpasiddhi. For the `nā` of `atta` he borrows the `ca` of Kaccāyana's `kamma` rule ([§160 (197): A kammantassa ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m160)), as Kaccāyana's own vutti of §160 does; the `a` it substitutes is a replacement, to which [79 (§103): Ato nena](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r79) does not apply, so `nā` stays and `attanā` results.

:::

> Brahma saddato smiṃ vacanassa ni hoti. Tu saddena kammacammamuddhā dito ca kvaci. Brahmani, brahmesu.

After the word `brahma` the inflection `smiṃ` becomes `ni`. By the word `tu`, also after `kamma` ("action"), `camma` ("hide"), `muddha` ("head") and the like, in some places. `brahmani`, `brahmesu`.

The whole paradigm of `brahma` ("Brahmā"), from 113–125:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | brahmā | brahmāno |
| ⓪ | bho brahme | bhavanto brahmāno |
| ② | brahmānaṃ, brahmaṃ | brahmāno |
| ③ | brahmunā | brahmehi, brahmebhi |
| ④ | brahmuno, brahmassa | brahmānaṃ, brahmūnaṃ |
| ⑤ | brahmunā | brahmehi, brahmebhi |
| ⑥ | brahmuno, brahmassa | brahmānaṃ, brahmūnaṃ |
| ⑦ | brahmani | brahmesu |

> Atta saddassa tatiyādīsveva viseso.

The word `atta` ("self") differs only in the third form and the following.

> Attā, attāno, bho atta bho attā, bhavanto attāno, attānaṃ attaṃ, attāno.

`attā`, `attāno`; `bho atta`, `bho attā`, `bhavanto attāno`; `attānaṃ`, `attaṃ`; `attāno`.

> Nā mhi ‘‘akammantassa cā’’ti ettha ca saddena atta ntassa attaṃ vā. Attanā attena vā.

Before `nā`, by the word `ca` in [§160 (197): A kammantassa ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m160), the final of `atta` optionally becomes `a`: `attanā`, or `attena`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑦⨀(brahma) | brahmani | in Brahmā |
| 🚹⑦⨂(brahma) | brahmesu | among the Brahmās |
| 🚻⑦⨀(kamma) | kammani | in the action (by the `tu`, in some places) |
| 🚹①⨀(atta) | attā | the self |
| 🚹①⨂(atta) | attāno | the selves |
| 🚹②⨀(atta) | attānaṃ, attaṃ | the self |
| 🚹③⨀(atta) | attanā, attena | by the self |

|     | brahma + smiṃ (⑦⨀)       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | brahma + (smiṃ→ni)       | §197 |
| →   | **brahmani**             |      |

|     | atta + nā (③⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | att + (a→a) + nā         | §160 (by its `ca`; `nā` is kept) |
| →   | **attanā**               |      |

|     | atta + nā (③⨀)           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------ | ---- |
| →   | atta + (nā→ena)          | §103 |
