Where Kaccāyana's vutti has only `caggahaṇamanukaḍḍhanatthaṃ` ("to draw along" the earlier words), Buddhappiya makes the `ca` `katthaci paṭisedhanivattanatthaṃ` — the reading Ruiz-Falqués lists at Rūp 201 among the uses of `ca` as a blocker of a blocker (*Boundaries and Domains* §3.5); the `pubbā`, `pubbe` it admits are derived at [208 (§165): Dvandaṭṭhā vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r208) from [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m108). The rule itself has no option marker — `neva`, "not at all" — but its `ca` opens a `katthaci` domain inside the prohibition: `pubbasmā` and `pubbā` are both good, `sabbā` as a fifth form is not.

:::

> A kārantehi sabbanāmehi paresaṃ smā smiṃ iccetesaṃ, catutthekavacanassa ca ā e āya iccete ādesā neva hontīti āyā desābhāvo. Ca ggahaṇaṃ katthaci paṭisedhanivattanatthaṃ, tena pubbādīhi smā smiṃnaṃ ā e ca honti. Sabbassa.

After pronouns ending in `a`, the three replacements — `ā` and `e` for `smā` and `smiṃ`, and `āya` for the fourth form singular — do not occur at all; hence there is no replacement by `āya`. The mention of `ca` is for cancelling the prohibition in some places; hence after `pubba` and the like `ā` and `e` do occur for `smā` and `smiṃ`. `sabbassa` ("of all; to all").

|     | sabba + sa (🚹④⨀)     | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabba + (s) + sa      | §61  |
| →   | **sabbassa**          |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(sabba) | sabbassa | to all (not `sabbāya`) |
| 🚹⑤⨀(pubba) | pubbā | from the former (by the `ca`, §108) |
| 🚹⑦⨀(pubba) | pubbe | in the former (by the `ca`, §108) |

:::

> ‘‘Akāro, e’’ti ca vattate.

And "`akāro`" (the `a`) and "`e`" continue [from [80 (§101): Suhisvakāro e](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r80)].

---

<a name="r202"></a>

### 202 (§102): Sabbanāmānaṃ naṃmhi ca (And pronouns in `naṃ`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§102 (202): Sabbanāmānaṃ naṃmhi ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m102" description="And pronouns in `naṃ`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §102 extends §101 `Suhisvakāro e` to `naṃ` for pronouns and stands beside it in the first section; Buddhappiya brings it to ④⨂ of `sabba`, before the rule that replaces the `naṃ` itself, so that `sabbesaṃ` is derived in the order of its operations — `e` for `a`, then `saṃ` for `naṃ`. Kaccāyana's `caggahaṇamanukaḍḍhanatthaṃ` he replaces with the explicit `anuvutti` "`akāro`, `e` continue" from [80 (§101): Suhisvakāro e](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r80), so the `ca` needs no gloss of its own. There is no option marker: a pronoun's `a` is always `e` before `naṃ` — `sabbesaṃ`, never `sabbānaṃ` — except where [209 (§166): Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r209) and [210 (§167): Bahubbīhimhi ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r210) suspend the pronominal treatment in compounds.

:::

> Sabbesaṃ sabbanāmānaṃ a kāro e ttamāpajjate naṃ mhi vibhattimhi.

The final `a` of all pronouns becomes `e` when the inflection `naṃ` (④⨂, ⑥⨂) follows.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨂(sabba) | sabbesaṃ | to all (with the `saṃ` of the next rule) |
| 🚹④⨂(sabba) | sabbesānaṃ | to all (with the `sānaṃ` of the next rule) |

:::

> ‘‘Sabbanāmato’’ti ca vattate.

And "`sabbanāmato`" (after a pronoun) continues [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r203"></a>

### 203 (§168): Sabbato naṃ saṃsānaṃ (`saṃ`, `sānaṃ` for `naṃ` after any pronoun) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§168 (203): Sabbato naṃ saṃ sānaṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m168" description="`saṃ`, `sānaṃ` for `naṃ` after any pronoun" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §168 closes his run of pronoun rules (§164–§168); Buddhappiya takes it together with 202 at ④⨂ and then completes the masculine paradigm of `sabba` — `sabbasmā`, `sabbasmiṃ` with the prohibition of 201 in force — before turning to the feminine. The gloss reads `sabbato` as `sabbanāmato`, as Kaccāyana's does. There is no option marker: `naṃ` never keeps its shape after a pronoun, and `saṃ` and `sānaṃ` are both always available. The lead-in to 204 declares yet another `anuvutti` from a rule still to come: `sabbato` from [224 (§178): Sabbato ko](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r224), Kaccāyana's §178, which immediately precedes §179 in his order.

:::

> Sabbato sabbanāmato parassa naṃ vacanassa saṃ sānaṃ iccete ādesā honti.

After every pronoun, the inflection `naṃ` (④⨂, ⑥⨂) is replaced by `saṃ` and `sānaṃ`.

|     | sabba + naṃ (🚹④⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabb + (a→e) + naṃ      | §102 |
| →   | sabbe + (naṃ→saṃ)       | §168 |
| →   | **sabbesaṃ**            |      |

|     | sabba + naṃ (🚹④⨂)      | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabb + (a→e) + naṃ      | §102 |
| →   | sabbe + (naṃ→sānaṃ)     | §168 |
| →   | **sabbesānaṃ**          |      |

> Sabbesaṃ sabbesānaṃ, sabbasmā sabbamhā, sabbehi sabbebhi, sabbassa, sabbesaṃ sabbesānaṃ, sabbasmiṃ sabbamhi, sabbesu.

The inflection of 🚹(sabba), with the forms given at 200–201:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | sabbo | sabbe |
| ⓪ | he sabba, he sabbā | he sabbe |
| ② | sabbaṃ | sabbe |
| ③ | sabbena | sabbehi, sabbebhi |
| ④ | sabbassa | sabbesaṃ, sabbesānaṃ |
| ⑤ | sabbasmā, sabbamhā | sabbehi, sabbebhi |
| ⑥ | sabbassa | sabbesaṃ, sabbesānaṃ |
| ⑦ | sabbasmiṃ, sabbamhi | sabbesu |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨂(sabba) | sabbesaṃ, sabbesānaṃ | to all |
| 🚹⑤⨀(sabba) | sabbasmā, sabbamhā | from all (no `sabbā`, by 201) |
| 🚹⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbasmiṃ, sabbamhi | in all (no `sabbe`, by 201) |
| 🚺①⨀(sabba) | sabbā pajā | all people |

:::

> Itthiyaṃ ‘‘itthiyamato āppaccayo’’ti ā ppaccayo. Aññaṃ kaññā saddasamaṃ aññatra sa naṃ smiṃ vacanehi. Sabbā pajā, sabbā sabbāyo, he sabbe, he sabbā sabbāyo, sabbaṃ, sabbā sabbāyo, sabbāya, sabbāhi sabbābhi.

In the feminine, by [§237 (176): Itthīyamato āpaccayo](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m237) the suffix `ā` [is added]. Apart from the inflections `sa`, `naṃ` and `smiṃ` it is the same as the word `kaññā` ([176 (§237): Itthiyamato āppaccayo](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r176) to [180 (§216): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r180)): `sabbā pajā` ("all people"); `sabbā`, `sabbāyo`; `he sabbe`; `he sabbā`, `he sabbāyo`; `sabbaṃ`; `sabbā`, `sabbāyo`; `sabbāya`; `sabbāhi`, `sabbābhi`.

> Catutthekavacane ‘‘sabbanāmato vā’’, sabbato ko ti ito ‘‘sabbato’’ti ca vattate.

In the fourth form singular: "`sabbanāmato`" and "`vā`" continue, and from [224 (§178): Sabbato ko](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r224) "`sabbato`" continues.

---

<a name="r204"></a>

### 204 (§179): Ghapato smiṃsānaṃ saṃsā (`saṃ`, `sā` for `smiṃ`, `sa` after `gha`, `pa` pronouns) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§179 (204): Ghapato smiṃ sānaṃ saṃsā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m179" description="`saṃ`, `sā` for `smiṃ`, `sa` after `gha`, `pa` pronouns" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

In Kaccāyana the rule follows §178 `Sabbato ko` and shares its `sabbato sabbanāmato`; Buddhappiya brings it to the feminine `sabbā` at ④⨀ and ⑦⨀ and leaves §178 for `amu` at [224 (§178): Sabbato ko](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r224), where the augment `ka` is at home — which is why he has just declared that `sabbato` continues from there. `gha` is a feminine stem in `ā` ([§60 (177): Ā gho](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m60)), `pa` one in `i`, `ī`, `u` or `ū` ([§59 (182): Te itthikhyā po](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m59)). What completes the forms he gives in the two rules he interposes next, [205 (§66): Gho rassaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r205) and [206 (§62): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r206), so that `sabbassā` is derived operation by operation. The `vā` is an open option, `vikappa` (*Boundaries and Domains* §3.1): `sabbassā` beside `sabbāya`, `sabbassaṃ` beside `sabbāyaṃ`; Buddhappiya states no domain for it.

:::

> Sabbato sabbanāmato ghapa saññato smiṃ sa iccetesaṃ yathākkamaṃ saṃsā ādesā honti vā.

After every pronoun that bears the name `gha` or `pa`, the inflections `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) and `sa` (④⨀, ⑥⨀) are optionally replaced by `saṃ` and `sā`, respectively.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺④⨀(sabba) | sabbassā | to all (women); completed at 206 |
| 🚺④⨀(sabba) | sabbāya | to all (women), by §111 |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbassaṃ | in all (women); completed at 207 |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbāyaṃ | in all (women), by §216 |

:::

> ‘‘Saṃsāsvekavacanesu cā’’ti vattate.

"`Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca`" ([206 (§62): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r206)) continues [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r205"></a>

### 205 (§66): Gho rassaṃ (`gha` becomes short) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§66 (205): Gho rassaṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m66" description="`gha` becomes short" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §66 belongs to the small group of feminine-pronoun rules he set among his opening definitions (§62–§66); Buddhappiya brings that group — `Gho rassaṃ`, `Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca`, `Tato sassa ssāya`, `Tassā vā`, `Etimāsami` — to the pronouns whose forms need them, each at its place in the derivation: after `saṃ` and `sā` have replaced the inflection (204), the `ā` is shortened (205) and the `s` inserted (206). The gloss's `saṃsāsvekavacanesu vibhattādesesu paresu` is supplied from the rule he has not yet stated, by the `vattate` that closed 204. There is no option marker: once `saṃ` or `sā` is chosen the shortening is invariable — `sabbassā`, never `sabbāssā`.

:::

> Gha sañño ā kāro rassamāpajjate saṃsā svekavacanesu vibhattādesesu paresu.

The `ā` that bears the name `gha` is shortened when the singular replacements of inflections `saṃ` and `sā` follow.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑥⨀(sabba) | sabbassā | of all (women): `sabbā` shortened before `sā` |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbassaṃ | in all (women): `sabbā` shortened before `saṃ` |

:::

> ‘‘Sāgamo’’ti vattate.

"`sāgamo`" (the augment `s`) continues [from [86 (§61): Sāgamo se](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r86)].

---

<a name="r206"></a>

### 206 (§62): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca (And in `saṃ`, `sā` singular inflections) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§62 (206): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m62" description="And in `saṃ`, `sā` singular inflections" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The `ca` of Kaccāyana's §62 extends §61 `Sāgamo se` to the replacements `saṃ` and `sā`; Buddhappiya states the extension as `anuvutti` ("`sāgamo` continues") and adds `ekavacanaṭṭhānasambhūtesu`, which excludes the plural `sabbāsaṃ` (④⨂), where `saṃ` stands for `naṃ`, and the `sā` of `manasā`, where the `s` belongs to the stem — the two counter-examples of Kaccāyana's vutti. There is no option marker.

:::

> Saṃ sā iccetesu ekavacanaṭṭhānasambhūtesu vibhattādesesu paresu liṅgamhā sa kārāgamo hoti.

When the replacements of inflections `saṃ` and `sā`, arisen in the place of a singular inflection, follow, the augment `s` is [inserted] after the stem.

> Sabbassā sabbāya, sabbāsaṃ sabbāsānaṃ, sabbāya, sabbāhi sabbābhi, sabbassā sabbāya, sabbāsaṃ sabbāsānaṃ.

`sabbassā`, `sabbāya`; `sabbāsaṃ`, `sabbāsānaṃ`; `sabbāya`; `sabbāhi`, `sabbābhi`; `sabbassā`, `sabbāya`; `sabbāsaṃ`, `sabbāsānaṃ` [the fourth, fifth and sixth forms].

|     | sabbā + sa (🚺④⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabbā + (sa→sā)        | §179 |
| →   | sabb + (ā→a) + sā      | §66  |
| →   | sabba + (s) + sā       | §62  |
| →   | **sabbassā**           |      |

|     | sabbā + naṃ (🚺④⨂)     | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabbā + (naṃ→saṃ)      | §168 |
| →   | **sabbāsaṃ**           |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺④⨀(sabba) | sabbassā, sabbāya | to all (women) |
| 🚺④⨂(sabba) | sabbāsaṃ, sabbāsānaṃ | to all (women) |
| 🚺⑤⨀(sabba) | sabbāya | from all (women) |
| 🚺⑤⨂(sabba) | sabbāhi, sabbābhi | from all (women) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(sabba) | sabbassā, sabbāya | of all (women) |
| 🚺⑥⨂(sabba) | sabbāsaṃ, sabbāsānaṃ | of all (women) |

:::

> Smiṃ mhi ‘‘sabbanāmato, ghapato’’ti ca vattate.

For `smiṃ`: and "`sabbanāmato`" and "`ghapato`" continue [from 204].

---

<a name="r207"></a>

### 207 (§180): Netāhi smimāyayā (No `āya`, `yā` for `smiṃ` after these) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§180 (207): Netāhi smimāya yā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m180" description="No `āya`, `yā` for `smiṃ` after these" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

In Kaccāyana §180 follows §179 directly; Buddhappiya has interposed 205–206 and reaches it at ⑦⨀ of `sabbā`. The `āya` it forbids is that of [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m111), the `yā` that of [§112 (183): Pato yā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m112); only `saṃ` (204) and the `yaṃ` of [§216 (180): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m216) remain. The `vā` the gloss invokes for `dakkhiṇāya`, `uttarāya` is the one carried from 204, and here it does the work of `kvaci`: it opens no free choice between `sabbassaṃ` and `sabbāya` but admits the prohibited shape for a few words — one of the anomalies Ruiz-Falqués records (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.4 and n. 26), `vā` = `kvaci`, "probably", at Rūp 207, a restricted use that the main paraphrase of the sutta does not name a `vavatthitavibhāsā`. The rest of the section closes `sabba` with the neuter, whose `sabbaṃ` and `sabbāni` come from 195–196, and introduces the seven pronouns from `katara` to `aññatama` that inflect like it.

:::

> Etehi sabbanāmehi ghapa saññehi parassa smiṃ vacanassa neva āya yā desā hontīti āyā bhāvo. Vā dhikārato kvaci hoti dakkhiṇāya uttarāyāti ādi.

After these pronouns that bear the names `gha` and `pa`, the inflection `smiṃ` (⑦⨀) is not at all replaced by `āya` or `yā`; hence there is no `āya`. By the governing `vā`, [the replacement] does occur in some places: `dakkhiṇāya` ("in the south"), `uttarāya` ("in the north") and the like.

> Saṃya mādesā, sabbassaṃ sabbāyaṃ, sabbāsu.

The replacements `saṃ` [204] and `yaṃ` [§216]: `sabbassaṃ`, `sabbāyaṃ`; `sabbāsu`.[^64]

|     | sabbā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)    | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabbā + (smiṃ→saṃ)     | §179 |
| →   | sabb + (ā→a) + saṃ     | §66  |
| →   | sabba + (s) + saṃ      | §62  |
| →   | **sabbassaṃ**          |      |

|     | sabbā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)    | rule |
| --- | ---------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sabbā + (smiṃ→yaṃ)     | §216 |
| →   | **sabbāyaṃ**           |      |

The inflection of 🚺(sabbā):

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | sabbā | sabbā, sabbāyo |
| ⓪ | he sabbe | he sabbā, he sabbāyo |
| ② | sabbaṃ | sabbā, sabbāyo |
| ③ | sabbāya | sabbāhi, sabbābhi |
| ④ | sabbassā, sabbāya | sabbāsaṃ, sabbāsānaṃ |
| ⑤ | sabbāya | sabbāhi, sabbābhi |
| ⑥ | sabbassā, sabbāya | sabbāsaṃ, sabbāsānaṃ |
| ⑦ | sabbassaṃ, sabbāyaṃ | sabbāsu |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbassaṃ | in all (women) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(sabba) | sabbāyaṃ | in all (women), by §216 |
| 🚺⑦⨀(dakkhiṇa) | dakkhiṇāya | in the south (the `āya` admitted `kvaci`) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(uttara) | uttarāya | in the north (the `āya` admitted `kvaci`) |
| 🚺⑦⨂(sabba) | sabbāsu | in all (women) |

:::

> Napuṃsake sabbaṃ cittaṃ, sabbāni, he sabba, he sabbāni, sabbaṃ, sabbāni. Sesaṃ pulliṅge viya ñeyyaṃ.

In the neuter: `sabbaṃ cittaṃ` ("every mind"); `sabbāni`; `he sabba`; `he sabbāni`; `sabbaṃ`; `sabbāni`. The rest is to be understood as in the masculine.

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | sabbaṃ | sabbāni |
| ⓪ | he sabba | he sabbāni |
| ② | sabbaṃ | sabbāni |

> Evaṃ katarā dīnaṃ aññatama saddapariyantānaṃ tīsupi liṅgesu rūpanayo.

In the same way runs the course of the forms of `katara` and the rest, down to the word `aññatama`, in all three genders.

> Tattha katarakatama saddā pucchanatthā.

Among them, the words `katara` and `katama` have the sense of a question ("which of two?", "which of many?").

> Ubhaya saddo dviavayavasamudāyavacano.

The word `ubhaya` denotes a whole made up of two parts ("both").

> Itara saddo vuttappaṭiyogavacano.

The word `itara` denotes the counterpart of what has been mentioned ("the other").

> Añña saddo adhikatāparavacano.

The word `añña` denotes what is other than the matter in hand ("another").[^65]

> Aññatara aññatama saddā aniyamatthā.

The words `aññatara` and `aññatama` have an indefinite sense ("a certain one", "one or other").

> ‘‘Yo, sabbanāmakārate paṭhamo’’ti ca vattate.

And "`yo`" and "`Sabbanāmakārate paṭhamo`" ([200 (§164): Sabbanāmakārate paṭhamo](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r200)) continue [into the next rule].

[^64]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) renders `Saṃyamādesā` as "The sound 'saṃ' is changed into 'yaṃ'"; the word is a `dvanda`, the two replacements `saṃ` (204) and `yaṃ` (§216) that give `sabbassaṃ` and `sabbāyaṃ`, and nothing turns `saṃ` into `yaṃ`.

[^65]: Rachiwong (1995) glosses `itara` as "other than that which is described" and `añña` as "additional reference", which blurs Buddhappiya's two definitions: `vuttappaṭiyogavacano` is the counterpart of what has been mentioned, `adhikatāparavacano` what is other than the matter in hand.

---

<a name="r208"></a>

### 208 (§165): Dvandaṭṭhā vā (Optionally [`e`] after [a pronoun] standing in a `dvanda`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§165 (208): Dvandaṭṭhā vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m165" description="Optionally [`e`] after [a pronoun] standing in a `dvanda`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §165 follows §164 at once; Buddhappiya defers it until `sabba` is done in all three genders and the pronouns that form compounds come up — `katara` and `katama` in a `dvanda`, then `pubba` and its group. The `vā` is an open option, `vikappa` (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.3): `katarakatame` and `katarakatamā` are both good Pāli. The option in ①⨂ of `pubba` no sutta states; Buddhappiya finds it in the wording of [§432 (362): Dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m432), where the grammar itself writes `parā` — the ①⨂ of the pronoun `para` with the nominal `ā` of [§107 (69): Sabbayonīnamāe](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m107) — and not `pare`: the sutta's own usage is a `niddesa` that licenses `pubbā` beside `pubbe` for the whole group of direction words. Their `ā` and `e` for `smā` and `smiṃ` by [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m108) are the `katthaci` domain that the `ca` of [201 (§110): Tayo neva ca sabbanāmehi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r201) opened inside the prohibition. The Bālāvatāra reaches the same `pubbe` by `nipātana` (¶151).

:::

> Dvandasamāsaṭṭhā sabbanāmakārato paro paṭhamo yo e ttamāpajjate vā. Kataro ca katamo cāti katarakatame, katarakatamā vā iccādi.

After the final `a` of a pronoun standing in a `dvanda` compound, the first `yo` (①⨂) optionally becomes `e`. "Which of two and which of many?" — `katarakatame`, or `katarakatamā`, and so on.

|     | katarakatama + yo (🚹①⨂)    | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | katarakatama + (yo→e)       | §165 |
| →   | katarakatam + (~~a~~ + e)   | §83  |
| →   | **katarakatame**            |      |

|     | katarakatama + yo (🚹①⨂)    | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | katarakatama + (yo→ā)       | §107 |
| →   | katarakatam + (~~a~~ + ā)   | §83  |
| →   | **katarakatamā**            |      |

> Pubbā dayo disādivavatthānavacanā.

`pubba` and the rest [down to `adhara`] denote the fixing of direction and the like [east and west, before and after, upper and lower].

> Pubbo kālo. Bahuvacane ‘‘dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā’’ti ettha parā ti niddesato pubbādīhi yo vacanassa vikappenekāro.

`pubbo kālo` ("the former time"). In the plural, because of the wording `parā` in [§432 (362): Dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā](/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m432), the inflection `yo` after `pubba` and the rest becomes `e` only optionally.

> Pubbe pubbā, he pubba, he pubbe he pubbā, pubbaṃ, pubbe, pubbena, pubbehi pubbebhi, pubbassa, pubbesaṃ pubbesānaṃ. ‘‘Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā’’ti vikappenākārekārā. Pubbā pubbasmā pubbamhā, pubbehi pubbebhi, pubbassa, pubbesaṃ pubbesānaṃ, pubbe pubbasmiṃ pubbamhi, pubbesu.

The inflection of 🚹(pubba):

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | pubbo | pubbe, pubbā |
| ⓪ | he pubba | he pubbe, he pubbā |
| ② | pubbaṃ | pubbe |
| ③ | pubbena | pubbehi, pubbebhi |
| ④ | pubbassa | pubbesaṃ, pubbesānaṃ |
| ⑤ | pubbā, pubbasmā, pubbamhā | pubbehi, pubbebhi |
| ⑥ | pubbassa | pubbesaṃ, pubbesānaṃ |
| ⑦ | pubbe, pubbasmiṃ, pubbamhi | pubbesu |

By [§108 (90): Smāsmiṃnaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m108) there are optionally `ā` and `e` [for `smā` and `smiṃ`].

|     | pubba + smā (🚹⑤⨀)      | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pubba + (smā→ā)         | §108 |
| →   | pubb + (~~a~~ + ā)      | §83  |
| →   | **pubbā**               |      |

|     | pubba + smiṃ (🚹⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | ----------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pubba + (smiṃ→e)        | §108 |
| →   | pubb + (~~a~~ + e)      | §83  |
| →   | **pubbe**               |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(katara + katama) | katarakatame | which of two and which of many? |
| 🚹①⨂(katara + katama) | katarakatamā | which of two and which of many? (by §107) |
| 🚹①⨀(pubba) | pubbo kālo | the former time |
| 🚹①⨂(pubba) | pubbe, pubbā | the former (ones) |
| 🚹⑤⨀(pubba) | pubbā, pubbasmā, pubbamhā | from the former |
| 🚹⑦⨀(pubba) | pubbe, pubbasmiṃ, pubbamhi | in the former |

:::

> Itthiyaṃ pubbā disā, pubbā pubbāyo iccādi sabbā saddasamaṃ.

In the feminine: `pubbā disā` ("the eastern direction"); `pubbā`, `pubbāyo`, and so on, the same as the word `sabbā`.

> Napuṃsake pubbaṃ ṭhānaṃ, pubbāni, he pubba, he pubbāni, pubbaṃ, pubbāni, sesaṃ pulliṅgasamaṃ. Evaṃ parāparadakkhiṇuttarādhara saddā.

In the neuter: `pubbaṃ ṭhānaṃ` ("the former place"); `pubbāni`; `he pubba`; `he pubbāni`; `pubbaṃ`; `pubbāni`; the rest is the same as the masculine. In the same way the words `para`, `apara`, `dakkhiṇa`, `uttara` and `adhara`.

> ‘‘Sabbanāmato, dvandaṭṭhā’’ti ca vattate.

And "`sabbanāmato`" and "`dvandaṭṭhā`" continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r209"></a>

### 209 (§166): Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ (No other pronominal operation [in a `dvanda`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§166 (209): Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m166" description="No other pronominal operation [in a `dvanda`]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The prohibition follows §165 in both books. Buddhappiya adds a device Kaccāyana's vutti does not have, `vinādhikārena yogena` — the rule applied without its governing word `dvandaṭṭhā` — to reach the `tappurisa` `māsapubbāya`, `māsapubbānaṃ`, which take the `āya` of [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m111) and the plain `ānaṃ` of [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m89) where a lone pronoun would have `ssā` (204) and `sānaṃ`. The rule has no option marker; its `na` simply withdraws the pronominal operations from the compound, so that `pubbāparānaṃ` is inflected as `purisānaṃ` is.

:::

> Dvandaṭṭhā sabbanāmato parassa yo vacanassa ṭhapetvā e ttaṃ aññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ kāriyaṃ na hotīti saṃsāna mādesābhāvo. Pubbāparānaṃ, pubbuttarānaṃ adharuttarānaṃ, ‘‘nāññaṃ sabbanāmika’’nti vinādhikārena yogena tatiyāsamāsepi. Māsapubbāya, māsapubbānaṃ.

After a pronoun standing in a `dvanda`, apart from the change of the inflection `yo` to `e`, no other pronominal operation takes place; hence there is no replacement by `saṃ` and `sānaṃ`: `pubbāparānaṃ` ("of the former and the latter"), `pubbuttarānaṃ` ("of the east and the north"), `adharuttarānaṃ` ("of the lower and the upper"). By applying "`Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ`" without its governing word [`dvandaṭṭhā`], [the same holds] in a compound of the third form too: `māsapubbāya` ("of the one a month senior"), `māsapubbānaṃ`.[^66]

|     | pubbāpara + naṃ (🚹⑥⨂)      | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | pubbāpar + (a→ā) + naṃ      | §89  |
| →   | **pubbāparānaṃ**            |      |

|     | māsapubbā + sa (🚺⑥⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | māsapubbā + (sa→āya)        | §111 |
| →   | māsapubb + (~~ā~~ + āya)    | §83  |
| →   | **māsapubbāya**             |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pubba + apara) | pubbāparānaṃ | of the former and the latter |
| 🚹⑥⨂(pubba + uttara) | pubbuttarānaṃ | of the east and the north |
| 🚹⑥⨂(adhara + uttara) | adharuttarānaṃ | of the lower and the upper |
| 🚺⑥⨀(māsa + pubba) | māsapubbāya | of the woman a month senior |
| 🚺⑥⨂(māsa + pubba) | māsapubbānaṃ | of the women a month senior |

:::

> ‘‘Nāññaṃ sabbanāmika’’nti ca vattate.

And "`Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ`" continues [into the next rule].

[^66]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) renders `vinādhikārena yogena` as "Not taking 'nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ' as governing, this is applicable also in the case of tatiyāsamāsa by yogavibhāga"; the Pāli applies the rule `Nāññaṃ sabbanāmikaṃ` without its governing word `dvandaṭṭhā` — the `adhikāra` is dropped, not the rule — and `yogavibhāga` is her paraphrase, not a word of the text.

---

<a name="r210"></a>

### 210 (§167): Bahubbīhimhi ca (And in a `bahubbīhi`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§167 (210): Bahubbīhimhi ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m167" description="And in a `bahubbīhi`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The `ca` extends 209 to the `bahubbīhi` and then, for pronouns with the sense of a direction, takes the prohibition back — the second job Kaccāyana's vutti reaches through its `Ceti kimatthaṃ?`. The `ca` thus cancels the prohibition in some places, as it did at [201 (§110): Tayo neva ca sabbanāmehi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r201) and will at [226 (§229): Sesesu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r226) (`katthacinivattanattha`, *Boundaries and Domains* §3.5); there is no option marker beyond it. The section then passes to `ya`, which is `sabba` throughout, and to `ta`, which needs rules of its own and for which Buddhappiya declares the `anuvutti` of `anapuṃsakassa … simhi` from [218 (§172): Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r218) and of `saṃ` from [223 (§173): Amussa mo saṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r223), both still ahead in his order.

:::

> Bahubbīhimhi ca samāse sabbanāmikavidhānaṃ nāññaṃ hoti. Piyapubbāya, piyapubbānaṃ, piyapubbe.

And in a `bahubbīhi` compound too, no other pronominal treatment takes place: `piyapubbāya` ("of the woman whose former [husband] was dear"), `piyapubbānaṃ`, `piyapubbe`.

> Ca saddaggahaṇena disatthasabbanāmānaṃ bahubbīhimhi sabbanāmikavidhānaṃva hoti.

By the mention of the word `ca`, for pronouns with the sense of a direction the pronominal treatment does take place in a `bahubbīhi`.

> Dakkhiṇassā ca pubbassā ca yadantarāḷanti atthe bahubbīhi, dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ, dakkhiṇapubbassā, evaṃ uttarapubbassaṃ, uttarapubbassā iccādi.

A `bahubbīhi` in the sense "the [direction] that lies between the south and the east": `dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ` ("in the south-east"), `dakkhiṇapubbassā` ("of the south-east"); in the same way `uttarapubbassaṃ`, `uttarapubbassā` ("north-east") and so on.

|     | dakkhiṇapubbā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dakkhiṇapubbā + (smiṃ→saṃ)      | §179 |
| →   | dakkhiṇapubb + (ā→a) + saṃ      | §66  |
| →   | dakkhiṇapubba + (s) + saṃ       | §62  |
| →   | **dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ**           |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺⑥⨀(piya + pubba) | piyapubbāya | of the woman whose former [husband] was dear |
| 🚺⑥⨂(piya + pubba) | piyapubbānaṃ | of the women whose former [husbands] were dear |
| 🚹⑦⨀(piya + pubba) | piyapubbe | in the man whose former [wife] was dear |
| 🚺⑦⨀(dakkhiṇa + pubba) | dakkhiṇapubbassaṃ | in the south-east |
| 🚺⑥⨀(dakkhiṇa + pubba) | dakkhiṇapubbassā | of the south-east |
| 🚺⑦⨀(uttara + pubba) | uttarapubbassaṃ | in the north-east |
| 🚺⑥⨀(uttara + pubba) | uttarapubbassā | of the north-east |

:::

> Yateta saddādīnamālapane rūpaṃ na sambhavati. Ya saddo aniyamattho.

For the words `ya`, `ta`, `eta` and the following [`ima`, `amu`, `kiṃ`] no form occurs in address. The word `ya` has an indefinite sense ("who", "which", "whatever").

> Yo puriso, ye purisā, yaṃ, ye. Yā kaññā, yā yāyo, yaṃ, yā yāyo. Yaṃ cittaṃ, yāni, yaṃ, yāni. Sesaṃ sabbattha sabba saddasamaṃ.

The first and second forms of `ya` in the three genders; the rest is everywhere the same as the word `sabba`:

| | 🚹 ⨀ | 🚹 ⨂ | 🚺 ⨀ | 🚺 ⨂ | 🚻 ⨀ | 🚻 ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ① | yo puriso | ye purisā | yā kaññā | yā, yāyo | yaṃ cittaṃ | yāni |
| ② | yaṃ | ye | yaṃ | yā, yāyo | yaṃ | yāni |

> Ta eta ima amu kiṃ iccete parammukha samīpa accantasamīpadūra pucchanatthavacanā.

`ta`, `eta`, `ima`, `amu` and `kiṃ` denote, respectively, what is out of sight ("that"), what is near ("this"), what is very near ("this here"), what is far ("that yonder") and a question ("who?", "what?").

> Ta saddassa bhedo. ‘‘Ta si’’ itīdha –

The word `ta` differs. When we have `ta` + `si` —

> ‘‘Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhīti, sa’’miti ca vattate.

"`Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhi`" ([218 (§172): Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r218)) — [its `anapuṃsakassa` and `simhi`] — and "`saṃ`" [from [223 (§173): Amussa mo saṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r223)] continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r211"></a>

### 211 (§174): Etatesaṃ to (`s` for the `t` of `eta`, `ta` [before `si`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§174 (211): Etatesaṃ to" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m174" description="`s` for the `t` of `eta`, `ta` [before `si`]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

In Kaccāyana §174 follows §172–§173, the rules for `ima` and `amu` before `si`, and takes `anapuṃsakassa`, `simhi` and `saṃ` (the letter `s`) from them; Buddhappiya treats `ta` before `eta`, `ima` and `amu`, so those rules ([218 (§172): Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r218), [223 (§173): Amussa mo saṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r223)) still lie ahead and he declared the `anuvutti` from them at the end of 210. The `vā` of §173 does not come with them: `so`, `sā`, `eso`, `esā` are the only first-form singulars.

:::

> Eta ta iccetesaṃ anapuṃsakānaṃ ta kāro sa kāramāpajjate si mhi vibhattimhi. So puriso.

The `t` of `eta` and `ta`, when they are not neuter, becomes `s` when the inflection `si` (①⨀) follows: `so puriso` ("that man").[^67]

|     | ta + si (🚹①⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (t→s) + a + si        | §174 |
| →   | sa + (si→o)           | §104 |
| →   | s + (~~a~~ + o)       | §83  |
| →   | **so**                |      |

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(ta) | so puriso | that man |

:::

> Sabbanāma ggahaṇañca, ito ta ggahaṇañca vattate.

The mention of `sabbanāma` [from 200–203] and, from here on, the mention of `ta` continue.

[^67]: The Thai edition read by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) adds `eso puriso` ("this man") before `so puriso` here; CST4 has `eso puriso` only where `eta` is run through, at the end of [216 (§64): Tassā vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r216).

---

<a name="r212"></a>

### 212 (§175): Tassa vā nattaṃ sabbattha (Optionally `n` for the `t` of `ta` everywhere) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§175 (212): Tassa vā nattaṃ sabbattha" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m175" description="Optionally `n` for the `t` of `ta` everywhere" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's order is kept. The gloss reads `sabbattha` as `sabbattha liṅgesu`, "in all genders", as Kaccāyana's vutti does, though the examples show the change holding for all inflections too — all but ①⨀, where 211 has already turned the `t` into `s`. The `vā` is an open option, `vikappa` (*Boundaries and Domains* §3.1): `ne` and `te`, `naṃ` and `taṃ`, `nena` and `tena` are all good Pāli, and Buddhappiya sets no domain — he does not, for instance, confine the `na` forms to the anaphoric use the texts give them.

:::

> Ta iccetassa sabbanāmassa ta kārassa na ttaṃ hoti vā sabbattha liṅgesu. Ne te, naṃ taṃ, ne te, nena tena, nehi nebhi tehi tebhi.

The `t` of the pronoun `ta` optionally becomes `n`, everywhere — in all genders: `ne`, `te`; `naṃ`, `taṃ`; `ne`, `te`; `nena`, `tena`; `nehi`, `nebhi`, `tehi`, `tebhi`.

|     | ta + yo (🚹①⨂)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (t→n) + a + yo        | §175 |
| →   | na + (yo→e)           | §164 |
| →   | n + (~~a~~ + e)       | §83  |
| →   | **ne**                |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨂(ta) | ne, te | they |
| 🚹②⨀(ta) | naṃ, taṃ | him |
| 🚹②⨂(ta) | ne, te | them |
| 🚹③⨀(ta) | nena, tena | by him |
| 🚹③⨂(ta) | nehi, nebhi, tehi, tebhi | by them |

:::

> ‘‘Sabbassa, tassa vā sabbatthā’’ti ca vattate.

And "`sabbassa`" [from [220 (§170): Sabbassimasse vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r220)] and "`tassa vā sabbattha`" continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r213"></a>

### 213 (§176): Sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ (`a` [for `ta`] before `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ`, `saṃ`, `sā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§176 (213): Sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m176" description="`a` [for `ta`] before `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ`, `saṃ`, `sā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's order is kept. `sabbasseva` — the whole `ta`, the `t` with its vowel — is the gloss's addition, as in Kaccāyana's vutti, drawn from the `sabbassa` just declared to continue; the `vā` continues from 212 and is again an open option: `assa` beside `nassa` and `tassa`. The plural `saṃ` of [203 (§168): Sabbato naṃ saṃsānaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r203) does not trigger the `a` — `nesaṃ`, `tesaṃ` — for the `saṃ` and `sā` of the sutta are the feminine singular replacements of 204.

:::

> Ta iccetassa sabbanāmassa sabbasseva a ttaṃ hoti vā sa smā smiṃ saṃ sā iccetesu vacanesu sabbattha liṅgesu. Assa nassa tassa, nesaṃ tesaṃ nesānaṃ tesānaṃ.

The whole of the pronoun `ta` optionally becomes `a` before the inflections `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ` and [the replacements] `saṃ`, `sā`, in all genders: `assa`, `nassa`, `tassa`; `nesaṃ`, `tesaṃ`, `nesānaṃ`, `tesānaṃ`.

|     | ta + sa (🚹④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ta→a) + sa           | §176 |
| →   | a + (s) + sa          | §61  |
| →   | **assa**              |      |

|     | ta + naṃ (🚹④⨂)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | t + (a→e) + naṃ       | §102 |
| →   | te + (naṃ→saṃ)        | §168 |
| →   | **tesaṃ**             |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(ta) | assa, nassa, tassa | to him |
| 🚹④⨂(ta) | nesaṃ, tesaṃ, nesānaṃ, tesānaṃ | to them |

:::

> ‘‘Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā’’ti ito ‘‘smāsmiṃnaṃ, mhāmhī’’ti ca vattate.

From [§99 (81): Smāhismiṃnaṃ mhābhimhi vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m99) "`smāsmiṃnaṃ`" and "`mhāmhi`" continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r214"></a>

### 214 (§100): Na timehi katākārehi (Not for `ta`, `ima` that have become `a`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§100 (214): Na timehi katākārehi" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m100" description="Not for `ta`, `ima` that have become `a`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §100 follows §99, the rule that gives `mhā`, `bhi`, `mhi`, as its exception and stands in the first section; Buddhappiya brings it to `ta`, straight after the rule that produces the `a`-forms, so that `asmā` and `asmiṃ` — and later the same forms of `ima` ([221 (§177): Imasaddassa ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r221)) — are stated without `amhā`, `amhi`. His gloss is Kaccāyana's without the examples; the rule is a prohibition and has no option marker. The long `vattate` that closes the sutta is a piece of grammar in its own right: the mentions of `etā` and `imā` in [217 (§63): Etimāsami](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r217) and of `tā` in [216 (§64): Tassā vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r216) continue as fifth-form endings — `tāto`, `etāto`, `imāto` — which is how Buddhappiya reads the `tato` of the next sutta.

:::

> Ta ima iccetehi katākārehi paresaṃ smāsmiṃ naṃ mhā mhi iccete ādesā na honti.

After `ta` and `ima` that have become `a`, the replacements `mhā` and `mhi` for `smā` and `smiṃ` do not occur.

|     | ta + smā (🚹⑤⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ta→a) + smā          | §176 |
| →   | a + smā (no `mhā`)    | §100 |
| →   | **asmā**              |      |

> Asmā nasmā tasmā namhā tamhā, nehi nebhi tehi tebhi, assa nassa tassa, nesaṃ tesaṃ nesānaṃ tesānaṃ, asmiṃ nasmiṃ tasmiṃ namhi tamhi, nesu tesu.

The inflection of 🚹(ta), with the forms given at 211–213; the pronoun has no vocative:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | so | ne, te |
| ② | naṃ, taṃ | ne, te |
| ③ | nena, tena | nehi, nebhi, tehi, tebhi |
| ④ | assa, nassa, tassa | nesaṃ, tesaṃ, nesānaṃ, tesānaṃ |
| ⑤ | asmā, nasmā, tasmā, namhā, tamhā | nehi, nebhi, tehi, tebhi |
| ⑥ | assa, nassa, tassa | nesaṃ, tesaṃ, nesānaṃ, tesānaṃ |
| ⑦ | asmiṃ, nasmiṃ, tasmiṃ, namhi, tamhi | nesu, tesu |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹⑤⨀(ta) | asmā | from that (no `amhā`) |
| 🚹⑤⨀(ta) | nasmā, tasmā, namhā, tamhā | from that |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ta) | asmiṃ | in that (no `amhi`) |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ta) | nasmiṃ, tasmiṃ, namhi, tamhi | in that |
| 🚹⑦⨂(ta) | nesu, tesu | in them |

:::

> Itthiyaṃ ‘‘tā si’’ itīdha sā desasi lopā. Sā kaññā, nattaṃ. Nā tā nāyo tāyo, naṃ taṃ, nā tā nāyo tāyo, nāya tāya, nāhi tāhi nābhi tābhi.

In the feminine, when we have `tā` + `si`, there is the replacement by `s` [211] and the elision of `si` [§220]: `sā kaññā` ("that girl"); the change to `n` [212]: `nā`, `tā`, `nāyo`, `tāyo`; `naṃ`, `taṃ`; `nā`, `tā`, `nāyo`, `tāyo`; `nāya`, `tāya`; `nāhi`, `tāhi`, `nābhi`, `tābhi`.

|     | tā + si (🚺①⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (t→s) + ā + si        | §174 |
| →   | sā + ~~si~~           | §220 |
| →   | **sā**                |      |

> ‘‘Etimāsamī’’ti ito etimā ggahaṇañca ‘‘tassā vā’’ti ito ta ggahaṇañca pañcamiyantavasena vattate ‘‘vā’’ti ca.

From [217 (§63): Etimāsami](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r217) the mention of `etā` and `imā`, and from [216 (§64): Tassā vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r216) the mention of `tā`, continue in the form of a fifth-form ending ["after `tā`, `etā`, `imā`"], and "`vā`" as well.

---

<a name="r215"></a>

### 215 (§65): Tato sassa ssāya (From those, of `sa` inflections there is `ssāya`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§65 (215): Tato sassa ssāya" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m65" description="From those, of `sa` inflections there is `ssāya`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §65 belongs with §62–§66 in his first section; Buddhappiya brings it to the feminine `tā` at ④⨀, reading its `tato` through the `anuvutti` he has just declared — "after `tā`, `etā`, `imā`" — so that the vutti's `tā etā imā to` names the three feminine stems and nothing else. The `vā` is an open option, `vikappa` (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.3): `tissāya` beside `tissā` and `tassā`, all three good Pāli, as Kaccāyana's `Vāti kimatthaṃ?` also shows. The `ssāya` replaces the whole `sa`; it is not the `sā` of [204 (§179): Ghapato smiṃsānaṃ saṃsā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r204) with something added, but at [217 (§63): Etimāsami](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r217) Buddhappiya will say that it "goes the way of `sā`", so that the `i` of 216–217 applies before it too (`tissāya`, `etissāya`, `imissāya`).

:::

> Tato tā etā imā to parassa sa ssa vibhattissa ssāyā deso hoti vā.

After those — `tā`, `etā`, `imā` — the inflection `sa` (④⨀, ⑥⨀) is optionally replaced by `ssāya`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺④⨀(ta) | tissāya | to her (derived at 216) |
| 🚺④⨀(eta) | etissāya | to this (woman) (derived at 217) |
| 🚺④⨀(ima) | imissāya | to this (woman) (derived at 221) |

:::

> ‘‘Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca, i’’iti ca vattate.

"`Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca`" ([206 (§62): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r206)) and "`i`" [from [217 (§63): Etimāsami](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r217)] continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r216"></a>

### 216 (§64): Tassā vā (Optionally, for `tā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§64 (216): Tassā vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m64" description="Optionally, for `tā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §64 stands next to §65 in the first section; Buddhappiya takes it after 215 and completes the feminine of `ta` with it, the `saṃsāsvekavacanesu vibhattādesesu` of the gloss coming by `anuvutti` from 206 and reaching `ssāya` too, since it "goes the way of `sā`". The `vā` is an open option (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.3): `tissā` beside `tassā`, `tissaṃ` beside `tassaṃ`; and with the `a` of [213 (§176): Sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r213) (`assā`, `nassā`, `assāya`, `nassāya`) and the `n` of [212 (§175): Tassa vā nattaṃ sabbattha](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r212) (`nāya`, `nāsaṃ`) the fourth form singular of the feminine `tā` has no fewer than ten shapes, all of them listed. The neuter takes a sentence — no `s` before `si`, since 211 is for the non-neuter — and `eta` is then run through as far as the forms that raise the next rule.

:::

> Tā iccetassa itthiyaṃ vattamānassa antassa i kāro hoti vā saṃ sā svekavacanesu vibhattādesesu. Tissāya tassāya assāya nassāya assā nassā tissā tassā nāya tāya, nāsaṃ tāsaṃ.

The final of `tā`, used in the feminine, optionally becomes `i` before the singular replacements of inflections `saṃ` and `sā`: `tissāya`, `tassāya`, `assāya`, `nassāya`, `assā`, `nassā`, `tissā`, `tassā`, `nāya`, `tāya`; `nāsaṃ`, `tāsaṃ`.

|     | tā + sa (🚺④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | t + (ā→i) + sa        | §64  |
| →   | ti + (sa→ssāya)       | §65  |
| →   | **tissāya**           |      |

|     | tā + sa (🚺④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tā + (sa→sā)          | §179 |
| →   | t + (ā→i) + sā        | §64  |
| →   | ti + (s) + sā         | §62  |
| →   | **tissā**             |      |

|     | tā + sa (🚺④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tā + (sa→sā)          | §179 |
| →   | t + (ā→a) + sā        | §66  |
| →   | ta + (s) + sā         | §62  |
| →   | **tassā**             |      |

|     | tā + sa (🚺④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tā + (sa→sā)          | §179 |
| →   | t + (ā→a) + sā        | §66  |
| →   | ta + (s) + sā         | §62  |
| →   | (ta→a) + ssā          | §176 |
| →   | **assā**              |      |

|     | tā + sa (🚺④⨀)        | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (t→n) + ā + sa        | §175 |
| →   | nā + (sa→āya)         | §111 |
| →   | n + (~~ā~~ + āya)     | §83  |
| →   | **nāya**              |      |

> Pañcamīchaṭṭhīsu tatiyācatutthīsamaṃ. Sattamiyaṃ assaṃ nassaṃ tissaṃ tassaṃ nāyaṃ tāyaṃ, nāsu tāsu.

In the fifth and sixth forms, the same as the third and fourth. In the seventh: `assaṃ`, `nassaṃ`, `tissaṃ`, `tassaṃ`, `nāyaṃ`, `tāyaṃ`; `nāsu`, `tāsu`.

|     | tā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)      | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | tā + (smiṃ→saṃ)       | §179 |
| →   | t + (ā→i) + saṃ       | §64  |
| →   | ti + (s) + saṃ        | §62  |
| →   | **tissaṃ**            |      |

The inflection of 🚺(tā), with the forms given at 214; there is no vocative:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | sā | nā, tā, nāyo, tāyo |
| ② | naṃ, taṃ | nā, tā, nāyo, tāyo |
| ③ | nāya, tāya | nāhi, tāhi, nābhi, tābhi |
| ④ | tissāya, tassāya, assāya, nassāya, assā, nassā, tissā, tassā, nāya, tāya | nāsaṃ, tāsaṃ |
| ⑤ | nāya, tāya | nāhi, tāhi, nābhi, tābhi |
| ⑥ | tissāya, tassāya, assāya, nassāya, assā, nassā, tissā, tassā, nāya, tāya | nāsaṃ, tāsaṃ |
| ⑦ | assaṃ, nassaṃ, tissaṃ, tassaṃ, nāyaṃ, tāyaṃ | nāsu, tāsu |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺④⨀(ta) | tissāya, tissā | to her (with `i`) |
| 🚺④⨀(ta) | tassāya, tassā | to her (with `a`) |
| 🚺④⨀(ta) | assāya, nassāya, assā, nassā | to her (by §176, §175) |
| 🚺④⨀(ta) | nāya, tāya | to her (by §111) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ta) | tissaṃ, tassaṃ, assaṃ, nassaṃ | in her |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ta) | nāyaṃ, tāyaṃ | in her (by §216) |

:::

> Napuṃsake si mhisā desābhāvā nattaṃ. Naṃ taṃ, nāni tāni, naṃ taṃ, nāni tāni, nena tena iccādi pulliṅgasamaṃ.

In the neuter, because there is no replacement by `s` before `si` [211 being for the non-neuter], the change to `n` [applies there too]: `naṃ`, `taṃ`; `nāni`, `tāni`; `naṃ`, `taṃ`; `nāni`, `tāni`; `nena`, `tena` and so on, the same as the masculine.

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | naṃ, taṃ | nāni, tāni |
| ② | naṃ, taṃ | nāni, tāni |

> Eta si, ‘‘etatesaṃ to’’ti sa kārādeso. Eso puriso, ete, etaṃ, ete iccādi sabba saddasamaṃ.

`eta` + `si`: by [211 (§174): Etatesaṃ to](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r211) the replacement by `s`: `eso puriso` ("this man"); `ete`; `etaṃ`; `ete`, and so on, the same as the word `sabba`.

> Itthiyaṃ etā si, sā deso. Esā kaññā, etā etāyo, etaṃ, etā etāyo, etāya, etāhi etābhi.

In the feminine, `etā` + `si`: the replacement by `s`: `esā kaññā` ("this girl"); `etā`, `etāyo`; `etaṃ`; `etā`, `etāyo`; `etāya`; `etāhi`, `etābhi`.

> Sa smiṃ su pana ‘‘saṃsāsvekavacanesu cā’’ti vattate.

But for `sa` and `smiṃ`, "`Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca`" ([206 (§62): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r206)) continues [into the next rule].[^68]

[^68]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) has "When, however, sa, smā, and smiṃ follow"; CST4 reads `Sasmiṃsu pana`, `sa` and `smiṃ` only — the feminine ⑤⨀ is `etāya` by [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m111) alone, as her own paradigm shows.

---

<a name="r217"></a>

### 217 (§63): Etimāsami (`etā`, `imā` endings become `i`) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§63 (217): Etimāsami" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m63" description="`etā`, `imā` endings become `i`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §63 stands with §62 and §64 in the first section; Buddhappiya takes it for `etā` at ④⨀. His gloss is fuller than Kaccāyana's on three points: the sixth form `etimāsaṃ` is said with reference to the final (`antāpekkhāyaṃ chaṭṭhī`), so that only the final vowel becomes `i`; the `ssāya` of [215 (§65): Tato sassa ssāya](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r215) "goes the way of `sā`" (`sādesagatika`), so the `i` appears before it too; and the `ca` of [206 (§62): Saṃsāsvekavacanesu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r206) is treated as an `adhikāra` to reach `aññissā`, `ekissā`, `itarissā` — forms no sutta gives. There is no option marker: before the singular `saṃ` and `sā` the `i` is mandatory (`etissā`, never `etassā`; the `etāya` of the paradigm is the `āya` of [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m111) for `sa`, not a `sā` form), whereas for `tā` 216 left it optional.

:::

> Antāpekkhāyaṃ chaṭṭhī, etā imā iccetesamanto saro i kāro hoti saṃsā svekavacanesu vibhattādesesu. Sā desagatikattā ssāyā desepi. Ca saddādhikārato aññekā saddādīnamantassa ca.

The sixth form [`etimāsaṃ`, "of `etā`, `imā`"] is said with reference to the final: the final vowel of `etā` and `imā` becomes `i` when the singular replacements of inflections `saṃ` and `sā` follow. Because the replacement `ssāya` [of 215] goes the way of `sā`, [the `i` appears] before the replacement `ssāya` too. By the governing word `ca` [of 206], also for the final of `aññā`, `ekā` and the like.

|     | etā + sa (🚺④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | et + (ā→i) + sa       | §63  |
| →   | eti + (sa→ssāya)      | §65  |
| →   | **etissāya**          |      |

|     | etā + sa (🚺④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | etā + (sa→sā)         | §179 |
| →   | et + (ā→i) + sā       | §63  |
| →   | eti + (s) + sā        | §62  |
| →   | **etissā**            |      |

|     | etā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | etā + (smiṃ→saṃ)      | §179 |
| →   | et + (ā→i) + saṃ      | §63  |
| →   | eti + (s) + saṃ       | §62  |
| →   | **etissaṃ**           |      |

> Etissāya etissā etāya, etāsaṃ etāsānaṃ, etāya, etāhi etābhi, etissāya etissā etāya, etāsaṃ etāsānaṃ, etissaṃ etāyaṃ, etāsu.

The inflection of 🚺(etā), with the forms given at 216; there is no vocative:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | esā | etā, etāyo |
| ② | etaṃ | etā, etāyo |
| ③ | etāya | etāhi, etābhi |
| ④ | etissāya, etissā, etāya | etāsaṃ, etāsānaṃ |
| ⑤ | etāya | etāhi, etābhi |
| ⑥ | etissāya, etissā, etāya | etāsaṃ, etāsānaṃ |
| ⑦ | etissaṃ, etāyaṃ[^69] | etāsu |

> Ca saddato aññissā aññāya, aññissaṃ aññāyaṃ. Ekissā ekāya, ekissaṃ ekāyaṃ. Itarissā itarāya, itarissaṃ itarāyaṃ iccādi.

By the word `ca`: `aññissā`, `aññāya`; `aññissaṃ`, `aññāyaṃ` ("another"). `ekissā`, `ekāya`; `ekissaṃ`, `ekāyaṃ` ("one"). `itarissā`, `itarāya`; `itarissaṃ`, `itarāyaṃ` ("the other"), and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺④⨀(eta) | etissāya, etissā | to this (woman) |
| 🚺④⨀(eta) | etāya | to this (woman), by §111 |
| 🚺⑦⨀(eta) | etissaṃ | in this (woman) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(eta) | etāyaṃ | in this (woman), by §216 |
| 🚺⑥⨀(añña) | aññissā, aññāya | of another (woman) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(añña) | aññissaṃ, aññāyaṃ | in another (woman) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(eka) | ekissā, ekāya | of one (woman) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(eka) | ekissaṃ, ekāyaṃ | in one (woman) |
| 🚺⑥⨀(itara) | itarissā, itarāya | of the other (woman) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(itara) | itarissaṃ, itarāyaṃ | in the other (woman) |

:::

> Napuṃsake etaṃ, etāni, etaṃ, etāni, sesaṃ ñeyyaṃ.

In the neuter: `etaṃ`, `etāni`; `etaṃ`, `etāni`; the rest is to be understood [as in the masculine].

> Ima saddassa bhedo. Ima si –

The word `ima` differs. `ima` + `si` —

> ‘‘Sabbassimassā’’ti vattate.

"`sabbassa imassa`" ("of the whole of `ima`") continues [from [220 (§170): Sabbassimasse vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r220)].

[^69]: The Thai edition read by [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) has two more forms at ⑦⨀, `etissaṃ etiyaṃ etāyaṃ etāya`: `etiyaṃ`, the `yaṃ` of [§216 (180): Ghapato smiṃ yaṃ vā](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m216) after the `i` of §63 without the `s` of §62, and `etāya`, a seventh of the kind CST4 itself admits as `imissā` at 221 and `amuyā` at 224. Neither is in the CST, whose paradigm is kept.

---

<a name="r218"></a>

### 218 (§172): Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhi (`ayaṃ` for the non-neuter [`ima`] before `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§172 (218): Anapuṃsakassā yaṃ simhī" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m172" description="`ayaṃ` for the non-neuter [`ima`] before `si`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §172 follows §170–§171; Buddhappiya puts ①⨀ first, as always — `ayaṃ` — then ③⨀ ([219 (§171): Animi nāmhi ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r219)), the plural before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi` ([220 (§170): Sabbassimasse vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r220)) and the `a`-forms ([221 (§177): Imasaddassa ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r221)), and has declared `sabbassa imassa` to continue from 220, Kaccāyana's §170. The `si` elided after the replacement goes by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m220). There is no option marker: `ayaṃ` is the only masculine and feminine ①⨀; the neuter has `idaṃ` by [222 (§129): Imassidamaṃsisu na puṃsake](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r222).

:::

> Ima saddassa sabbasseva anapuṃsakassa ayaṃ ādeso hoti si mhi vibhattimhi, si lopo. Ayaṃ puriso, ime, imaṃ, ime.

The whole of the word `ima`, when not neuter, is replaced by `ayaṃ` when the inflection `si` (①⨀) follows; the `si` is elided: `ayaṃ puriso` ("this man"); `ime`; `imaṃ`; `ime`.

|     | ima + si (🚹①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→ayaṃ) + si       | §172 |
| →   | ayaṃ + ~~si~~         | §220 |
| →   | **ayaṃ**              |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(ima) | ayaṃ puriso | this man |
| 🚹①⨂(ima) | ime | these |
| 🚹②⨀(ima) | imaṃ | this |
| 🚹②⨂(ima) | ime | these |

:::

---

<a name="r219"></a>

### 219 (§171): Animi nāmhi ca (`ana`, `imi` for `ima` before `nā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§171 (219): Animi nāmhi ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m171" description="`ana`, `imi` for `ima` before `nā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's order (§171 after §170) is not kept: Buddhappiya takes ③⨀ before the plural. His gloss adds `anitthiliṅgassevetaṃ gahaṇaṃ`, "this applies only to the non-feminine" — the feminine `imā` has `imāya` by [§111 (179): Ghato nādīnaṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m111) — a restriction Kaccāyana's vutti leaves to its examples and which the stanza at 220 will argue for the `e` as well. The `ca` of the sutta, which Kaccāyana's vutti uses to draw along `sabbassa`, Buddhappiya reflects in `sabbasseva`. There is no option marker: `anena`, with the `ena` of [§103 (79): Ato nena](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m103), and `iminā` are both always available.

:::

> Ima saddassa sabbasseva ana imi ādesā honti nā mhi vibhattimhi. Anitthiliṅgassevetaṃ gahaṇaṃ. Anena iminā.

The whole of the word `ima` is replaced by `ana` and `imi` when the inflection `nā` (③⨀) follows. This applies only to the non-feminine: `anena`, `iminā` ("by this").

|     | ima + nā (🚹③⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→ana) + nā        | §171 |
| →   | ana + (nā→ena)        | §103 |
| →   | an + (~~a~~ + ena)    | §83  |
| →   | **anena**             |      |

|     | ima + nā (🚹③⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→imi) + nā        | §171 |
| →   | **iminā**             |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨀(ima) | anena | by this |
| 🚹③⨀(ima) | iminā | by this |

:::

> ‘‘Sunaṃhisū’’ti vattate.

"`sunaṃhisu`" (before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`) continues [from [117 (§169): Rājassa rāju sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r117)].[^70]

[^70]: [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) refers the `sunaṃhisu` here to [87 (§89): Sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r87), the sutta that consists of the word; Kaccāyana's §170 inherits it from §169, and that is followed here. Both are defensible.

---

<a name="r220"></a>

### 220 (§170): Sabbassimasse vā (Optionally `e` for the whole of `ima` [before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§170 (220): Sabbassimasse vā" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m170" description="Optionally `e` for the whole of `ima` [before `su`, `naṃ`, `hi`]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §170 opens his `ima` rules; Buddhappiya reaches it at the plural before `su`, `naṃ` and `hi`. The `vā` is an open option, `vikappa` (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.3): `ehi` beside `imehi` ([§101 (80): Suhisvakāro e](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m101)), `esaṃ` beside `imesaṃ`, `esu` beside `imesu`. The stanza that fixes the rule's gender is his addition: the `sabbattha` it misses is that of [213 (§176): Sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r213), and the upshot is that `imāhi`, `imāsaṃ`, `imāsu` have no `ehi`, `esaṃ`, `esu` beside them.

:::

> Sabbassa ima saddassa e kāro hoti vā su naṃ hi iccetesu vacanesu.

The whole of the word `ima` optionally becomes `e` when the inflections `su`, `naṃ` and `hi` follow.

> Āppaccayantāniddesā, sabbatthāti avuttato; \
> Anitthiliṅgassevettha, gahaṇañhi imassiti.

Because no [stem] ending in the suffix `ā` is indicated, and because `sabbattha` is not said, \
here too the word `ima` is taken only in the non-feminine.

> Ehi ebhi imehi imebhi.

`ehi`, `ebhi`, `imehi`, `imebhi` ("by these").

|     | ima + hi (🚹③⨂)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→e) + hi          | §170 |
| →   | **ehi**               |      |

|     | ima + hi (🚹③⨂)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | im + (a→e) + hi       | §101 |
| →   | **imehi**             |      |

|     | ima + hi (🚹③⨂)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→e) + hi          | §170 |
| →   | e + (hi→bhi)          | §99  |
| →   | **ebhi**              |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | ehi, ebhi | by these |
| 🚹③⨂(ima) | imehi, imebhi | by these (by §101) |

:::

> ‘‘Sabbassa, vā, sabbattha, sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvatta’’nti ca vattate.

And "`sabbassa`", "`vā`", "`sabbattha`" [from 212] and "`sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ`" [from 213] continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r221"></a>

### 221 (§177): Imasaddassa ca (And for the word `ima`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§177 (221): Imasaddassa ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m177" description="And for the word `ima`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's order is kept (§177 after §176). The `ca` extends [213 (§176): Sasmāsmiṃsaṃsāsvattaṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r213) to `ima` with everything that rule carried — the whole word, `vā`, the five inflections, all genders — and the `vā` is an open option: `assa` beside `imassa`, `asmiṃ` beside `imasmiṃ`, `assā` beside `imissā`. The `imissā` he adds at ⑦⨀ is a form no sutta gives, secured from the catch-all rule of the Taddhita chapter. For the neuter he declares `savibhattissa` and `vā` to continue from [104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r104), Kaccāyana's §128, which his §129 follows. [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) refers `savibhattissa` instead to its source sutta, [§120 (243): Amhassa mamaṃ savibhattissa se](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m120) — [243 (§120)](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r243) in Buddhappiya's order, twenty-two rules ahead — here and again at 222 and 224; both are defensible.

:::

> Ima saddassa ca sabbasseva a ttaṃ hoti vā sa smā smiṃsaṃ sā iccetesu vacanesu sabbattha liṅgesu.

And the whole of the word `ima` optionally becomes `a` before the inflections `sa`, `smā`, `smiṃ` and [the replacements] `saṃ`, `sā`, in all genders.

|     | ima + sa (🚹④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→a) + sa          | §177 |
| →   | a + (s) + sa          | §61  |
| →   | **assa**              |      |

|     | ima + naṃ (🚹④⨂)      | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima→e) + naṃ         | §170 |
| →   | e + (naṃ→saṃ)         | §168 |
| →   | **esaṃ**              |      |

|     | ima + naṃ (🚹④⨂)      | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | im + (a→e) + naṃ      | §102 |
| →   | ime + (naṃ→saṃ)       | §168 |
| →   | **imesaṃ**            |      |

> Assa imassa, esaṃ esānaṃ imesaṃ imesānaṃ, asmā imasmā imamhā, ehi ebhi imehi imebhi, assa imassa, esaṃ esānaṃ imesaṃ imesānaṃ, asmiṃ imasmiṃ imamhi, esu imesu.

The inflection of 🚹(ima), with the forms given at 218–220; there is no vocative:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | ayaṃ | ime |
| ② | imaṃ | ime |
| ③ | anena, iminā | ehi, ebhi, imehi, imebhi |
| ④ | assa, imassa | esaṃ, esānaṃ, imesaṃ, imesānaṃ |
| ⑤ | asmā, imasmā, imamhā | ehi, ebhi, imehi, imebhi |
| ⑥ | assa, imassa | esaṃ, esānaṃ, imesaṃ, imesānaṃ |
| ⑦ | asmiṃ, imasmiṃ, imamhi | esu, imesu |

> Itthiyaṃ imā si, aya mādesasilopā.

In the feminine, `imā` + `si`: the replacement by `ayaṃ` [218] and the elision of `si`.

> Ayaṃ kaññā, imā imāyo, imaṃ, imā imāyo, imāya, imāhi imābhi. Catutthiyaṃ a ttaṃ, i kāra- ssāyā desā ca, assāya imissāya assā imissā imāya, imāsaṃ imāsānaṃ. Sattamiyaṃ assaṃ imissaṃ imissā vā, ‘‘tesu vuddhilopā’’dinā smiṃ vacanassa vā sā deso. Imāyaṃ, imāsu. Sesaṃ ñeyyaṃ.

`ayaṃ kaññā` ("this girl"); `imā`, `imāyo`; `imaṃ`; `imā`, `imāyo`; `imāya`; `imāhi`, `imābhi`. In the fourth form, the change to `a` [221], and the changes to `i` [217] and to `ssāya` [215]: `assāya`, `imissāya`, `assā`, `imissā`, `imāya`; `imāsaṃ`, `imāsānaṃ`.[^71] In the seventh form: `assaṃ`, `imissaṃ`, or `imissā` — by [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404) the inflection `smiṃ` is optionally replaced by `sā`; `imāyaṃ`; `imāsu`. The rest is to be understood.

The inflection of 🚺(imā); there is no vocative:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | ayaṃ | imā, imāyo |
| ② | imaṃ | imā, imāyo |
| ③ | imāya | imāhi, imābhi |
| ④ | assāya, imissāya, assā, imissā, imāya | imāsaṃ, imāsānaṃ |
| ⑤ | imāya | imāhi, imābhi |
| ⑥ | assāya, imissāya, assā, imissā, imāya | imāsaṃ, imāsānaṃ |
| ⑦ | assaṃ, imissaṃ, imissā, imāyaṃ | imāsu |

|     | imā + sa (🚺④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | im + (ā→i) + sa       | §63  |
| →   | imi + (sa→ssāya)      | §65  |
| →   | **imissāya**          |      |

|     | imā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | imā + (smiṃ→saṃ)      | §179 |
| →   | im + (ā→i) + saṃ      | §63  |
| →   | imi + (s) + saṃ       | §62  |
| →   | **imissaṃ**           |      |

|     | imā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | imā + (smiṃ→saṃ)      | §179 |
| →   | im + (ā→a) + saṃ      | §66  |
| →   | ima + (s) + saṃ       | §62  |
| →   | (ima→a) + ssaṃ        | §177 |
| →   | **assaṃ**             |      |

|     | imā + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | imā + (smiṃ→sā)       | §404 |
| →   | im + (ā→i) + sā       | §63  |
| →   | imi + (s) + sā        | §62  |
| →   | **imissā**            |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹④⨀(ima) | assa, imassa | to this |
| 🚹④⨂(ima) | esaṃ, esānaṃ, imesaṃ, imesānaṃ | to these |
| 🚹⑤⨀(ima) | asmā, imasmā, imamhā | from this (no `amhā`, by 214) |
| 🚹⑦⨀(ima) | asmiṃ, imasmiṃ, imamhi | in this (no `amhi`, by 214) |
| 🚺①⨀(ima) | ayaṃ kaññā | this girl |
| 🚺④⨀(ima) | assāya, imissāya, assā, imissā, imāya | to this (woman) |
| 🚺⑦⨀(ima) | assaṃ, imissaṃ, imissā, imāyaṃ | in this (woman) |

:::

> Napuṃsake ima si, ‘‘savibhattissa, vā’’ti ca vattate.

In the neuter, `ima` + `si`: and "`savibhattissa`" (together with its inflection) and "`vā`" continue [from [104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r104)].

[^71]: Rachiwong (1995) renders `attaṃ, ikāra-ssāyādesā ca` as "the 'a'-state as well as ssāya-substitute for 'i' are applicable"; the words are a `dvanda` — the change to `a` (221) and the replacements `i` (217) and `ssāya` (215) — as `assāya` beside `imissāya` shows. Her statement of the rule also drops `saṃ` from `sasmāsmiṃsaṃsā iccetesu`.

---

<a name="r222"></a>

### 222 (§129): Imassidamaṃsisu na puṃsake (`idaṃ` for neuter `ima` before `aṃ`, `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§129 (222): Imassidamaṃsisu napuṃsake" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m129" description="`idaṃ` for neuter `ima` before `aṃ`, `si`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §129 stands among the `ntu` rules of his third section (§124–§130), sharing their `savibhattissa`; Buddhappiya brings it to the neuter of `ima`, after the masculine and the feminine, and declares `savibhattissa` and `vā` to continue from [104 (§128): Naṃmhi taṃ vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r104). The `vā` is an open option (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.3): `idaṃ` beside `imaṃ`. The `anuvutti` declared for `amu` at the close — `vā` from [220 (§170): Sabbassimasse vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r220), `anapuṃsakassa` and `simhi` from [218 (§172): Anapuṃsakassāyaṃ simhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r218) — is the one Kaccāyana's §173 takes from §170–§172.

:::

> Napuṃsake vattamānassa sabbasseva ima saddassa savibhattissa idaṃ hoti vā aṃ si su paresu.

The whole of the word `ima` used in the neuter, together with its inflection, optionally becomes `idaṃ` when `aṃ` (②⨀) and `si` (①⨀) follow.

|     | ima + si (🚻①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (ima + si → idaṃ)     | §129 |
| →   | **idaṃ**              |      |

|     | ima + si (🚻①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ima + (si→aṃ)         | §219 |
| →   | im + (~~a~~ + aṃ)     | §83  |
| →   | **imaṃ**              |      |

> Idaṃ cittaṃ virocati, imaṃ, imāni, idaṃ pupphaṃ passasi, imaṃ, imāni, anena iminā, ehi ebhi imehi imebhi iccādi pulliṅge viya ñeyyaṃ.

`idaṃ cittaṃ virocati` ("this mind shines"), `imaṃ`; `imāni`; `idaṃ pupphaṃ passasi` ("you see this flower"), `imaṃ`; `imāni`; `anena`, `iminā`; `ehi`, `ebhi`, `imehi`, `imebhi`, and so on, to be understood as in the masculine.

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | idaṃ, imaṃ | imāni |
| ② | idaṃ, imaṃ | imāni |
| ③ | anena, iminā | ehi, ebhi, imehi, imebhi |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(ima) | idaṃ cittaṃ virocati | this mind shines |
| 🚻①⨀(ima) | imaṃ | this |
| 🚻②⨀(ima) | idaṃ pupphaṃ passasi | you see this flower |
| 🚻②⨀(ima) | imaṃ | this |
| 🚻①⨂(ima) | imāni | these |

:::

> Amu saddassa bhedo. Amu si –

The word `amu` differs. `amu` + `si` —

> ‘‘Vā, anapuṃsakassa, simhī’’ti ca vattate.

And "`vā`" [from 220], "`anapuṃsakassa`" and "`simhi`" [from 218] continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r223"></a>

### 223 (§173): Amussa mo saṃ (`s` for the `m` of `amu` [before `si`]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§173 (223): Amussa mo saṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m173" description="`s` for the `m` of `amu` [before `si`]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's order is kept (§173 after §172). The `vā` continues from [220 (§170): Sabbassimasse vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r220) and is an open option (*Boundaries and Domains* §2.3): `asu` beside `amu` — which Buddhappiya, like Kaccāyana, shows only with the augment `ka` of the next rule, `amuko`, `amukā`. The `saṃ` of the sutta is the letter `s`.

:::

> Anapuṃsakassa amu saddassa ma kāro sa kāramāpajjate vā si mhi pare. Asu rājā.

The `m` of the word `amu` ("that yonder"), when not neuter, optionally becomes `s` when `si` (①⨀) follows: `asu rājā` ("that king yonder").

|     | amu + si (🚹①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | a + (m→s) + u + si    | §173 |
| →   | asu + ~~si~~          | §220 |
| →   | **asu**               |      |

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(amu) | asu rājā | that king yonder |

:::

> ‘‘Sabbanāmato, vā’’ti ca vattate.

And "`sabbanāmato`" and "`vā`" continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r224"></a>

### 224 (§178): Sabbato ko (Optionally the augment `ka` after any pronoun) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§178 (224): Sabbato ko" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m178" description="Optionally the augment `ka` after any pronoun" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §178 precedes §179 and lends it `sabbato sabbanāmato`; Buddhappiya used §179 at [204 (§179): Ghapato smiṃsānaṃ saṃsā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r204) and brings §178 here, to `amu`, where the augment `ka` is the usual shape of the word (`asuko`, `amuko`), and he reads the rule without Kaccāyana's `simhi`. [Rachiwong (1995)](/rupasiddhi/rachiwong) calls the two applications `vinādhikārena` — `Amussa mo saṃ` for `asuko` here and `Amussāduṃ` for `adussa` below — a `yogavibhāga`, the reading of a sutta apart from its context, which is Buddhappiya's own name for the device at [124 (§117): Jhalato sassa no vā](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r124); his phrase here is `vinādhikārena yogena`. The `vā` that keeps `vo` out of the plural is the one carried into [§119 (155): Lato vokāro ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m119): at [155 (§119): Lato vokāro ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r155) Buddhappiya read it as a `vavatthitavibhāsā` and put `amu` into its `asanta` domain — "`na ca vo no ca amuppabhutito bhave`", the stanza Ruiz-Falqués quotes (*Boundaries and Domains* §3.5) — so `amū`, never `amuvo`. It is the one `vavatthitavibhāsā` invoked in the pronoun section, and it is invoked to block a form. The `vā` of this rule itself is an open option: `asuko`, `amuko`, `asu` and `amu` are all first forms. The `amusaṃ`, `amuhi` without lengthening rest on the `ca` of §89, which Buddhappiya read at [87 (§89): Sunaṃhisu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r87) as cancelling the lengthening `kvaci`.

:::

> Sabbato sabbanāmato paro ka iccayamāgamo hoti vā. Puna sabbato ggahaṇena hīnā ditopi ko. ‘‘Amussa mo sa’’nti vinādhikārena yogena ka kārepi sā deso.

After every pronoun the augment `ka` is optionally [inserted]. By the repeated mention of `sabbato` ("after all"), `ka` also comes after `hīna` ("low") and the like [`hīnako`]. By applying "`Amussa mo saṃ`" [223] without its governing word [`simhi`], the replacement by `s` occurs before the `k` too.

> Asuko, asukā, asukaṃ. Sā desābhāve amuko, amukā, amukaṃ iccādi.

`asuko`, `asukā`, `asukaṃ` ("that one yonder" — masculine, feminine, neuter). Without the replacement by `s`: `amuko`, `amukā`, `amukaṃ`, and so on.

|     | amu + si (🚹①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (ka) + si       | §178 |
| →   | a + (m→s) + uka + si  | §173 |
| →   | asuka + (si→o)        | §104 |
| →   | asuk + (~~a~~ + o)    | §83  |
| →   | **asuko**             |      |

|     | amu + si (🚹①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (ka) + si       | §178 |
| →   | amuka + (si→o)        | §104 |
| →   | amuk + (~~a~~ + o)    | §83  |
| →   | **amuko**             |      |

> Bahuvacane ‘‘lato vokāro cā’’ti sutte anuvattamānavā ggahaṇena vo kāro na hoti, niccaṃ yo lopo, dīgho ca.

In the plural, by the `vā` that continues into the sutta [§119 (155): Lato vokāro ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m119), the change [of `yo`] to `vo` does not take place; the `yo` is always elided [by §118] and [the vowel] lengthened [by §88].

|     | amu + yo (🚹①⨂)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + ~~yo~~          | §118 |
| →   | am + (u→ū)            | §88  |
| →   | **amū**               |      |

> Amū purisā, amuṃ, amū, amunā, amūhi amūbhi amuhi amubhi, amussa. ‘‘Amussādu’’nti vinādhikārena yogena aduṃ ādeso, adussa, amūsaṃ amūsānaṃ amusaṃ amusānaṃ, amusmā amumhā, amūhi amūbhi amuhi amubhi, amussa adussa, amūsaṃ amūsānaṃ amusaṃ amusānaṃ, amusmiṃ amumhi, amūsu amusu.

The inflection of 🚹(amu); there is no vocative:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | asu, asuko, amuko | amū purisā |
| ② | amuṃ | amū |
| ③ | amunā | amūhi, amūbhi, amuhi, amubhi |
| ④ | amussa, adussa | amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ, amusaṃ, amusānaṃ |
| ⑤ | amusmā, amumhā | amūhi, amūbhi, amuhi, amubhi |
| ⑥ | amussa, adussa | amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ, amusaṃ, amusānaṃ |
| ⑦ | amusmiṃ, amumhi | amūsu, amusu |

`adussa`: by applying "`Amussāduṃ`" ([225 (§130): Amussāduṃ](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r225)) without its governing words [`aṃsisu napuṃsake`], the replacement `adu` [for `amu`]. `amusaṃ`, `amuhi`: without the lengthening, by the `ca` of [§89 (87): Sunaṃhisu ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m89).

|     | amu + sa (🚹④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (amu→adu) + sa        | §130 |
| →   | adu + (s) + sa        | §61  |
| →   | **adussa**            |      |

> Itthiyaṃ si mhi sā desādi.

In the feminine, before `si` the replacement by `s` and the rest.

> Asu kaññā asukā amukā vā, amū amuyo, amuṃ, amū amuyo, amuyā, amūhi amūbhi, amussā amuyā, amūsaṃ amūsānaṃ, amuyā, amūhi amūbhi, amussā amuyā, amūsaṃ amūsānaṃ, amussaṃ amuyaṃ amuyā, amūsu.

The inflection of 🚺(amu); there is no vocative:

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | asu kaññā, asukā, amukā | amū, amuyo |
| ② | amuṃ | amū, amuyo |
| ③ | amuyā | amūhi, amūbhi |
| ④ | amussā, amuyā[^72] | amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ |
| ⑤ | amuyā | amūhi, amūbhi |
| ⑥ | amussā, amuyā | amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ |
| ⑦ | amussaṃ, amuyaṃ, amuyā | amūsu |

|     | amu + sa (🚺④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (sa→sā)         | §179 |
| →   | amu + (s) + sā        | §62  |
| →   | **amussā**            |      |

|     | amu + smiṃ (🚺⑦⨀)     | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (smiṃ→saṃ)      | §179 |
| →   | amu + (s) + saṃ       | §62  |
| →   | **amussaṃ**           |      |

|     | amu + sa (🚺④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | amu + (sa→yā)         | §112 |
| →   | **amuyā**             |      |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(amu) | asuko, amuko | that one yonder |
| 🚺①⨀(amu) | asukā, amukā | that (woman) yonder |
| 🚻①⨀(amu) | asukaṃ, amukaṃ | that (thing) yonder |
| 🚹①⨀(hīna) | hīnako | a low one (by the repeated `sabbato`) |
| 🚹①⨂(amu) | amū purisā | those men yonder |
| 🚹④⨀(amu) | amussa, adussa | to that one yonder |
| 🚹④⨂(amu) | amūsaṃ, amūsānaṃ, amusaṃ, amusānaṃ | to those yonder |
| 🚺④⨀(amu) | amussā, amuyā | to that (woman) yonder |
| 🚺⑦⨀(amu) | amussaṃ, amuyaṃ, amuyā | in that (woman) yonder |

:::

> Napuṃsake amusi. ‘‘Savibhattissa’’, imassida miccādito ‘‘aṃsisu napuṃsake’’ti ca vattate.

In the neuter, `amu` + `si`: "`savibhattissa`" and, from [222 (§129): Imassidamaṃsisu na puṃsake](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r222), "`aṃsisu napuṃsake`" (before `aṃ` and `si`, in the neuter) continue [into the next rule].

[^72]: The Thai edition read by Rachiwong (1995) gives the feminine paradigm otherwise: `amussā amussāya` at ④⨀ and ⑥⨀ — a `ssāya` form that [§65 (215): Tato sassa ssāya](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m65), read of `tā`, `etā`, `imā`, does not obviously license for a stem in `u` — no `amuyā` at ⑦⨀, and a bare `amu` among the first forms beside `asukā`, `amukā`. CST4's `amussā amuyā`, `amussaṃ amuyaṃ amuyā` and `asu kaññā asukā amukā vā` are kept.

---

<a name="r225"></a>

### 225 (§130): Amussāduṃ (`aduṃ` for neuter `amu` before `aṃ`, `si`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§130 (225): Amussāduṃ" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m130" description="`aduṃ` for neuter `amu` before `aṃ`, `si`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §130 follows §129 and closes the run of `savibhattissa` rules in his third section; Buddhappiya takes it for the neuter of `amu` and, as the Kaccāyana page observes, drops the `vā`: his gloss says `hoti`, not `hoti vā`, and the `vā` that he carried into 223–224 is not declared for this rule. `aduṃ` is therefore the only neuter form before `aṃ` and `si` — the option that [222 (§129): Imassidamaṃsisu na puṃsake](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r222) left open between `idaṃ` and `imaṃ` has no counterpart here — and the rule is `nicca`. The `kissa` and `ka` declared for `kiṃ` at the close come from [270 (§227): Kissa ka ve ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r270), Kaccāyana's §227, two rules before §229 in his order and forty-four suttas after it in Buddhappiya's — the longest `anuvutti` leap in the section.

:::

> Napuṃsake vattamānassa sabbasseva amu saddassa savibhattissa aduṃ hoti aṃsi su paresu. Aduṃ pupphaṃ, amū amūni, aduṃ, amū amūni, amunā iccādi pulliṅgasamaṃ.

The whole of the word `amu` used in the neuter, together with its inflection, becomes `aduṃ` when `aṃ` (②⨀) and `si` (①⨀) follow: `aduṃ pupphaṃ` ("that flower yonder"); `amū`, `amūni`; `aduṃ`; `amū`, `amūni`; `amunā`, and so on, the same as the masculine.

|     | amu + si (🚻①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (amu + si → aduṃ)     | §130 |
| →   | **aduṃ**              |      |

| | ⨀ | ⨂ |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ① | aduṃ | amū, amūni |
| ② | aduṃ | amū, amūni |
| ③ | amunā | amūhi, amūbhi, amuhi, amubhi |

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚻①⨀(amu) | aduṃ pupphaṃ | that flower yonder |
| 🚻①⨂(amu) | amū, amūni | those (things) yonder |
| 🚻②⨀(amu) | aduṃ | that (thing) yonder |

:::

> Kiṃ saddassa bhedo. ‘‘Kiṃ si’’ itīdha –

The word `kiṃ` ("what?", "who?") differs. When we have `kiṃ` + `si` —

> ‘‘Kissa ka ve cā’’ti ito ‘‘kissa, ka’’iti ca vattate.

From [§227 (270): Kissa ka ve ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m227) ([270 (§227): Kissa ka ve ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r270)) "`kissa`" (of `kiṃ`) and "`ka`" continue [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r226"></a>

### 226 (§229): Sesesu ca (and `ka` before the remaining [inflections]) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§229 (226): Sesesu ca" href="/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m229" description="and `ka` before the remaining [inflections]" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana's §229 stands in his fourth section after §227 `Kissa ka ve ca` and §228 `Ku hiṃhaṃsu ca`, among the rules for the suffixes `to`, `tra`, `dā` and the like; Buddhappiya brings it forward to the pronoun `kiṃ` and leaves §227–§228 for the section on those suffixes ([270 (§227): Kissa ka ve ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r270), [272 (§228): Ku hiṃhaṃsu ca](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r272)). He confines `sesesu` to the inflections, where Kaccāyana's vutti has "inflections and suffixes": the `thamādi` already covered by the `ca` of §227 is `thaṃ` ([§399 (422): Kimimehi thaṃ](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m399), whose `kathaṃ` is the example Kaccāyana's vutti to §227 gives for its `ca`) and the rest, `kadā`, `kattha`. The `ca` of this sutta, `katthaci nivattanatthaṃ`, is the reading Ruiz-Falqués records for Rūp 226 (*Boundaries and Domains* §3.5): a `katthaci` domain — `kissa`, `kismiṃ`, `kimhi` beside `kassa`, `kasmiṃ` — inside an otherwise general replacement. The neuter's `kiṃ etaṃ` is put down to the `paribhāsā` [§56 (64): Tadanuparodhena](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m56) because `kaṃ` is not what the Buddha's usage has for the neuter. The `tu` of [§205 (160): Tato yonamo tu](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m205) that gives `ubho` `kvaci` at the close, and the `o` for `e` of `ubhohi` by [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404), are the `tu` read as `kvaci` that Ruiz-Falqués notes for Rūp 226 (§2.2).

:::

> Ki miccetassa ka saddo ādeso hoti va ppaccayato sesesu vibhattibhedesu. Ettha ca ‘‘kissa ka ve cā’’ti sutte ca saddena va ppaccayāvasiṭṭha tha mādippaccayānaṃ gahitattā sesa ggahaṇena vibhattiyova gayhante. Ca ggahaṇaṃ katthaci nivattanatthaṃ, tena ‘‘kissa, kismi’’ntiādi ca sijjhati. ‘‘So’’ti si ssa o, saralopādi.

The word `ka` is the replacement of `kiṃ` in the remaining kinds of inflection, other than the suffix `va`.[^73] And here, since by the word `ca` in the sutta [§227 (270): Kissa ka ve ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m227) the suffixes other than `va` — `thaṃ` and the rest [`kathaṃ`, `kadā`, `kattha`] — are [already] taken, by the mention of `sesa` ("the remaining") only the inflections are taken. The mention of `ca` is for cancelling in some places; hence `kissa`, `kismiṃ` and the like are also established. By [66 (§104): So](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r66) the `si` becomes `o`; elision of the vowel and the rest.

|     | kiṃ + si (🚹①⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (kiṃ→ka) + si         | §229 |
| →   | ka + (si→o)           | §104 |
| →   | k + (~~a~~ + o)       | §83  |
| →   | **ko**                |      |

|     | kiṃ + sa (🚹④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | (kiṃ→ka) + sa         | §229 |
| →   | ka + (s) + sa         | §61  |
| →   | **kassa**             |      |

|     | kiṃ + sa (🚹④⨀)       | rule |
| --- | --------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kiṃ + sa (no `ka`)    | §229 (`ca`) |
| →   | ki + ~~ṃ~~ + sa       | §38  |
| →   | ki + (s) + sa         | §61  |
| →   | **kissa**             |      |
